   **And one host-side interaction that would otherwise undo it.** Orca re-grants ACEs across `userData` at startup: `ensureWindowsUserDataAclGrant` (`startup/windows-user-data-acl.ts:125-163`) runs `icacls <userData>\* /grant:r <identity>:(OI)(CI)(F) /C` over the immediate children and then over the root (`runIcaclsGrant` `:84-100`, targets at `:147-148`), marker-gated to run at most once per identity (`:136-140`), fired from `index.ts:1316-1326` because "Chromium's BrowserWindow ctor resets userData to a Protected DACL" **[code]**; and `writeFileAtomically`'s EPERM path calls `grantDirAcl(dirname(targetPath))` — `icacls <dir> /grant:r <identity>:(OI)(CI)(F)` (`win32-utils.ts:184-202`, args at `:189`) — on every failed write (`codex-accounts/fs-utils.ts:24-38`, the call at `:26`) **[code]**. `<userData>\claude-lanes` is a direct child of `userData`, so the startup grant **does** touch the lane root, and both calls add an **inheritable** ACE. **[inferred, and graded as such] — and rev 12's version of this inference was wrong in its mechanism while right in its conclusion, so the mechanism is restated rather than the conclusion softened.** The two calls do not have the same target. `ensureWindowsUserDataAclGrant` grants over `<userData>\*` and then `<userData>` (`runIcaclsGrant` targets at `:147-148`) **[code]** — the wildcard names the lane **root**, `<userData>\claude-lanes`, and a lane directory one level below it is defended by protection exactly as rev 12 said: `/grant:r` writes an *inheritable* ACE on the root, and a protected child does not inherit it. But `writeFileAtomically`'s EPERM path calls `grantDirAcl(dirname(targetPath))` (`codex-accounts/fs-utils.ts:26` → `win32-utils.ts:184-202`, args at `:189`) **[code]**, whose target for a lane credential write **is the lane directory itself** — and `/grant:r` on a directory writes an **explicit** ACE, which DACL protection does not block at all; protection only stops *inherited* ACEs. So protection is not what defends that second call. What defends it is that the granted identity is `resolveCurrentIdentity()` (`win32-utils.ts:185`) — the same current-user principal the hardening script already puts in its allowed set (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:71`, granted at `:85-95`, verified at `:102-106`) **[code]** — so the ACE it adds is one the read-back verification already accepts. The read-back still passes; the reason it passes is this one, not the other. That inference is exactly the kind this design refuses to rest on silently: **the control is a test, not the reasoning** — after a simulated `ensureWindowsUserDataAclGrant` and a simulated `grantDirAcl` on the lane root, re-read the lane directory's DACL and assert it is still protected and still names only the three allowed SIDs, with a mutation proof (creating the lane unprotected turns it red). Windows-only test, §5.

**(2) Atomic writes while the CLI holds `.credentials.json` open — Orca already has the retry, and it names this exact case.** On Windows a rename over a file another process holds open fails `EPERM`/`EACCES`/`EBUSY`. `writeFileAtomically` does not call `renameSync` directly: it calls `renameFileWithWindowsRetry` (`codex-accounts/fs-utils.ts:15` → `:200-202` → `runFileOperationWithWindowsRetry` `:208-223`), which retries **6 times on `win32`** (`:209`) on exactly those three codes (`:216`), parking the thread with `Atomics.wait` on a 50 ms-per-attempt ramp (`:217`, `:227-230`) — ~750 ms total — and the comment names the offenders outright: *"antivirus, Claude CLI, Codex CLI"* (`:195-199`) **[code]**. So rev 10's §2c decision to build the lane writer on `writeFileAtomically` is **validated on Windows for the rename**, which was not obvious and is now checked.
   **But neither existing primitive is sufficient alone, and that is a new rev-11 decision.** `writeFileAtomically` has the Windows rename retry and **no ACL work**. `writeSecureFile` has the ACL work — it restricts the *temp* file synchronously **before** the rename so the credential is never briefly published under inherited ACEs (`secure-file.ts:120-122`), then restricts the published path and caches only on confirmed success (`:123-126`) — but it publishes with a bare `renameSync` (`:122`) **[code]**, i.e. **no retry**, so a live `claude` holding the lane's `.credentials.json` makes a push throw where `writeFileAtomically` would have succeeded. **Decision: the lane writer combines them** — ACL-restrict the temp file synchronously, publish through `renameFileWithWindowsRetry`, re-assert and verify the published path's DACL, and treat a failed verification as a failed write (`accounts.lane.switch_write_failed` after rev 32, `accounts.lane.push_write_failed` before it) rather than a silent success. **And the retry can be exhausted, which rev 12 defined no refusal for.** `runFileOperationWithWindowsRetry` **rethrows** the last error once the six attempts are spent (`codex-accounts/fs-utils.ts:220`, inside `:208-223`) **[code]** — the window is ~750 ms and a `claude` that holds `.credentials.json` open past it is entirely possible on a loaded box. **Decision: the lane writer catches that rethrow and refuses by name — `accounts.lane.switch_write_locked` after rev 32 (§3), `accounts.lane.push_write_locked` before it)**, with a complete human sentence naming the cause and the remedy ("a Claude session on the host is holding this lane's credential file; retry in a moment"), because an untyped throw is the one class §3's Rule-3 row says every new refusal must not be — the client has no string for the code and nothing at all for a raw `EBUSY`. The lane is unchanged when it fires: the rename never published, so the old credential is still in place — and after rev 32 there is no watermark left to move, which makes the unchanged-lane guarantee simpler rather than weaker (§2c). Extracted once, in `claude-accounts/lane-credential-writer.ts` (§6, S9b), not by widening either existing helper. **Rev 32 changes only what this writer is writing *for*, and nothing about how.** Its callers were the push handler and the sync driver; they are now the **in-lane switch** (§2c(i)) and the **login-session capture** (§2b), both of which rewrite the same two files under the same per-lane gate. The ~750 ms retry window, the ACL-restricted temp, the published-path DACL verification and the two temp-name shapes the sweep must match are all unchanged, and the offender the retry's own comment names — *"antivirus, Claude CLI, Codex CLI"* — is if anything more likely now, because the lane's own `claude` is what holds that file open.
   **Two consequences for §2f's wipe sweep.** First, the two primitives use **different temp-name shapes** — `${target}.${pid}.${randomUUID()}.tmp` (`fs-utils.ts:12`) and `${target}.${pid}.${Date.now()}.${8 hex}.tmp` (`secure-file.ts:111`) **[code]** — so the sweep must match the pattern, not either literal, and §5's planted-tmp test plants **both**. Second, `writeFileAtomically`'s EPERM retry writes a **second** temp file (`fs-utils.ts:27-33`) and removes it only in its own catch (`:33`) **[code]**: on Windows a crash mid-retry can leave *two* full-credential blobs at rest. The sweep already covers this by matching the pattern; §5 asserts it.

**(3) Where the Windows CLI keeps the credential — graded honestly, and not assumed.** Orca's own model is unambiguous and is **[code]**: `writeRuntimeCredentials(credentialsJson)` writes `<configDir>/.credentials.json` on **every** platform (`runtime-auth-service.ts:477-478`, path from `runtime-paths.ts:21`), and only `darwin` *additionally* writes the Keychain (`:479-489`, the guard at `:479` and its comment at `:480`) **[code]**; there is no Windows branch, and `grep -rni "dpapi\|wincred\|CredRead" src` returns nothing Claude-related — its only hits are Git Credential Manager suppression (`hooks.ts:775`, `ipc/repos.ts:2434`) **[code]**. Orca also drives the Windows CLI through `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` today: `runClaudeCommand`'s `win32` arm sets `env: { ...process.env, CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR: configDir.windowsPath }` (`claude-accounts/service.ts:1078-1085`) **[code]**. **What that establishes:** Orca *treats* the plain file under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` as the whole credential store on Windows, and the variable is honoured. **What it does not establish** is that the Windows CLI keeps **no** additional store — a DPAPI blob or a Credential Manager entry outside the config dir. Nothing in this tree observes that either way, and the [doc] statement "`.credentials.json` is a plain file at `0600` on Linux" says nothing about Windows. **Grade: [inferred], unverified.** It is load-bearing — if the Windows CLI keeps any credential state outside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, lanes on Windows collapse the way macOS collapses under a pre-2.1 CLI, silently. **Decision, mirroring §2i's macOS rule exactly: Windows lane provisioning is gated on a live probe** (§5, Windows-only) that provisions two lanes, logs in to a different account in each, and asserts each lane's `claude auth status --json` reports its own account with the other lane's credential file deleted. Until that probe passes on the pinned build, Windows lane provisioning stays **off** — a hard gate in §6, not a note.

**(4) Path canonicalization — the containment checks are not Windows-correct today.** Every containment check in this design canonicalizes the way `managed-auth-path.ts` does: refuse a symlink (`:23-25`), `realpathSync` both sides (`:26-27`), then `canonicalCandidate === canonicalRoot || !canonicalCandidate.startsWith(canonicalRoot + sep)` (`:28-33`), then an exact relative-segment match (`:37-47`) **[code]**. On Windows that is four separate problems, and Orca already knows the fix for three of them elsewhere in the tree.
   - **8.3 short names and case.** `realpathSync` is Node's own resolver; `realpathSync.native` calls the OS and returns the true long name and true case. Orca uses `.native` precisely where canonical identity matters — `codex/config-toml-trust.ts:167`, `:209`, `:812`, `codex-trust-config-rollback.ts:27`, `agent-trust-presets.ts:163`, `ssh/ssh-config-include-expander.ts:290`, `git/repo.ts:238` **[code]** — and `managed-auth-path.ts` is one of the places that does **not**. So `--config-dir C:\Users\OWNER~1\AppData\...\claude-lanes\<id>` (an 8.3 component) or a drive-letter case flip can defeat a `startsWith` on a non-native realpath. **Decision:** every lane containment check uses `realpathSync.native` on both sides, and compares **case-insensitively on `win32`** — the precedent for the comparison is already here: `linkTargetsMatch` branches on platform and folds through `normalizeWindowsLinkTarget`, `linkTarget.replace(/^\\\\\?\\/, '').toLowerCase()` (`codex/codex-home-paths.ts:249-258`) **[code]**, which also strips the extended-length prefix.
   - **Junctions.** A directory junction needs **no privilege** on Windows — Orca's own worktree code prefers it for exactly that reason (`ipc/worktree-symlinks.ts:40-54`, the function at `:47-55`) **[code]** — so it is the cheap escape a `--config-dir` capture would use. Node reports a junction as **both** a symlink and a directory, and Orca has already been bitten: *"check `isSymbolicLink` before `isDirectory` — a Windows junction reports both, and the symlink branch must win"* (`opencode/hook-service.ts:1100`) **[code]**. The lane check's existing `lstatSync(...).isSymbolicLink()` refusal (`managed-auth-path.ts:30-33`) therefore does catch a junction — **provided it stays ordered first**, which §5 asserts with a Windows-only planted-junction test rather than leaving it to the reading.
   - **Extended-length and UNC forms.** `\\?\C:\…` and `\\server\share\…` compare unequal to their ordinary forms. Precedents for normalizing both are in-tree: `codex/codex-session-resume-home.ts:33-36` strips `\\?\` from a drive path, and `window/clipboard-windows-image-file.ts:39-42` matches both the `\\?\X:\` and `\\?\UNC\` shapes **[code]**. The lane check normalizes the prefix before comparing. **A *native* lane path is additionally required to be a local drive path** — never a remote UNC share — because such a root's ACLs are set by a machine this design has said nothing about; it is refused at provisioning. **The rule is scoped to native lanes, and rev 12's unscoped version could not ship beside §2n: S9e writes *every* lane through `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\home\<user>\.local\share\orca\claude-lanes\<principalId>\`, which is the exact shape the unscoped rule refused.** **Decision: the refusal carves out the WSL redirector forms and nothing else.** A UNC candidate is accepted only when `parseWslUncPath` matches it — `^//(wsl\.localhost|wsl\$)/([^/]+)(/.*)?$`, case-insensitive, both the modern and legacy prefixes (`src/shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`, regex at `:8`) **[code]** — and it is then handed to §2n's **in-distro** canonicalizer rather than to a Windows-side `startsWith`. That is the in-tree precedent doing exactly this, not an exception invented for S9e: `assertManagedAuthPath` **parses** the WSL UNC path (`claude-accounts/service.ts:969-970`) and on `win32` re-canonicalizes *inside* the distro — `readlink -f` on candidate and root, the marker file read and compared, and a `case "$candidate_real" in "$managed_root_real"/*/auth)` match with exit 35 otherwise (`:978-1014`) **[code]**. Every other UNC root is refused as before. **Two consequences stated rather than left to the reader.** (a) For an S9e lane the containment *proof* lives inside the distro, not in `shared/lane-path-containment.ts`, so its mutation proof lives in §2n's in-distro tests; the Windows-side module's job for that shape is to recognise it and refuse everything else. (b) **The redirector's own reachability is the permission story §2n(i) does not cover.** `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>` is not a remote share: it is served by the WSL 9p redirector for *this* Windows user on *this* machine, so no foreign ACL authority is introduced — but the file's permissions on the other side are **Linux** modes, not a DACL, which is why §2n(i) mandates `chmod 0700/0600` in-distro and why §2m(1)'s DACL step is **not run** there at all — rev 13 wrote "explicitly a no-op", and a `restrictWindowsPathSync` that cannot set a DACL does not no-op, it returns `false` (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]**, which under rev 13's unconditional fail-closed rule refused every S9e lane. §2m(1) now carries the same `parseWslUncPath` carve-out this bullet does. What that buys is the §2n residual, unchanged: on the one-account shared host both developers are the same Linux uid inside the distro, so those modes bound nothing between them either.
   - **Where this applies, so the list is not decorative:** the lane root containment in `principal-credential-lane.ts`; §2d's `addClaudeFromConfigDir`/`addCodexFromHome` capture refusal (which already had to be realpath-based, and on Windows must additionally be `.native` + case-folded); §2a's resume-path and `ompResumeFilePath` containment; guard 3(a)'s "no value in the merged env canonicalizes under `<userData>\claude-lanes\`"; **and — decided rather than left named, which is what rev 13 did — `resolveOwnedClaudeManagedAuthPath` itself (`managed-auth-path.ts:36-42`: plain `realpathSync` on both sides at `:26-27`, case-sensitive `startsWith(canonicalRoot + sep)` at `:37-41`) **[code]**.** §2a states that the pre-lane injection target "that exists right now is `<userData>/claude-accounts/<id>/auth`, a real managed-account credential store", so the one containment check guarding *that* store is Windows-incorrect on the pivot platform while this design calls it the live target. Guard 3(b) and §2d close the two S9 surfaces, so the residual was narrow — but "narrow" is not a disposition. **Decision: it adopts `shared/lane-path-containment.ts` too**, in S9a, which is a two-line import swap at an existing call site rather than new machinery, and it gets the same Windows-only mutation proof as the rest of the list. Each gets its own Windows-only mutation proof: reverting any one of them to `realpathSync` + case-sensitive compare must turn a named test red.

**(5) Environment: case-insensitivity, ConPTY, and the WSLENV export.**
   - **The env-key guards are case-sensitive and Windows environment variables are not. This is a genuine hole, found by re-opening §2a's guards against `win32`.** `CLAUDE_AUTH_ENV_VARS` is a literal tuple (`claude-accounts/environment.ts:9-14`); `applyClaudeEnvPatch` deletes by exact key, `delete baseEnv[key]` (`:23-25`), deletes `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` by exact key too (`:22-23`), and writes `baseEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`:44-46`); `hasClaudeAuthEnvConflict` tests `CLAUDE_AUTH_ENV_VARS.some((key) => Boolean(env[key]))` and `env.ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` (`:66-70`) **[code]**. Every one is exact-case — `environment.ts:9-14`, `:19-23`, `:66-70` re-opened **[code]**, and *that* is the load-bearing support for what follows. **Rev 13 supported it instead with a grep claim that does not reproduce, so the claim is corrected rather than restated:** `grep -rn "case-insensitive\|caseInsensitive" src/main/providers src/main/daemon src/main/pty --include="*.ts" | grep -v "\.test\."` returns **four** hits, not nothing — `pty/legacy-terminal-shim-dir.ts:258`, `:261`, `:264` (an explicitly case-insensitive PATH-entry matcher, used by a strip that already folds Windows keys at `:204-206`, `:216`, `:222`) and `pty/windows-path-segment-merge.ts:18` **[code]**. The spawn env is an ordinary `Record<string, string>` built by spreads (`local-pty-provider.ts:675-684`) **[code]**, so `env.claude_config_dir` and `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` are two distinct keys to Orca — and one variable to Windows. **Consequence on the shared host:** a client-supplied `env: { claude_config_dir: 'C:\\…' }`, or `envToDelete: ['Claude_Config_Dir']`, passes §2a's guard 1 and guard 3 untouched and reaches the child, where Windows resolves it as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` — the *exact* injection §2a exists to close, wearing different capitalization. **Decision: on `win32`, guard 1, guard 3, `hasClaudeAuthEnvConflict` *and `applyClaudeEnvPatch`'s own strip loop* compare env keys case-insensitively**, and the lane keys are **collapsed** to one canonical casing — every case-variant deleted before the canonical one is written. **Two things rev 13 got wrong about that collapse, and the second of them is round 3's third blocker.** *First, the justification.* Rev 13 said "a record carrying two casings of one Windows variable has undefined precedence". The precedence is **defined**, and this tree states it: "Why: Win32 uses the first case-insensitive key, and object order preserves block order" (`pty/windows-path-segment-merge.ts:18`), tested at `pty/windows-environment-path.test.ts:652-655` **[code]**. The collapse is still right; its reason is the opposite of the one given — a residual duplicate is a **deterministic** wrong resolution, not a coin flip, which is what makes the second point a blocker rather than a hygiene note. *Second, the site.* `computeLaneLaunch` runs at the **anchor** (`spawnForStablePane`, `pty.ts:907-919`), i.e. it writes `spawnOptions.env` — and the provider then **rebuilds the env after it**: `...mergeGitConfigEnvProtocol(stripInheritedBuildModeEnv(process.env), args.env)` (`local-pty-provider.ts:675-676`; daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:610`), whose body is `{ ...baseEnv, ...overrideEnv }` (`shared/git-credential-prompt-env.ts:11`) **[code]** — plain exact-key overwrite. A collapse performed upstream of that merge does not survive it: row 19 explicitly supports the Orca process carrying its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`runtime-paths.ts:14-25`) **[code]**, so if the *process* env holds it under a different casing — `Claude_Config_Dir` — the spread reinstates that key **before** `args.env` adds the canonical one, both land in the plain object, and by the rule above the child resolves the inherited shared-lane path. A lane pane then runs on `%USERPROFILE%\.claude` — the other developer's credential — while §2h renders `credentialLane: 'grant'` and §2k bills its usage to the lane owner. R1 and R3 fail silently, on the pivot platform, through the one post-anchor step the §2 preamble's invariant asserts is safe. **Decision: the *comparisons* stay at the anchor (guard 1, guard 3, `hasClaudeAuthEnvConflict`, `applyClaudeEnvPatch`), and the *collapse* moves post-merge into the provider — the in-tree precedent is exact and already sits there.** `collapseWindowsPathEnvKeys(env, requestedEnv)` runs **after** the daemon's merge (`daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:163-190`, called at `:833`, under the comment "Removes the second PATH key only when the daemon's env merge created it") **[code]** — the same problem, the same shape, for PATH. S9a adds the lane-key twin beside it and, because the **local provider has no equivalent at all**, adds one there too, at the corresponding point after `expandWindowsPathEnvironmentVariables(finalEnv)` (`local-pty-provider.ts:847`). The rule is the daemon's own: a one-key lane patch is authoritative, so every other casing of that key is deleted; two or zero came from inherited state and are left to the guards. This is the same shape as §2a's WSL-shell refusal — a decision that cannot be evaluated at the anchor, moved to the step that can. Not a POSIX change: on Linux and macOS the keys stay case-sensitive, which is correct there. **`applyClaudeEnvPatch` is the call site rev 11 named nowhere and newly put on a lane path, so it gets its own sentence.** Its strip is `for (const key of CLAUDE_AUTH_ENV_VARS) { delete baseEnv[key] }` (`claude-accounts/environment.ts:36`; `:35` is the `if (options?.stripAuthEnv)` that opens the block — rev 15 cited `:18-21`, corrected here) plus the exact-case `delete baseEnv.ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` (`:22-23`) **[code]** — and §2k Fact 3 hands the **lane's** `authPreparation` to `rate-limits/claude-pty.ts:247-251` **[code]**, which runs that strip over `{ ...process.env, TERM }`, i.e. over the whole host process environment. On the shared Windows host an inherited lowercase `anthropic_api_key` survives the exact-case strip, reaches the hidden probe's `claude` as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, **outranks** the lane's stored login **[doc]**, and the probe then reports the wrong account's usage under the lane's row — the misattribution class §2k exists to remove, arriving through the one path that does not go near `computeLaneLaunch`. Same file, same fix, one more call site; the probe is the named vector in §5. **And rev 15 fixed only the *auth-var* half of that call site, which is round 4's second major — the same paragraph names `applyClaudeEnvPatch` “the call site rev 11 named nowhere and newly put on a lane path” and then leaves the *lane key* on it with no collapse.** Re-opened: `claude-pty.ts:247-251` builds `{ ...process.env, TERM: 'xterm-256color' }` — a plain `Record`, so the **stored casing** of every inherited variable survives the spread — and `applyClaudeEnvPatch` then writes `baseEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR = patch.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` by **exact key** (`claude-accounts/environment.ts:44-46`) **[code]**. Row 19 explicitly supports the Orca process carrying its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`runtime-paths.ts:14-25`, the read at `:15`) **[code]**; stored as `Claude_Config_Dir`, **both** keys reach `pty.spawn` (`:303-311`) **[code]** and, by this bullet's own rule — Win32 resolves the first case-insensitive key and object order preserves block order (`pty/windows-path-segment-merge.ts:18`, tested `windows-environment-path.test.ts:652-655`) **[code]** — the probe's `claude` runs on `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`, the shared lane. **The collapse decision above is written as a property of every lane key, and this is the one path with no site for it, because the collapse was placed post-merge *inside the provider* and the probe never reaches a provider (row 32).** **Decision: the probe's env build is the *third* collapse site**, beside the provider's and the daemon's — `shared/lane-env-key-case.ts`'s collapse runs over the object `applyClaudeEnvPatch` returns, before `pty.spawn` at `claude-pty.ts:303-311`, so exactly one casing of `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` reaches the hidden `claude`. **What it prevents, spelled out because §2k's join makes each consequence a wrong write rather than a wrong read:** with the shared config dir winning, §2k would (a) attribute the probe's usage to the **lane's** row through the new paneKey/configDir join, (b) hold the **shared** lane's token while the synthetic gate id defers **the lane's** account rotation, and (c) run the post-probe lane identity read over the lane, so a rotation of the *shared* credential would be recorded against the **lane's** row (through rev 31 this third consequence was stated as a watermark move; the watermark is deleted in rev 32 and the read that replaces it lands on the same wrong row, so the hazard and the fix are unchanged). **Latent, not live, and only on the pivot platform — stated rather than used as a reason to defer it:** §6 gate (iv) ships the per-lane pull disabled on `win32` (`claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` returns `null` for every per-account preparation) **[code]**, so the lane arm of row 32 does not run there yet. The collapse lands with the lane arm, in the same slice, because the alternative is a decision stated as universal with a known hole on the platform it was written for. **Mutation proof:** a lane create supplying `claude_config_dir` must be refused/stripped and a lane *probe* launched with a lowercase `anthropic_api_key` in the host env must strip it, and reverting either comparison to exact-case must turn its test red.
   - **ConPTY.** Both spawn implementations pass `useConptyDll: true` on `win32` — the daemon directly (`daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:556-565`, the option at `:563`), and the local provider through `windowsConptyDllOptions()` (`providers/local-pty-utils.ts:206-207`), spread into the primary spawn inside `spawnShellWithFallback` (`:246`, applied at `:275`) and into its fallback chain (`:225`), reached from `local-pty-provider.ts:883` **[code]** — the local half of a claim about "both implementations", uncited in rev 12 and re-opened here — and Windows console membership is probed out-of-process through node-pty's console-list agent (`providers/windows-conpty-process-membership.ts:1-30`) **[code]**. Neither reads or writes an environment variable, so the two-part post-anchor invariant (§2 preamble) is **unaffected by ConPTY**: the env handed to `pty.spawn` is the env the child gets. Stated because the invariant claims to be a property of *every* post-anchor step, and on Windows there is one more step than on Linux.
   - **The WSLENV export sites, re-read.** Unchanged and confirmed at the cited lines: `if (finalEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR) { addWslEnvKeys(finalEnv, ['CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR']) }` (`providers/local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`) and its daemon twin (`daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`) **[code]**. Two facts sharpen §2a's invariant now that Windows is the host rather than a footnote. First, **both sites are nested inside `if (pathWin32.basename(shellPath).toLowerCase() === 'wsl.exe')`** — `local-pty-provider.ts:733` for the local twin, and `pty-subprocess.ts:723` for the daemon one, whose export at `:762-764` sits inside that block; `:775` is a *later, sibling* basename check guarding `addOrcaWslInteropEnv(env)` after the `:723` block closes at `:774`, and cannot gate a line above it (rev 11 cited `:775`; corrected here on re-opening) **[code]**. Meanwhile the *shell* classifier further up the same function is broader — `const isWslShell = Boolean(wslInfo) || pathWin32.basename(shellPath).toLowerCase() === 'wsl.exe'` (`local-pty-provider.ts:707`) **[code]**. So the two classifiers §2a warned about really are different predicates in the same function, **22 lines apart**, with `buildSpawnEnv` (`:712-722`), the `envToDelete` replay (`:725-726`) and the `TERM` restore (`:728-730` — `:728` `if (args.env?.TERM) {`, `:729` the assignment, `:730` the closing brace; rev 19 corrected the §2 preamble's copy of this citation and left this one at `:728-729`, corrected here on re-opening so the two sites agree, round 8's minor) between them — the distance strengthens the point rather than softening it. Second, the export keys on the variable's **mere presence**, so the invariant *"a lane path is never present in a spawn env that takes the WSL branch"* is the only thing standing between a Windows lane path and a distro that cannot use it. On the Windows host this is no longer a hypothetical: it is the default platform. §5 keeps its positive control and mutation proof and adds a Windows-only case — a lane pane whose shell resolves to `wsl.exe` must be **refused in the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm** (`local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`, over the ctx's own `shellPath`/`isWsl` at `:718-719`, using the export gate's own predicate at `:733`; daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:723`), not silently exported. **Not at `computeLaneLaunch`, where rev 12 sited it:** the two classifiers this bullet has just measured at 22 lines apart both live *inside* the provider, and `PtySpawnOptions` (`pty-provider-contract.ts:41-108`) carries neither the resolved shell nor `isWsl` **[code]** — so the anchor cannot evaluate this rule, and §2a moves it to the one site that already has both values and the lane (§2a, the WSLENV invariant).
   - **Correction to a rev-10 [code] claim, since this line was re-opened.** Rev 10 described step (a) of the post-anchor enumeration as the provider's base merge `{ ...process.env, ...args.env }` at `local-pty-provider.ts:676`. The line is `...mergeGitConfigEnvProtocol(stripInheritedBuildModeEnv(process.env), args.env)` (`:675-676`) **[code]** — process env is filtered and the two are merged by a helper rather than spread directly. The substance rev 10 relied on is intact **on POSIX**: `args.env` still wins over the process env by exact-key overwrite (`shared/git-credential-prompt-env.ts:11`, `{ ...baseEnv, ...overrideEnv }`) **[code]**, so the lane patch travelling in `args.env` still wins. **On `win32` it does not, and rev 13 carried the POSIX reading forward as if it were platform-neutral:** exact-key overwrite leaves a differently-cased inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` in the object beside the canonical one, and Win32 resolves the *first* case-insensitive key in block order — the inherited one. That is why the collapse above is sited **after** this merge and not at the anchor. The description was imprecise twice; both corrections are made here rather than left standing.

**(6) Threat model: one `orca serve` under one Windows account, serving both developers.** The Linux statement was "both developers run as the same Unix user, so `0600` protects nothing from a peer who *is* that user". The Windows statement is the same shape and slightly worse. **Say it plainly: on the shared box there is one Windows account, one `orca serve` process under it, and both developers' grants are served by it.** The Windows equivalents of the Linux residuals:
   - **`/proc/<pid>/environ` → the process environment block.** Any process running as the same user can open another's handle with `PROCESS_QUERY_INFORMATION | PROCESS_VM_READ` and read its PEB environment block; `Get-Process`/WMI expose enough to locate it. So the lane path — and §2a already conceded the grant id inside it — is readable by a co-tenant, exactly as on Linux.
   - **`ptrace` → `DebugActiveProcess` / `ReadProcessMemory`.** Same-user debugging needs no extra privilege, so a running `claude`'s in-memory token is readable. `SeDebugPrivilege` is the *administrator* escalation, not the same-user one.
   - **Reading the lane file at rest.** This one is **worse than Linux, and it is worth naming exactly.** The lane's own DACL grants FullControl to three SIDs — the current user, `S-1-5-18`, and **`S-1-5-32-544`, the local Administrators group** (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:71`, `:85-95`) **[code]**. On a Windows **Server** shared by two developers, both are plausibly administrators, and an administrator is inside the lane's ACL **by construction**. So on Windows the "co-tenant can read a resident lane file" residual holds for *any local administrator*, not only for the account Orca runs as. Changing the script's allowed-SID set is not a fix — SYSTEM and Administrators are there because a backup, an installer or a repair otherwise cannot touch the file, and an administrator can rewrite any DACL anyway.
   - **The named pipe — and rev 13 named the wrong one, which matters because this bullet is what scopes every refusal §2d deliberately leaves open.** The endpoint an anonymous local `orca` CLI reaches the **runtime** on is `createRuntimeTransportMetadata` (`runtime/runtime-rpc.ts:1853-1870`): `\\.\pipe\orca-${pid}-${suffix}` on `win32` (`:1864`), `join(userDataPath, 'o-<pid>-<suffix>.sock')` on POSIX (`:1869`) **[code]**. `\\?\pipe\orca-terminal-host-v<version>-<hash>` (`daemon/daemon-spawner.ts:98-110`, win32 branch `:105-107`) **[code]** is the **daemon PTY-host** endpoint — a different pipe, a different client — and rev 13 cited it here, in §2a and in §4. Corrected at all three. **And the substance the right file carries, which rev 13's citation could not:** the in-tree comment reads "named pipes lack the chmod hardening of Unix sockets; a per-runtime suffix avoids a stable, guessable endpoint name" (`:1863`) **[code]**. So on Windows the POSIX socket's mode-bit protection is **replaced by obscurity plus the pipe's default DACL** — not transposed into an equivalent. §2d's "anonymous local caller" and §2a's "local `orca` equivalent over the Unix socket" therefore mean *whoever can open that pipe on this machine*, which on a default Windows ACL is any process of the same user plus administrators — and the per-pid, per-suffix name is a discovery cost, not an access control. Every §2d refusal that is deliberately scoped to leave the anonymous local caller unchanged — `selectClaude` for a lane-less caller, `resolveInheritedLane` case (ii) — is therefore scoped to that set. Unchanged in intent, restated in Windows terms so the envelope is not accidentally read as narrower than it is.
   - **The conclusion is unchanged and should not be softened:** lanes defend against accidental cross-switching and the `selectClaude`-class hijack; they do **not** defend against a hostile co-tenant. On Windows, "co-tenant" additionally includes any local administrator.
   **The alternative, named as an alternative and not recommended.** Two `orca serve` instances under **two Windows user accounts** would make the residuals real boundaries: separate profiles, separate `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`, separate `userData`, DACLs that actually exclude the other person, and separate named pipes. It would make S9's lane machinery largely unnecessary on that box. **Why it is not the recommendation:** it forfeits the thing the owner is building toward — B2 **shared presence**. Two serves are two hosts: R4 dies (neither developer can view or type into the other's terminals, because a terminal belongs to one runtime), the presence registry has nothing to join across, `orchestration.workerStart` cannot span them, one worktree cannot be shared without a second sharing mechanism, and each developer pairs to a different host from their phone. Lanes exist precisely to keep one shared host *and* per-person credentials; two serves buy isolation by giving up the shared host. Recorded so the trade is visible, and so that if the owner ever chooses it, the reason S9 shrinks is on the record.

**(7) The §5 live probes must run on Windows Server, and CI cannot run them.** The §5 in-repo suite (vitest, `config/vitest.config.ts`) runs anywhere; the ACL, junction, 8.3, rename-retry and env-case controls are **Windows-only tests** that must be skipped on other platforms rather than faked — a `describe.runIf(process.platform === 'win32')` shape, with the mutation proofs attached to the Windows arm. The **live** probes are harder: §5's steps (0)–(8) drive a real `claude` binary, a real Keychain or DACL, and a real second developer, and the Linux VPS CI has none of them. **They cannot execute on the Linux VPS CI and must not be marked green there.** They need either a **Windows runner** or the **owner's own Windows Server box**, run by hand before S9b merges. Which live steps are Windows-only: (0)/(0b) `echo %CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR%` in a lane pane under **PowerShell** and **Git Bash** — the two shells the owner named (§2m(8)) — and under `cmd`, which is not a habit but is the resolver's middle fallback (`local-pty-provider.ts:599`) **[code]**; the new **(12b)**, the three-shell repeat of (0b) — PowerShell, Git Bash and `cmd` — **at least one of which must be driven through a per-tab `shellOverride` rather than the host setting** (row 36), which rev 16 introduced in §5 and left out of this list (round 5 minor); the new **(12c)**, the Git Bash environment-passthrough probe §2m(8) gates its refusal on; the new **(9)** DACL read-back (`Get-Acl` on the lane directory: protected, three SIDs, FullControl, no deny); the new **(10)** credential-store probe of item (3); the new **(11)** rename-under-lock probe — push to a lane while a `claude` in it holds `.credentials.json` open, and assert the push succeeds inside the ~750 ms retry window rather than throwing; and **(12)** the 8.3/junction/UNC containment attempts of item (4). Step (8), the macOS Keychain probe, stays macOS-only and stays a gate for macOS provisioning only. Steps (1)–(7b) are platform-neutral in intent but must be **re-run on Windows** as well as on the platform they were first written for, because the pinned-CLI assumption (§4) is per-platform.

**(8) The two native shells the developers actually use — the owner's answer, and what each one does to a lane's environment.** Owner, 2026-08-22: on the shared box the developers use **native Windows terminals only** — the owner works in **Git Bash**, the other developer in **PowerShell**, deliberately not WSL. That resolves §7's first new open question as **native** and **defers S9e** (§2n) out of the S9 delivery order and out of every gate; §2n stays designed and independently shippable, and the trigger that would revive it is named there. Three consequences follow, and two of them are obligations on the lane environment rather than notes.
   - **The shell setting is host-wide, so both shells are in scope for every lane — and on path B the *resolved* shell is per-pane, which rev 15 missed and rev 16 states as round 4's third major.** `terminalWindowsShell` is one setting, read into the provider as `getWindowsShell: () => getSettings?.()?.terminalWindowsShell` (`ipc/pty.ts:2433`) and consumed at `local-pty-provider.ts:596-600` as `args.shellOverride || this.opts.getWindowsShell?.() || process.env.COMSPEC || 'powershell.exe'` **[code]**. Rev 15 disposed of the **first** disjunct by citing path A, where `requestedShellOverride: undefined` (`pty.ts:4529-4536`, the field at `:4532` → `shared/local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:30-67`, the branch at `:41-44`) **[code]** leaves the setting deciding. **Path B does not pass undefined.** `pty:spawn`'s args declare `shellOverride?: string` (`ipc/pty.ts:5923`) and **both** of its resolution sites pass `requestedShellOverride: args.shellOverride` (`:6074`, `:6167`) **[code]**, which `resolveLocalWindowsTerminalRuntimeOptions` prefers over the setting (`shared/local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:41`) **[code]**; the renderer supplies it from the tab (`terminal-pane/pty-connection.ts:3591`, sent at `:3831`; minted at `terminal/terminal-tab-create.ts:39`; a whole `newTerminalWithShell` entry point at `tab-group/useTabGroupWorkspaceModel.ts:656-672`) **[code]**. Path B is **row 24** — the cold-restore respawn, the sleeping-agent wake and the nine renderer launch builders — i.e. the edge every *existing* lane pane re-enters through, so a lane pane's resolved shell is a property of the pane, not of the host. **Two consequences, both carried rather than absorbed.** (1) The fact set is wider than two: a lane pane can resolve to `pwsh.exe` or `cmd.exe` as well as to Git Bash and PowerShell, so the requirement below is *“every fact below holds for every lane, whatever the pane resolved to”*, and the `cmd` statusline note at the end of this section is what makes the third case harmless. (2) `shellOverride` is a **launch-shaping input reaching the provider** that the §2 enumeration did not carry — the same class as row 18, one layer lower and client-reachable — so it is enumerated and dispositioned as **row 36** (allowlisted: it picks a shell binary, never a credential), and a `wsl.exe` override is caught by §2a's provider-sited refusal over the *resolved* `shellPath` (`local-pty-provider.ts:733`) and by nothing upstream of it. There is still no per-principal shell in this design and rev 16 does not add one. Git Bash resolution itself falls back: `resolveWindowsGitBashShellPath(shellFamily)` at `:606`, used at `:622-625`, drops to `powershell.exe` when Git for Windows is not installed **[code]** — one more reason the PowerShell fact is not optional. And **"native only" is a statement about habits, not an enforcement:** a project runtime of kind `wsl` still forces `shellOverride: 'wsl.exe'` (`shared/local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:52-57`) **[code]** whatever the developers type, so §2a's `terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported` refusal remains the enforcement and §2n remains the slice that would lift it.
   - **Git Bash: MSYS rewrites path-looking *arguments*, not the environment block — which is what makes the lane path safe there, and exactly why it must stay an environment value. [inferred], unverified in this tree, gated by §5 step (12c).** The MSYS/Git-for-Windows runtime converts POSIX-looking values on the **command line** into Windows form when it spawns a native (non-MSYS) program, and `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV` is the documented escape hatch for suppressing that conversion; environment variables are handed to the child **unchanged** except for the names the runtime treats as path lists, which is what `MSYS2_ENV_CONV_EXCL` selects **[doc/inferred — neither behaviour is observable from this tree]**. `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is not one of those names, and Orca never puts a lane path on a command line: `computeLaneLaunch` writes it into `spawnOptions.env` and the provider hands it to the child in the environment block (§2 preamble) **[code]**. **So a native `claude.exe` launched from a Git Bash pane receives the Windows lane path verbatim, exactly as it would from PowerShell**, and no Git-Bash arm is needed on `computeLaneLaunch`. **What that makes load-bearing, stated because it was previously incidental:** the rule *never pass the lane path as an argument* — `claude --config-dir <lane>` is precisely the shape §2m(4) already refuses at the capture surfaces, and it is also the one shape MSYS would rewrite, so the two rules now protect each other. Orca already branches on Git Bash inside this same env build — `if (isWindowsGitBashShellPath(shellPath)) { spawnEnv.CHERE_INVOKING ??= '1' }` (the branch at `local-pty-provider.ts:701`, its comment at `:702` — "Git for Windows login files otherwise cd to $HOME, ignoring node-pty's cwd for repo-scoped terminals" — and the body at `:703`; rev 15 cited `:700-703`, whose `:700` is `spawnEnv.PYTHONUTF8 ??= '1'`, corrected here on re-opening) **[code]** — so if the probe shows otherwise the fix has a site and is one branch, not a redesign. **Failure mode if (12c) fails, and its refusal:** the value arrives as `/c/Users/…`, a native `claude.exe` does not find the lane, the pane still renders `credentialLane: 'grant'` (§2h) and §2k bills its usage to the lane owner — the misattribution class this design exists to remove — so a failing (12c) **refuses Git Bash lane panes** (`terminal.lane_shell_unsupported`, the same fail-closed shape as the WSL refusal and enforced at the same site, the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm, which is where the resolved shell is) until the lane preparation carries a Git-Bash arm. **One thing the shell does *not* change:** the managed statusline is the **cmd** variant on this host whatever the pane's shell is — `getManagedStatusLineScript` branches on `target === 'local' && process.platform === 'win32'` (`claude/statusline-script.ts:17-18`) and reads `%CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR%` (`:57-58`) **[code]**, which `cmd` resolves case-insensitively — so §2k's attribution path is identical under both shells, and §2m(5)'s collapse is what makes that read unambiguous: `cmd` will resolve *a* casing, and exactly one must survive to the child.
   - **PowerShell: `$PROFILE` runs at pane start, after every guard in this design — a §4 residual of the shell-rc class, named and not closed.** PowerShell executes its profile scripts when a session starts **[doc]**, so one line — `$env:CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR = 'C:\Users\Public\x'`, or `Remove-Item Env:CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` — takes effect **after** `computeLaneLaunch`, **after** the provider's post-merge collapse (§2m(5)) and **after** every assertion guard 3 makes on the spawn env, because all of those act on the environment block handed to the shell and the profile runs inside the shell. Git Bash carries the exact twin (`/etc/profile`, `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.bashrc` — the files `CHERE_INVOKING` exists because of), which is why this is stated as a **class** and not as a PowerShell quirk. **It is not closable host-side, and it sits inside §4's envelope rather than outside it:** the profile is a file belonging to the one Windows user both developers run as, and §4's shell-equivalence concession already covers files that user can write — a co-tenant who can edit that profile can equally `type` the lane credential while it is resident. **What it changes is what a green test proves,** so it is stated rather than left implicit: §5 step (0b) reads `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` *inside* the pane, after the profile has run, and is therefore the only check in this document that can observe a profile rewrite at all — a lane whose profile rewrites the variable turns (0b) red instead of passing silently. Named residual in §4; **no refusal**, because a refusal would have to inspect the user's own dotfiles, which §2j declines for the same reason it declines to police `~/.claude/settings.json`.

### (n) WSL on the Windows host — slice S9e

Rev 10 deferred the WSL-visible lane with a one-line "later slice". On a Windows Server host that is no longer a footnote: if the two developers run their agents inside WSL, S9a–S9d isolate **nothing** for them, because every WSL launch falls through to §2i's per-*account* WSL branch and both people's WSL panes share it. So it is designed here, as **S9e**, independently shippable. **The owner has since answered (§2m(8)): native shells only — Git Bash and PowerShell, deliberately not WSL — so S9e is deferred**, out of the S9 delivery order and out of every gate, and this section is a design held ready rather than a slice waiting on a question. What would revive it is named rather than left to taste: a project runtime of kind `wsl` forces a WSL shell whatever anyone types (`shared/local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:52-57`) **[code]**, and until S9e ships such a lane pane is **refused** (`terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported`, §2a) rather than silently un-isolated — which is why deferring the slice costs correctness nothing.

**The precedent is complete and is not a sketch.** Orca already builds per-account, Linux-side, Orca-owned auth directories inside a distro. `createManagedWslAuthStorage` mints `wslLinuxAuthPath = ${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-accounts/${accountId}/auth` (`claude-accounts/service.ts:939`), writes its ownership marker **inside the distro** through `wsl.exe -d <distro> -- bash -lc 'mkdir -p … && printf … > …'` (`:940-953`), and converts the result to a Windows-visible UNC path with `toWindowsWslPath` (`:954`) **[code]**. Containment is enforced *on the Linux side*: `assertManagedAuthPath` refuses a path whose Linux form is outside `/.local/share/orca/claude-accounts/` or does not end in `/auth` (`:969-977`), and on `win32` re-canonicalizes **inside the distro** — `readlink -f` on both candidate and root, the marker file read and compared, and a `case "$candidate_real" in "$managed_root_real"/*/auth)` pattern match, exit 35 otherwise (`:978-1014`) **[code]**. And the launch side already knows how to split the two paths: `getPreparation`'s managed-WSL branch returns `configDir: activeAccount.managedAuthPath` (the Windows UNC path Orca reads and writes through) alongside `wslLinuxConfigDir` and `envPatch: { CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR: activeAccount.wslLinuxAuthPath }` (the Linux path the CLI sees) (`runtime-auth-service.ts:683-697`, esp. `:686-692`) **[code]**. **S9e is that machinery re-parameterised from `accountId` to `principalId`** — a per-lane Linux-side config dir at `${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-lanes/${principalId}` — not a new mechanism.

**How the blob reaches the distro side.** It does not travel through `wsl.exe`. The host writes it to the **UNC path** (`\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\home\<user>\.local\share\orca\claude-lanes\<principalId>\.credentials.json`) with the same lane writer as a native lane, after `assertManagedAuthPath`-style canonicalization inside the distro. Three consequences, each stated because each is a real difference from a native lane: **(i) the §2m(1) DACL is not run there** — not "runs and no-ops": `restrictWindowsPathSync` returns `false` when it cannot set a DACL (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]**, and §2m(1)'s fail-closed rule is therefore carved out by `parseWslUncPath` rather than applied and ignored (§2m(1), corrected in rev 14). The file's permissions are the **Linux** file's, so the lane writer's WSL arm must `chmod 0700`/`0600` inside the distro (the existing marker write at `:949` uses a bare `printf >` with no mode, so this is an addition, not an assumption) **and read those modes back inside the distro, provisioning failing closed on the read-back** — the substitute for §2m(1)'s DACL verification, in the place S9e's isolation actually lives; and the **UNC root itself is permitted by name**, under §2m(4)'s carve-out for the `wsl.localhost`/`wsl$` forms `parseWslUncPath` recognises (`shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`) **[code]** — it is the local WSL redirector for this Windows user, not a remote share, which is the reachability half §2m(4) now states and this clause no longer has to imply; **(ii) `writeFileAtomically`'s rename retry still applies**, because the write goes through the 9p/`\\wsl.localhost` redirector from the Windows side and can still hit `EBUSY`; **(iii)** the wipe (§2f) must sweep the Linux-side directory, including both temp-name shapes (§2m(2)).

**How the WSLENV invariant changes — and this is the part that must not be got wrong.** §2a's invariant today is absolute: *"a lane path is never present in a spawn env that takes the WSL branch."* S9e **narrows** it rather than deleting it, and the narrowing is the whole slice: *a **Windows** lane path is never present in a spawn env that takes the WSL branch; a **Linux** lane path is present there and is exported through `WSLENV` exactly as the per-account WSL path is today* (`local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`, daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`) **[code]**. Concretely, the lane preparation gains a WSL arm that emits `wslLinuxConfigDir` instead of the host lane path — resolved the way `getPreparation`'s managed-WSL branch already resolves one, from the *target*, which `normalizeClaudeAccountSelectionTarget` gives `computeLaneLaunch` at the anchor (§2a) — while the **guard** on what actually crossed sits where the resolved shell does, in the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm (`local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`, daemon twin `pty-subprocess.ts:723`), and becomes a **typed** assertion rather than a presence test — **scoped to lane-pinned spawns, not to WSL-branch spawns generally**: on a WSL-branch spawn **of a lane-pinned pane**, assert `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` parses as a Linux path under that distro's *lane* root (`${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-lanes/${principalId}`), and refuse otherwise. **The scoping is load-bearing, not fastidiousness: stated over WSL-branch spawns generally the assertion breaks the feature S9e is layered on.** A lane-**less** WSL pane — the case this section itself keeps alive, below, as `credentialLane: 'wsl'` — carries `envPatch: { CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR: activeAccount.wslLinuxAuthPath }` (`runtime-auth-service.ts:683-697`, patch at `:693`) **[code]** over a path minted as `${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-accounts/${accountId}/auth` (`claude-accounts/service.ts:939`) **[code]**, which lives under the **account** root and not the lane root; a lane-root assertion applied to it would refuse **every managed-WSL account launch** on any host where S9e had shipped. **The lane-less predicate is therefore stated separately and is unchanged from today:** either no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` at all, or the selected managed account's `wslLinuxAuthPath` under that distro's `claude-accounts` root — and what stays refused for it, exactly as before S9e, is a **Windows** path, so §2a's invariant is undiminished for every pane S9e does not provision. Deleting that assertion must turn a named test red — and the mutation proof is more important after S9e than before it, because before S9e the guard was "no lane path at all", which is trivially checkable, and after it the guard is "the *right kind* of lane path", which is not.

**Ownership, adopt and presence for a WSL pane.** Unchanged in shape, and that is the point of doing it as a slice rather than a special case: a WSL lane pane is bound to a principal at the funnel like any other pane (§2a), the ownership predicate and the adopt/materialize gate compare principals with no WSL arm at all, the wake and split inherit from the pane record, and the wipe is the principal's. What changes is one label: the pane renders `credentialLane: 'grant'` (owned, isolated) instead of rev 10's `'wsl'` (isolated per account, not per person), and `'wsl'` survives to mean exactly what it means today — a WSL pane whose distro has **no** lane, either because S9e is not shipped or because that distro was never provisioned. So a mixed host is legible on the presence row rather than ambiguous, and a person who provisions one distro and not another sees which is which.

**What S9e cannot do, stated.** A WSL distro is registered per **Windows user**, so on the one-account shared host both developers use the **same distro instance** with the same Linux uid — the §2m(6) residual, transplanted: Linux mode bits inside the distro protect a lane from nothing, because the peer is the same Linux user there too. S9e buys the same thing native lanes buy — authority isolation, not confidentiality — and it must be described that way in the UI, not as "your WSL agents are now private".

**Cost, honestly.** New: a `principal-credential-lane-wsl.ts` (~130) holding the Linux-side mint, the in-distro canonicalization and the chmod, plus a WSL arm on `computeLaneLaunch` (~40 lines) and a WSL arm on the lane writer and wipe (~50). Edits: `runtime-auth-service.ts:683-697` gains a lane-before-account branch for WSL targets, and the `credentialLane` union gains nothing (it reuses `'grant'`). Tests: the WSLENV narrowing with its mutation proof, in-distro containment (junction, escape, foreign marker) mirrored from `assertManagedAuthPath`'s shape, and a live step. **The real cost is not lines, it is that every live probe doubles**: each of §5's steps must be run again inside WSL, on the Windows host, because the classifier gap of §2m(5) means a pane can be host-classified one way and shell-classified the other. Budget it as **the largest slice after S9a**, and, if it is ever revived, do not ship it in the same change as S9a: the invariant it narrows is the one guarding a credential-directory mismatch, and it deserves to land alone.

## 3. Wire changes

All gated behind one new runtime capability, **bumped in rev 32 from `agent.identity-lanes.v1` to `agent.identity-lanes.v2`**, declared beside its siblings (`src/shared/protocol-version.ts:109`, `AGENT_IDENTITY_LANES_RUNTIME_CAPABILITY`) and added to `RUNTIME_CAPABILITIES` (`:111-150`) **[code]**; the client check follows `src/cli/handlers/account.ts:276-284`'s `capabilities?.includes(...)` precedent (check at `:278`) **[code]**. **The bump is not cosmetic and is not a Rule-1 addition:** v2 hosts do not implement `accounts.lane.push`, `pullRotated`, `clear`, `setDelegableAccounts` or `requestSwitch` at all, so a v1 client that finds `agent.identity-lanes.v2` must not attempt them — it has to say "update this client", which is exactly what the capability string is for. **That capability is host-advertised and client-checked, and it says one thing only — "this host has lanes, in the v2 shape".** Nothing in §2a, §2d, §2e or §2l reads `ctx.clientCapabilities`, and rev 19's deletion of the client-declared namespace stands.

**Rule classification note.** The three rules in `docs/reference/remote-wire-compatibility.md` do not cover new RPC *methods*. Rule 2 is about stream **opcodes**, and its rationale is that an unknown opcode "vanishes, and the feature behind it appears to hang" (`:31-44`) **[code]**. New methods do the opposite: `dispatcher.ts:44-54` and `:139-150` return `method_not_found` loudly **[code]**. So new methods are a **fourth case** the doc does not cover: failure is loud, but the client should still capability-gate so the user sees "update the host". Add that distinction to the wire doc alongside S9.

| # | Change | Rule | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **`accounts.lane.loginStart({ expectedEmail })`** → `{ loginSessionId, authorizeUrl, expiresAt }`; **`accounts.lane.loginSubmitCode({ loginSessionId, code })`** → `{ status, identity }`; **`accounts.lane.loginCancel({ loginSessionId })`**; **`accounts.lane.loginStatus({ loginSessionId })`** — **rev 32 replaces the deleted `accounts.lane.push` row with these four** | new-method (4th case) + capability gate (`v2`) | The lane is derived from `principalOf(ctx.pairedDeviceId)` and there is **no lane or principal field in any of the four schemas** (§2d). Three bindings, each a named refusal rather than an implicit property: the session is started only by the principal's **designated** grant (`accounts.lane.login_not_designated`; `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` when the principal has none), **at most one in flight per lane** (`accounts.lane.login_already_in_flight` — refused, never queued), and the code is accepted only from the **same grant and the same session** while the minting process lives (`accounts.lane.login_session_unknown`, `accounts.lane.login_session_expired`). `authorizeUrl` is the URL the CLI **printed**, never the browser-opener URL, and the handler asserts its `redirect_uri` host is `platform.claude.com` before it leaves the host (§2b, §5). `expectedEmail` is passed through as `claude auth login --claudeai --email <…>` and the resulting identity is verified from `auth status --json` **before** `laneState` becomes `loaded`, refused `accounts.lane.login_identity_mismatch` and swept otherwise (I6, §2b). **`expectedEmail` is required and non-empty, and its scope is corrected in rev 33:** it is *caller-supplied*, so I6 binds the lane to the account **the starting human named** and is a control against mis-authentication, not against an attacker who names someone else's email (§2b, §4). **`loginStatus` returns state, `expiresAt`, the attempt count and — only on completion — the verified identity, and never re-serves `authorizeUrl`** (re-serving it would defeat the delivery binding for the principal's other grants), **and rev 34 gives it the caller rule the other three already had: accepted only from the grant that started the session or from the host's own surface, every other caller refused `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown`** — the session id is published to every bound grant on the `login-started` frame (row 2), so without the comparison the obvious map lookup answers an audience binding (i-b) refused at start; **`loginCancel` is accepted only from the grant that started the session**, or from the host's own consent/CLI surface; **and `loginSubmitCode` is accepted only from the grant that started the session, never from any grant into a CLI-started one, which has no owning grant (§2d(iii), rev 34)**. **Sessions are process-local (§2d):** the map is never persisted, so an Orca restart, an unexpected child exit, and a designation clear, **a designation *move* (rev 34)** or a revoke of the owning grant each end the session, and every later submit is the named `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown` rather than an untyped `EPIPE` into a dead pipe. **The single-in-flight lock is a property of the session, not of this RPC surface**, so the host-inline `orca lane login` takes it too (§2d(iv), §9 step 0 item 8). `code` is rate-limited, never logged, never written outside the session, and never passed through PTY stdout. `authorizeUrl` is delivered **only** on the requesting grant's own connection, never on a peer-visible row (§2d(ii)). **Three things rev 34 adds to this row.** `expectedEmail` is required on **every** entry point that mints a session, not only on this RPC (§2d, §6's S9-L2, §9 step 0 item 8); a `loginStart` that would take the lane past `MAX_LANE_LOGINS` (eight) is refused `accounts.lane.login_store_full` **before any spawn** (§2c); and a restart ends the session *record* but neither the directory nor the child, so the store is reconciled at startup against the lane's own account index — **`<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json`, in the lane and not in Orca's persisted state (rev 35)**, deleting unindexed directories only against an index that parsed and **quarantining** them when it did not (§2a, §2f) |
| 2 | `accounts.lane.status`, `accounts.lane.statusSubscribe` (streaming) and `accounts.lane.statusUnsubscribe`, **carrying login-session events as of rev 32**; **`accounts.lane.pullRotated` and `accounts.lane.clear` are deleted**, and **`accounts.lane.logout` replaces `clear`** | new-method + capability gate; **Rule 1** for the added `status` fields; **Rule 3** for `delegatedGrantId`'s meaning change | `statusSubscribe` is a new *method*, not a new stream **opcode**, so Rule 2 does not apply; its subscription rides the one `registerSubscriptionCleanup`/`cleanupSubscriptionsForConnection` join `accounts.subscribe` already uses, and a subscriber is filed by its **grant** with the principal re-resolved on every delivery (`runtime/lane-status-stream.ts:38-40`) **[code]**, so an unbind or a re-bind revokes the stream without touching the socket. **The `LaneStatusFrame` union (`lane-status-stream.ts:24-36`) [code] loses `switch-requested` and `switch-failed` and gains three login-session frames** — `login-started` (carrying `loginSessionId` and `expiresAt`, *never* the URL, which goes only on the starting grant's `loginStart` reply — the id rides so the principal's other grants can render “a login is in progress” and correlate the completion frame, **not** so they can call `loginStatus`, which refuses them: row 1, §2d, rev 34), `login-completed` (carrying the verified identity) and `login-failed` (carrying a refusal code and the host's complete sentence). `status` loses the watermark's `refreshTokenSha256`, the `delegable` list, `heldDelegatedAccountId` and `delegationCleared`, and gains the lane's **own** account list (id, verified identity, owner-set label, which one is active — Rule 1, additive), **which is a projection of the lane's persisted account *index*, the store's authority as of rev 34, and never a directory walk (§2a, §2c)**. `delegatedGrantId` **stays on the wire and changes meaning** from "the grant that may push" to "the grant that may start a login and see the URL" (§2d(i)) — Rule 3, the name kept for the same reason §2h keeps `credentialLane: 'grant'`. **`accounts.lane.logout`** wipes **every** credential artifact of that lane — `.credentials.json`, the `.claude.json` `oauthAccount` block, **every `<lane>/claude-accounts/<laneAccountId>/auth` login directory** and every `*.tmp` sibling of any of them (§2f, corrected in rev 33: through rev 32 this row said "the lane's credential", singular, while §2c had already given a lane N logins; **and corrected again in rev 34: the third class is a directory walk, so *every* `<laneAccountId>` directory goes, indexed or not and marker-valid or not, because the crashed login's has neither** — §2c rule (2), §2f) — and, when §5 clears it, runs `claude auth logout` in the lane first; it refuses `accounts.lane.logout_incomplete` rather than reporting a sweep that did not finish (§2f) |
| 3 | `accounts.selectClaude` refused **only for a caller that holds a lane** (`accounts.selection_out_of_scope`); **the target-residency refusals on `selectClaude` and `removeClaude` are deleted**; `list`/`subscribe` per-connection projection unchanged | **Rule 3** — host-published meaning changes for old clients with no wire change, **in both directions this time** | Rev 32 *removes* two Rule-3 refusals and keeps one. The caller-scope refusal stands and now points at a real verb (`accounts.lane.selectAccount`, row 9) instead of at a push. The residency edge is **gone for every caller class**, including the anonymous local shell and the renderer: L1 is retired (§2e), so `accounts.lane.account_resident_elsewhere` and `accounts.lane.residency_unverifiable` are deleted, `assertManagedClaudeAccountNotLaneResident` is deleted from `claude-accounts/service.ts:415` and `:472`, and `assertClaudeAccountNotDelegatedToLane` from `:475` **[code]** — the two lines that produced the 2026-08-27 incident. **An old client that learned to expect a refusal now succeeds**, which is the benign direction, and the client needs no change. With zero lanes nothing changes and the projection is byte-identical to today |
| 4 | `credentialLane` required on `TerminalCreateOptions` **and on `createManagedWorktree`'s args**, derived at every spawning RPC — `terminal.create`, `terminal.createAgentSession`/`ensureAgentSession`, `session.tabs.createTerminal`, `worktree.create`, `orchestration.workerStart` (**both** topology paths, `orchestration-worker-topology.ts:66` and `:137`), federated worker start and federated worktree create (`orchestration-federation.ts:171`, `:90`), `terminal.split` (inherit), **and `worktree.activate`, which gains `pairedDeviceId` for the sleeping-agent wake, and `terminal.recoverPane`, and `session.tabs.createTerminal`, whose lane callers are routed to the host create path, and `session.tabs.activate`, whose materialize keeps its `identity` and is gated by the adopt predicate instead (§2a, row 35)** — plus `lanePrincipalId` on the pane record, on the sleeping-agent record, on `notifier.splitTerminal` opts and on the persisted host-session binding, **carrying a principal id and never a device id** (§2a, §2h) | **Rule 3** for derivation (no new client-supplied param on any of them) | Derivation is host-observed — `ctx.pairedDeviceId` for the direct-create RPCs, and for the `inherit` edges the source pane's own `lanePrincipalId` **plus §2a's ownership predicate**; no client-supplied lane param anywhere. **The federated worktree create (`orchestration-federation.ts:90`) is the one site neither derivation reaches** — `lineage: { noParent: true }` (`:106`) leaves no source pane and its only caller identity is a home-peer fingerprint (`:63`), not a `pairedDeviceId` **[code]** — so it derives from the **link's** principal binding and **fails closed** (`terminal.lane_link_unbound`) when the link has none, which today is always (§2a). `orchestration.workerStart` and `terminal.split` newly destructure `pairedDeviceId` (`orchestration-workers.ts:29`, `terminal.ts:1276`); `RuntimeAgentSessionRpcCaller` and `worktree.activate` gain one (internal types, not wire); `session.tabs.activate` already receives it in ctx (`session-tabs.ts:41`) and merely spends it as caller identity as well as `clientNavigationId`, so that edge is not a wire change at all. The derived value is **bound to the pane**, not carried onward as a launch option — that is rev 8's correction |
| 5 | Lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` applied to **every** lane-pinned spawn, not only command-detected Claude launches (§2a); the whole launch **computed** for a lane per §2's closure principle — client `env`/`envToDelete`/`agentEnv` scrubbed of config-dir and auth keys, `agentArgs`/`agentCommand` allowlisted, and host-wide `settings.agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` **ignored**; `credentialLane`/`laneState` on terminal + presence rows, **plus the additive optional `laneWipePending` of rev 16 (Rule 1 — old clients ignore it and render `absent` alone, §2f/§2h)**, enforced by one `computeLaneLaunch` at `pty.ts`'s spawn convergence (`spawnForStablePane` `:907-918` plus the ensure spawn `:4993`) so the renderer-backed create, the mobile-session create, the sleeping-agent wake and cold restore are covered on the same anchor, with the pane's lane also threaded into the provider's `buildSpawnEnv` ctx (`:2449-2492` → `local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`); `agentStatusHooksEnabled` is read lane-scoped (row 16) and an OpenClaude launch into a lane is refused (§2a) | **Rule 1** for the fields, **Rule 3** for the silent strip, the arg refusal, and the newly-armed auth-env refusal on plain-shell lane panes | An old client sending a redirecting `agentArgs`, `envToDelete: ['CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR']`, or an `env` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` on a lane pane now gets a typed refusal it has no string for; a client whose host-wide `agentDefaultArgs` silently shaped lane launches now finds they do not; a client that set `focus`/`rendererBacked` on a lane create gets a background-spawned, revealed pane instead of a renderer-created one, and a lane holder's `session.tabs.createTerminal` gets a host-minted `tabId` where it used to get the renderer's — matrix rows below. **On `win32` the lane-key *collapse* is applied post-merge inside the provider and the daemon, not at the anchor** (`local-pty-provider.ts:847+`, `daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:833`'s `collapseWindowsPathEnvKeys` precedent), because the provider rebuilds the env after `computeLaneLaunch` runs (§2m(5)); the case-insensitive **comparisons** stay at the anchor |

A watermark row left behind by a pre-S9-L3 build is ignored on read and dropped; the old "no watermark → first push accepted" migration note goes with the push |
| 7 | `configDir`/`sourceHome` containment refusal on `addClaudeFromConfigDir`/`addCodexFromHome`; `DeviceRegistry` `loadSucceeded` flag gating orphan reconciliation | local behavior, not wire | Both apply to the anonymous local caller too — that is the point (§2a, §2d) |
| 8 | **`principalId` on `DeviceEntry`** (`device-registry.ts:25-41`) **[code]**, minted and bound only by the host-side consent action; every lane-keyed structure re-keyed from `pairedDeviceId` to `principalId` — lane directory, ownership predicate, adopt gate, wipe predicate, presence owner label, **login-session authority** (`delegatedGrantId` — this row said "push authorization" through rev 32; the value survives, re-meant, and the residency index that stood beside it here is deleted outright, §2d(i)/§2e), **the pane's persisted binding row (`lanePrincipalId`, resolved at bind time — row 4)**, the sleeping-agent record and the revoke wipe's survivor query (§2a, §2f) | local schema for the field; **Rule 3** for the meaning change | No client-supplied field anywhere: derivation is socket → `device.deviceId` (`runtime-rpc.ts:1384`) → registry row. A row with no `principalId` is a lane-less grant and behaves exactly as today. **Old-client consequence:** a person's phone and desktop now share one lane, so a switch from one moves the other's terminals — intended (Q1), and invisible on the wire |
| 9 | **`accounts.lane.selectAccount({ laneAccountId })`** and **`accounts.lane.removeAccount({ laneAccountId })`** — the in-lane switch and the removal of one of the lane's own logins (§2c) — **replacing the deleted `accounts.lane.requestSwitch`, the delegable-account list, the `switch-requested` frame and the entire delegated-selection flow** | new-method (4th case) + capability gate (`v2`) | The lane is derived from `principalOf(ctx.pairedDeviceId)`; **any** grant bound to the principal may switch, including the phone, because a switch moves no secret and needs no other device to be reachable (§2l). `laneAccountId` is a lane-local id the host minted at login; an unknown one is refused `accounts.lane.account_unknown`. `selectAccount` is the atomic `0600` rewrite of §2c under `beginClaudeAuthSwitch(laneId)` (`live-pty-gate.ts:205`) **[code]**, so it fences spawns in that lane and only that lane; it completes synchronously and never returns `pending`. **Deleted with the old row:** `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` and the pooled-shared-control liveness precondition rounds 7–9 built for it, `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`, `accounts.lane.switch_lane_cleared`, and the `delegatedGrantId`-as-lease-holder read this precondition performed. `removeAccount` refuses removing the lane's **active** login unless the lane is being logged out (§2f). **Rev 34 states the serialization the second writer made necessary, and rev 35 completes it:** `selectAccount` enters `lane-auth-state.ts`'s per-lane write queue (`serializeLaneWrite`, `:102-109`) **[code]** *before* taking `beginClaudeAuthSwitch`, and so does the login session's capture step, so a phone tapping an account while a capture lands **waits** rather than colliding. **§2f's wipe is that queue's third member and takes precedence** (`principal-lane-lifecycle.ts:210`, wired at `lane-credential-coordinator.ts:87`) **[code]**: it marks the lane wipe-pending before entering, and both of these re-check that mark **after** acquiring the queue — the capture also re-checking that its session is still live — refused `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress` if either changed while they waited, rather than writing into a lane a logout has just certified empty (§2c, §2f). The guard belongs **inside** the turn: `lane-wire-authority.ts:112` vs `:120-122` **[code]** is the shipped instance of putting it outside. The gate's bare `Error` (`live-pty-gate.ts:205-210`) **[code]** becomes the named `accounts.lane.switch_in_progress` on the defensive arm the queue makes unreachable (§2c, §5) **Rev 36:** the capture step refuses `accounts.lane.login_cancelled` when its session was cancelled before its queue turn; the wipe-pending mark is advisory only (§2c). |
| 10 | **Per-lane usage pull (Q7)**, unchanged in mechanism: a `getManagedUsagePanelAuthPreparation`-shaped preparation per loaded lane feeding `resolveOAuthCredentialReadOptions` (`claude-fetcher.ts:236-248`) **[code]** and row 32's probe (`claude-pty.ts:247-311`); `paneKey` on `ClaudeStatusLineRateLimits`; `laneAccountLabel` + `laneUsage` on terminal/agent rows (§2k) | **Rule 1** for the two row fields and the local type; local behavior for the pull | The row join is still `paneKey → lane → current account → usage`, not a config-dir string compare, and the probe still scrubs inherited pane identity and still collapses lane env keys on `win32` at its third collapse site (§2k Fact 3, §2m(5)). **Two rev-32 changes.** (a) The preparation now reads the lane's **own** grant rather than a copy of the desktop's, so the probe's `cli-observed` rotation is unambiguously that lane's business — and the fetcher's own refresh arm (`claude-fetcher.ts:1215-1230`) is re-gated from `!isClaudeAccountDelegatedToLane(account.id)` (`:1216`) **[code]** to a **lane-preparation** predicate: Orca never rotates a lane's chain, always, not only when a lease says so (§2e). (b) `laneAccountLabel` is now the **owner-set** label of §2b rather than a pushed `displayName`. **Unchanged and still gated on Windows** by `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` (§2k Fact 2, §6) |
| 12 | ~~`accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts`~~ — **deleted in rev 32** | — | Rev 22 added this row because §2l's phone flow needed a list for the phone to tap and §2b forbade the list riding the push (§8 item 2). Under per-lane login the phone taps the **lane's own** account list, which the host already holds and publishes on `status` (row 2). Deleted with it: `runtime/lane-delegation-directory.ts`, `claudeLaneDelegationRows`, `ClaudeLaneDelegableAccount`, `MAX_LANE_DELEGABLE_ACCOUNTS`, `LANE_CLIENT_REF_MAX_LENGTH`, and both `accounts.lane.delegable_*` refusals (`shared/claude-lane-delegation.ts:13-33`, `shared/claude-lane-refusals.ts:22-23`) **[code, by deletion]**. **This un-ships the privacy widening §7's host-pivot question 10 and §8 item 5 were opened for**: the host no longer holds the owner's ticked account names or any opted-in email, and §2b's original sentence is true again |
| 11 | **S9e (§2n)**: per-lane Linux-side config dir inside the distro, `${home}/.local/share/orca/claude-lanes/${principalId}`; `computeLaneLaunch` gains a WSL arm emitting the **Linux** path; the WSLENV invariant narrows from "no lane path" to "only a Linux lane path, under this distro's lane root", asserted in the provider's lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm where the resolved shell lives (§2a), not at `computeLaneLaunch` | local behavior, not wire | **Deferred by the owner's answer of 2026-08-22 — native shells only (§2m(8)) — so this row is a design held ready, not a scheduled delivery:** S9e is out of the S9 delivery order and out of every §6 gate, nothing in S9b–S9d waits on it, and the revival trigger is a WSL-forced pane (§2n), not a preference. Independently shippable and **not** to be landed with S9a. Presence relabels a provisioned WSL pane `credentialLane: 'grant'`; `'wsl'` survives to mean "WSL pane with no lane", so a mixed host stays legible |

**Degradation matrix.** **Rev 32 rewrites every row that named a push, a lease, L1, R-DR2 or the delegated switch, and deletes the rows whose subject no longer exists.** Rows about the launch anchor, the pane binding, Windows paths and the closure principle are untouched — none of them is about where a credential came from.

| Peer | Behavior |
|---|---|
| New desktop / old host | Capability absent → the lane verbs are unavailable; the client keeps host-wide switching and shows the shared-host warning. Unchanged in shape from rev 31; what it can no longer do is push |
| **New client / host on `agent.identity-lanes.v1`** | **New in rev 32.** A v2 client must not fall back to `accounts.lane.push`: a v1 host has no per-lane store and no login sessions, so a push would load a lane in the *deleted* model on a host that will be upgraded out from under it. The client renders "this host needs updating for per-lane sign-in" and offers no lane verb at all |
| Old desktop / new host | Its `accounts.lane.push` gets `method_not_found` — loudly, per the 4th-case classification, never silently. Its terminals launch in the shared lane as today, and its `selectClaude` is **unchanged in every case**, including the one rev 31 refused: naming an account a lane is signed into now succeeds (§2d) |
| Mobile (either developer's) | Identified (`runtime-rpc.ts:1376-1387`) **[code]** but normally **bound to no principal**, so its terminals land in the shared lane and it keeps host-wide account control. **Once bound to a principal it shares that person's lane and gains two real capabilities rather than one refusal:** `accounts.lane.selectAccount` among the lane's own logins, always, and — when it holds the designation — `accounts.lane.loginStart` (§2l).
