On the **current** mobile client the pre-S9-L2 behaviour is still a refusal: `mobile/app/h/[hostId]/accounts.tsx:228`/`:237` **[code]** sends `accounts.selectClaude` unconditionally, which §2d refuses `accounts.selection_out_of_scope` once the caller's principal holds a lane. S9-L2 budgets the mobile edit that routes it to `accounts.lane.selectAccount` and renders the lane's own account list |
| Second **grant**, same human (phone, tablet, second desktop) | **Q1 = YES:** bound to the same principal, so it shares **one** lane. Rev 32 removes what used to make this row complicated: there is no lease to read, no suppression to perform, and no `push_not_delegated` to refuse, because a second desktop of the same principal holds **its own** grants and rotates **its own** chains. Its Orca still refreshes its own managed store, and that has no effect on the lane's chain. What it may not do without the designation is *start a login* for that lane (`accounts.lane.login_not_designated`); what it may always do is switch among the lane's existing logins |
| **A lane whose designated device is a phone, or is offline** | **Rewritten in rev 32, and it is the row that changed most.** Offline no longer matters for a *switch*: any bound grant switches directly, with no other device involved, so `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` and `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out` are deleted (§2l). Offline matters only for *starting a login*, and there it is not a refusal about reachability at all — the designated grant simply is not the one asking, so the asking grant gets `accounts.lane.login_not_designated` and the remedy is one re-tick. **A designated phone is a working configuration, not an inert one:** it can display a URL and take a code, so it can load the lane |
| **A wrongly-designated grant** | **No longer inert — the honest correction to §2a, made in rev 32.** Under push a wrong tick produced nothing at all. Under per-lane login the wrong device *of the right person* can complete a login and load that person's own lane. It is bounded by the fact that designation ranges only over grants already **bound** to that principal (§2a rule (i)) so it cannot reach another principal's lane — **and rev 34 drops the I6 half of this bound: the mis-designated grant supplies `expectedEmail` itself, so I6 constrains its own mistyping and not which account the lane ends up holding (§2b, §4)**. It is recovered by a re-tick — **which, as of rev 34, also cancels the session that grant already has in flight (§2d rule (3)); before that the re-tick left the wrong device free to complete the login it had already started** — and it is **not prevented** (§2l, §4) |
| Revoking **one** of a person's several grants | The grant is revoked and its sockets drop; **the lane survives**, because another grant of the principal remains bound. Only revoking the principal's **last** grant wipes the lane (§2f). **If the revoked grant held the designation, the designation is cleared with it** (§2a rule (iii)): the lane keeps working — it stays `loaded`, its terminals keep launching, and any bound grant can still switch — but **no new login can be started** until someone re-ticks. That is strictly better than rev 31's version of this row, where clearing the designation left the lane unable to receive a credential at all. **And rev 34 says who re-ticks on a headless box, because rev 33's §2d(iv) read as an exemption from this row:** the host-inline `orca lane login` does **not** bypass the missing designation — it is refused the same `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` — so the operator's path back is `orca lane designate` and then `orca lane login`, both at the host's own shell and both audited (§2a, §2d(iv)) |
| The person's desktop disconnects while their phone stays connected | **The lane is not wiped, and after rev 32 it is not wiped when the phone disconnects either.** The disconnect wipe is deleted (§2f); the credential stays until an explicit logout, a last-grant revoke or a deprovision. The wipe predicate's principal-scoped survivor query survives for the revoke arm, which is the one arm that still uses it |
| **Orca restarts, or the host reboots, with a lane loaded** | **Nothing is wiped.** The startup wipe is deleted (§2f); startup sweeps `*.tmp` artifacts, **reconciles each lane's account store against its account index (rev 34)**, seeds the live-PTY gate and binds the listener. An **indexed** login survives that reconciliation untouched — that is the point of the index — and only a directory no completed login ever recorded is deleted. **Rev 35: that judgement is made against the lane's own `<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json`**, so a lost or corrupt Orca state file cannot make every login on the box unindexed at once, and an index this pass cannot read **quarantines** that lane's directories instead of deleting them (§2a, §2f). The lane comes back signed in, which is the point of the whole re-basing — and it is also the residual §7 question 12 asks the owner to ratify |
| A lane whose grant is revoked upstream (password reset, session revoke on claude.ai) | `laneState: 'reauth-required'`; live terminals fail `invalid_grant` on their next refresh; new launches fail closed. The remedy is **one lane login**, not a re-push and not a re-pair. This row replaces the R-DR2 row entirely: it is the person's own deliberate act on their own account, it is visible, and no other grant caused it |
| ~~Second Orca **install** holding the same account~~ | **Deleted in rev 32 — R-DR2 has no members.** There are no two copies of one grant anywhere in the model, so a laptop, a reinstall, an old build or a second desktop of the same principal rotates its **own** chain and cannot touch the lane's. §4's largest accepted residual, the `foreign-rotation` receipt, `laneState: 'reauth-required'`-as-foreign-rotation and the desktop's operating-rule text all go with it |
| ~~A plain `claude` in a non-Orca terminal on the lease-holding desktop~~ | **Deleted in rev 32.** This row was rule (iv)'s subject: the desktop's own runtime `~/.claude` held a third copy of the lane's credential, so a plain `claude` there rotated the lane's token. There is no third copy. `runtime-credential-lane-clearing.ts` and the local `selectClaude` refusal are deleted, and §7 question 9 — "would you rather rule (iv) were a warning?" — is answered by removing its subject |
| **`accounts.selectClaude` naming an account a lane is signed into** | **Succeeds.** Two grants, two chains, nothing to race. Through rev 31 this was refused `accounts.lane.account_resident_elsewhere` for every caller class including the local shell and the renderer, and that refusal plus rule (iv)'s clearing is the 2026-08-27 incident. This is a **Rule 3 meaning change in the permissive direction** — an old client that expected a refusal now gets a success and needs no change |
| **An authorization URL shown on a device that did not request it** | Cannot happen through the supported path: the URL is returned only on the requesting grant's own `loginStart` reply and is never published on the lane status stream (§2d(ii), §3 row 2). What the design does **not** close is a person who is socially engineered into completing a labelled prompt that names someone else's lane — §4's named residual, mitigated but not removed by the labelled confirmation and by I6 |
| **A co-tenant during a lane's login window** | The lane's `claude` binds a **loopback listener** for the login's duration **[code, observed on 2.1.247 — `ss -lntp` showed `LISTEN 127.0.0.1:<ephemeral> users:(("claude",…))` on three separate runs]**. The co-tenant cannot inject a usable code (PKCE: a code minted against their challenge fails the victim's verifier), but can reach the callback with `?error=` to abort the login, and can detect that a login is in progress. **A DoS and a metadata channel, not a takeover** — inside §4's shell-equivalence envelope, but new, so it is named rather than absorbed (§4) |
| **A lane and the desktop signed into the same account** | Supported and expected — it is the configuration the owner asked for. They share **one rate-limit quota**, because limits are per account and not per grant, so the two usage readings must be de-duplicated by `accountUuid` and rendered as one account seen twice, never summed (§2k). They do **not** share a refresh chain, which is the entire point |
| A lane usage probe in flight when the lane is logged out or deprovisioned | The wipe **kills the probe first and sweeps second**, unchanged from S9c: mark wipe-pending, abort through `fetchViaPty`'s `options.signal` → `settleAborted` → `cleanupHiddenRateLimitPty(…, { kill: true })`, drop the result, sweep, re-read, clear the mark (§2f, §2k) **[code]**. While that runs the row publishes `laneState: 'absent'` **and** `laneWipePending: true`: launches key on the first and fail closed, and nothing claims the credential is gone until the re-read is clean. Only the **trigger set** changed — disconnect and startup no longer fire it |
| **A lane login session in flight when the lane is logged out, revoked or deprovisioned** | **New in rev 33 — the fence above, applied to the one writer rev 32 added.** A login child holds the lane's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and writes a complete grant into it at capture time, so it is a post-sweep writer exactly as a probe is. The wipe **marks wipe-pending, cancels every in-flight login session in that lane** (kill the process group, sweep the half-written `<laneAccountId>/auth`, emit `login-failed` with the refusal's own sentence), **confirms the child dead on the same bounded window, sweeps, re-reads, clears the mark**; every later `loginSubmitCode` for that id is `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown`. **Start-side:** a `loginStart` into a lane whose wipe is pending — from a grant **or** from the host-inline CLI (§2d(iv)) — is refused `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress`. Without both arms a logout racing a login started moments earlier sweeps clean and the CLI writes a full grant back afterwards, with `laneState: 'absent'` and §2a consequence 2's fail-closed promise both false (§2f, §5). **Rev 34 adds the outcome for the window itself:** a login child that cannot be confirmed dead publishes `laneState: 'absent'` **and** `laneWipePending: true` and retries, reusing `accounts.lane.probe_not_confirmed_dead` with a generalized sentence, exactly as the probe arm does — `accounts.lane.logout_incomplete` is for a sweep that did not finish, which is a different failure. **Rev 35 adds the queue arm, which is the half rev 34's fence was missing:** the wipe is the third member of the lane's write queue and sets its mark **before** entering, so a capture step — or a `selectAccount` — already queued behind it re-checks wipe-pending **and** session-liveness when its turn comes and is refused `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress`, writing nothing; and `cancel` invalidates a session in any state before `captured`, so a child that has exited with its capture tail still running is reached too (§2c, §2d rule (2), §2f) |
| **A lane login session that outlives the host process** | **New in rev 34 — the fence above holds only while Orca is alive, and round 2's blocker is what lives on the other side of that.** A `kill -9`, a crash or a power loss between the CLI's capture write and Orca's own bookkeeping leaves `<lane>/claude-accounts/<laneAccountId>/auth/` holding a complete, independently usable grant at `0600` that no session owns; on POSIX the child is `detached` (`claude-accounts/service.ts:1109`) **[code]** and can still be alive and still writing. **Three arms.** The ordinary quit path **cancels every session before exit**. The marker is written **before** the child is spawned, so the directory is always resolvable and therefore always purgeable, and the purge is a directory walk rather than a marker-gated list loop (§2a, §2c). And **startup reconciles each lane's store against that lane's own index — `<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json`, in the lane as of rev 35** — deleting every unindexed directory when the index parsed, **quarantining** them when it did not, re-reading, and — if one reappears — publishing that lane `absent` **and** `laneWipePending: true` rather than reporting a clean store (§2f). **No pid is persisted and no surviving child is killed by pid**: pid reuse makes that an unsafe kill, and §2d rule (1) keeps session state out of storage. **Accepted cost:** a crash in the window between capture and the index write destroys a usable grant on the next start — the fail-closed direction, one browser round-trip, and it does **not** revoke that grant upstream (R-34a, §4, §7 question 19) |
| A lane pane that is a **plain shell** | Unchanged: it carries the lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from spawn (§2a), so typing `claude` later resolves the lane; auth env vars are stripped and an auth-env-defining launch is refused |
| A lane owner's `terminal.split` | Unchanged: inherits the parent pane's lane; splitting someone else's pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` |
| A lane owner's agent-session / worker / worktree-create spawn | Unchanged: derives the caller's lane at the funnel; a spawner that cannot resolve one throws rather than landing in the shared lane |
| Daemon-hosted Claude surviving an Orca restart | **Simpler than rev 31's row.** Startup no longer wipes, so the surviving session's credential is still on disk and still its own; startup order is sweep-tmp → **reconcile the account store** → seed → listen (§2f, rev 34). The `push_stale` check the old row turned on is deleted with the watermark |
| Corrupt/unreadable device registry at startup | Unchanged: reconciliation is skipped entirely (§2a) — no lane directory is deleted |
| **A lane's account index is missing or unparseable at startup** | **New in rev 35.** The store reconciliation cannot judge that lane's directories, so it **quarantines** them — each `<laneAccountId>` renamed `<laneAccountId>.quarantined-<ts>` — logs the lane, the count and the reason, and deletes nothing (§2f arm B). The lane's **active** credential is untouched, because it lives in `<lane>/.credentials.json` and not in the store: the lane comes back `loaded` and keeps working, while `listLaneAccounts` reads **empty** — never a directory walk — until a fresh login writes a new index (§2c rule (1)). Per lane and never host-wide, which is the whole point of moving the index into the lane (§2a rule (2)): one bad file costs one lane its switch list, not every lane its logins. A quarantined directory is a complete grant still at rest under a name nothing resolves, in class (3)'s scope for that lane's next logout / last-grant revoke / deprovision, and named as a residual under **R-34a** (§4, §7 q19) |
| WSL target from a lane-holding grant | Unchanged (§2i/§2n): refused `terminal.lane_wsl_unsupported` until S9e; no lane path crosses WSLENV |
| A lane launch on the **Windows** host whose shell resolves to `wsl.exe` | Unchanged: refused `terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported` in the provider's lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm (§2a, §2m(5)) |
| A client supplying `claude_config_dir` (any casing) on the **Windows** host | Unchanged: stripped or refused; `win32` comparisons are case-insensitive and the collapse is post-merge (§2m(5)) |
| The **Orca process itself** carrying a differently-cased `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` on **Windows** | Unchanged, and now with a fourth site to check: the lane **login** spawn builds env the same way `runClaudeCommand` does — `{ ...process.env, CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR: configDir.windowsPath }` by exact key (`claude-accounts/service.ts:1080-1085`, `:1091-1097`) **[code]** — so an inherited `Claude_Config_Dir` survives beside the canonical one and Win32 resolves the first in block order. A login could then land in the **wrong config dir, writing a real grant outside the lane**. S9-L1 makes it the **fourth** collapse site, beside the provider's, the daemon's and the probe's (§2m(5)) |
| A lane on the **Windows** host, usage bars | Unchanged: statusline-derived only until the §9 Windows probe clears `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` (§2k Fact 2) |
| A **native** lane directory whose DACL could not be verified at creation | Unchanged: provisioning **fails closed** (§2m(1)) |
| A write to a lane file while a live `claude` in that lane holds it open (Windows) | Unchanged in mechanism, changed in subject: the write is now an **in-lane switch** or a **login capture** rather than a push, and it still publishes through `renameFileWithWindowsRetry` (6 attempts, ~750 ms) and still refuses by name when the window is exhausted — the code is renamed with the verb (`accounts.lane.switch_write_locked`), never an untyped `EBUSY` (§2m(2)) |
| **A migrated lane that was loaded by a push before S9-L3** | **New in rev 32.** Its `.credentials.json` is a copy of a desktop credential Orca can no longer reason about, so the lane is marked **`unverified-legacy`**: it keeps running, it is never silently trusted as a lane-owned grant, no rotation is attempted against it, and the UI asks for one lane login to replace it. It is not wiped on sight, because that would break a working box to make a bookkeeping point (§6's S9-L3). **And it is the last R-DR2 member, on a clock — rev 33 states what rev 32 left implicit.** That lane holds a byte copy of the *desktop's* grant, and the same slice deletes the delegation lease and rule (iv)'s local clearing — the two mechanisms that suppressed the delegating desktop's own rotator against exactly that account and kept it out of that desktop's runtime file (§2e). With both gone the desktop's managed refresh re-arms and rotates the single-use refresh token the legacy copy still carries, so the legacy lane is expected to fail `invalid_grant` at the **desktop's next rotation** — plausibly hours after S9-L3 lands, not "whenever the owner gets round to it". "It keeps running" is a statement about the upgrade instant, not about the week after it: `unverified-legacy` is a **bridge**, and the landing-order obligation is to log that lane in before or immediately after S9-L3 (§4, §6's S9-L3) |
| Any old client that trips a **new** refusal | Unchanged rule: every new code carries a **complete human-readable sentence** naming cause and remedy, and none depends on the client mapping the code. **Minted in rev 32:** `accounts.lane.login_not_designated`, `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated`, `accounts.lane.login_already_in_flight`, `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown`, `accounts.lane.login_session_expired`, `accounts.lane.login_code_rejected`, `accounts.lane.login_identity_mismatch`, `accounts.lane.login_url_unparsed`, `accounts.lane.login_cli_unsupported`, `accounts.lane.account_unknown`, `accounts.lane.logout_incomplete`, `accounts.lane.switch_write_locked`. **Minted in rev 34:** `accounts.lane.switch_in_progress` (the switch gate's contention, named instead of a bare `Error` — §2c, row 9) and `accounts.lane.login_store_full` (the eight-login cap on the lane's own store — §2c). **Retired in rev 32** (`shared/claude-lane-refusals.ts:7-65`, the array literal, whose **57** entries at `:8-64` are the authority **[code]**; **Fifteen minted against sixteen retired is a net shrink of one, to 56** — rev 32 said "shrinks by fifteen", which matched neither list; rev 33 re-counted twelve-against-sixteen to 53 against the file, and rev 34's two additions carry it to 55. `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress` (`:44`) and `accounts.lane.probe_not_confirmed_dead` (`:43`) are **already in that array** and are neither minted nor retired **[code]**): `push_stale`, `push_malformed`, `push_not_delegated`, `push_write_failed`, `push_write_locked`, `no_pusher_designated`, `account_resident_elsewhere`, `residency_unverifiable`, `delegated_elsewhere`, `local_clear_locked`, `delegable_account_unknown`, `delegable_list_invalid`, `desktop_unavailable`, `switch_timed_out`, `switch_lane_cleared`, `clear_incomplete`. Every other code in that file is unchanged. Rule 3 |
| A grant that sets host-wide `settings.agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` | Unchanged: ignored for every lane launch (§2 rows 13–14) |
| A lane holder's `terminal.create` with `focus`/`rendererBacked`, `session.tabs.createTerminal`, or `session.tabs.activate` | Unchanged (§2a(i), §2a(ii), rows 29 and 35) — none of these is about credential provenance |
| An OpenClaude launch in a lane | Unchanged: refused `terminal.lane_agent_unsupported`; a hand-typed session downgrades the row to `credentialLane: 'shared-runtime'` and its usage is dropped (§2a) |
| A **federated worktree create** whose link is bound / not bound | Unchanged (§2a, §7 q8): bound creates in the link's principal's lane; unbound is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound` |
| Grant whose lane is logged out while its terminals live | Terminals keep running on the CLI's in-memory token and fail on the next refresh; presence shows `laneState: 'absent'`; new launches fail closed. Unchanged except that reaching this state is now always someone's deliberate act |
| A grant paired through the coalesced regenerate-QR link | Unchanged: binding is refused (no `pendingExpiresAt`, §2a) |
| SSH pane whose client supplies its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | Unchanged: preserved verbatim; no host lane is ever pinned to a `connectionId` pane |
| A lane spawn on the **non-daemon** provider | Unchanged: carries the agent-hook env even while a peer holds `agentStatusHooksEnabled: false` (§2 rows 16, 30) |

## 4. Threat model

**What the design fixes.** Remote account hijack of a *lane* via `accounts.selectClaude` and deletion via `removeClaude` (today: any paired client moves or deletes everyone's credential, `accounts.ts:118-127`, `:147-151`) **[code]**; silent cross-switching between the two developers; misattribution of work — and of usage (§2k) — to the wrong account, **including on plain-shell panes**; the process-global switch gate stalling the other developer's spawns; the `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` injection in §2a — override *and* deletion, on all three spawning RPCs, and today it reaches the real managed-account store; **cross-grant launch redirection through host-wide settings**, which §2's closure principle removes by computing lane launches instead of customizing them; the `--config-dir` capture path in §2d, symlinks included; the unscoped account-snapshot disclosure in `list`/`subscribe`; the two client-reachable spawn edges rev 7's anchor missed; the `agentStatusHooksEnabled` strip on the non-daemon provider; a lane holder's `session.tabs.createTerminal` landing in the shared `~/.claude`; and a client-hinted `tabId`/`leafId` adopting a pane bound to another grant's lane.

**Rev 32 adds three fixes that are larger than anything on that list, and they are structural rather than another guard.** **(1) Revocation becomes lane-scoped.** Through rev 31 a lane credential *was* the desktop's credential, one grant materialized in two places, so revoking a compromised lane meant revoking the account — logging out the desktop, the phone, every laptop and every other lane — and the only containment on offer was a wipe, which removes a copy without invalidating anything an attacker already exfiltrated. Under per-lane login the lane holds its **own** grant: revoking it kills the lane and touches nothing else, and exfiltrated lane bytes go dead at that moment instead of staying live until an account-wide reset. **(2) R-DR2 is retired as a class** — §4's largest accepted residual through rev 31, unfixable host-side and detection-only, together with the operating rule it forced into the UI (§2e) — **with one bounded, dated exception rev 33 states rather than absorbing:** the migration. The lane loaded today by a push keeps a byte copy of the desktop's grant while it is marked `unverified-legacy`, and S9-L3 deletes the lease and rule (iv) that suppressed the desktop's rotator against precisely that copy. For the length of the cutover R-DR2 has **one** member, on the one box that matters, and it ends the way R-DR2 always ended — `invalid_grant` at the desktop's next rotation (§3's migration row, §6's S9-L3). **(3) The 2026-08-27 incident's two mechanisms are deleted rather than tuned**: `service.ts:472`/`:475`'s refusals and `runtime-credential-lane-clearing.ts`'s local clearing were pure consequences of blob-copying and have no analogue under per-lane login (§2d, §2e).

**And one authority hole that is not a confidentiality concession.** Without §2a's ownership predicate, `orchestration.workerStart` (`orchestration-workers.ts:29`, pane from `params.from` at `:31`) **[code]** — on **both** its topology paths — and `terminal.split` (`terminal.ts:1276`) **[code]** would let grant B *spend* grant A's Claude account through Orca's own supported RPCs. That is categorically different from the concession below: not "B can read bytes B could read anyway", but Orca granting B the use of A's subscription. Unchanged by rev 32.

**The new attack class rev 32 creates, and it is the exact inverse of the one a relay invites you to defend.** PKCE means the **code** is safe to relay: the `code_verifier` never leaves the lane's `claude` process, so an intercepted code cannot be redeemed and cannot be replayed, and codes are single-use besides. **The dangerous half is the URL.** An attacker — a co-tenant, or any grant that can reach a login-start RPC — begins a login in *their own* lane and obtains `URL_attacker`, carrying the attacker's `code_challenge`. If Orca's relay can be made to display `URL_attacker` to the victim — a mis-routed frame, an unlabelled "complete your sign-in" prompt, a race between two concurrent starts, or plain social engineering across a shared box — the victim authenticates with **their** Claude account, `platform.claude.com` shows them a code bound to the attacker's challenge, and they paste it into whatever UI showed them the URL, which routes it to the attacker's lane. **The attacker's lane then holds an independent, durable grant on the victim's account, surviving every wipe rule in this document.** Under the push model this had no analogue: a lane could only ever receive bytes the desktop already held. **Four bindings close it, none of them about the code — rev 33 re-counts, because rev 32's fifth does not hold. Three are §2d's bindings (i), (ii) and (iii)+(iv); the fourth is a property of the prompt §6's S9-L2 renders and is stated there and here, not in §2d, which specifies the RPCs and not the UI:** the login-start RPC derives its lane from `principalOf(ctx.pairedDeviceId)` with no lane field in any of the four schemas; the URL is delivered only on the requesting grant's own connection, never on the status stream, and `loginStatus` never re-serves it (§2d(ii), §3 row 1); at most one login is in flight per lane and a second start is refused rather than queued — **across every entry point, the host-inline `orca lane login` included** (§2d(iv)), which is what stops "two live URLs for one lane" from being reachable through the runbook's own mandatory path; and the human-facing prompt **names the principal, the lane and the expected account**, so a substituted URL has to survive a labelled confirmation. **I6 was rev 32's fifth binding, and rev 33 withdraws it from this list.** `expectedEmail` is a parameter the *starting caller* supplies, and the host holds no allowlist to check it against — R5 keeps the account inventory on the desktop and §2b deletes the delegable list — so in the attack above the attacker simply declares the **victim's** email: the victim authenticates as that account, `auth status --json` in the attacker's lane reports that account, the comparison **passes**, and the lane loads. I6 refuses the honest caller's mistake (a wrong account, a wrong device of the right person, §2l) and contributes nothing against a caller who chooses the expectation. **The residual after the four, stated and accepted:** the labelled confirmation naming principal, lane and expected account is the **only** control left on this path, so the residual is a person socially engineered into confirming a prompt that names someone else's lane. That is not closable host-side, and it is the one place where "the human at the machine is the authority" (§2a) is also the weakest link.

**What it does not fix, and this is the load-bearing concession — unchanged.** A paired grant is **shell-equivalent** on this host. `terminal.create` has no `clientKind` gate and its params include `command` (`terminal.ts:1007`), `env` (`:1009`) and `launchConfig.agentCommand`/`agentArgs`/`agentEnv` (`:1013-1015`), all passed straight through **[code]**; Orca's own authorization precedent says so outright (`orchestration-remote-run-mailbox.ts:11-22`) **[code]**. Grant B can call `terminal.create` with `command: cat <userData>/claude-lanes/<A>/.credentials.json` and read the output over a stream it is authorized to read. **So: lanes defend against accidental cross-switching and the `selectClaude`-class hijack; they do not defend against a hostile paired grant.**

**On the Windows Server host this whole paragraph transposes, and §2m(6) does the transposition** — one Windows account, one `orca serve`, both developers' grants served by it; `/proc/<pid>/environ` becomes the process environment block, `ptrace` becomes `ReadProcessMemory`, the Unix socket becomes a named pipe whose mode-bit protection is **replaced** by obscurity plus a default DACL (`runtime/runtime-rpc.ts:1853-1870`, endpoint `:1864`, comment `:1863`) **[code]**, and reading a resident lane file at rest is *worse* than on Linux because the lane's own DACL grants FullControl to `S-1-5-32-544` (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:71`, `:85-95`) **[code]**. The alternative — two serves under two Windows users — is stated and rejected in §2m(6). Same for Linux: both developers run as the **same Unix user**, so `0600` protects nothing from a peer who *is* that user, and a shell profile (`$PROFILE`, `~/.bashrc`) can rewrite `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` after every guard in this design (§2m(8)).

**Residuals accepted and named. Rev 32 removes four and adds four, and the two lists are worth reading against each other.** **Removed:** **R-DR2** in full, with its second-desktop, laptop, reinstall and old-build members and the operating rule it put in the UI — **except across the migration window, where the `unverified-legacy` lane is its one remaining member until that lane is logged in, and where the desktop's re-armed rotator is expected to end it (§3's migration row, §6's S9-L3)**; **rule (iv)'s third copy** on the delegating desktop, together with the clearing action and the local refusal that made it visible; the **bounded-residency-window** framing, replaced below by its opposite; and the **host holding the owner's ticked account list** (§8 item 5, §7's host-pivot question 10), which un-ships with the delegable list. **Added:** **(R-32a) the at-rest window is unbounded** — from login until an explicit logout, a last-grant revoke or a deprovision, where it used to end at close, idle timeout or the next Orca start. On the Windows host that means "as long as the lane is signed in", inside a DACL that includes local Administrators by construction. **This is the one axis on which the new model is weaker than push**, it is the direct price of the owner's "no token synchronization" requirement, and §7 question 12 asks for ratification rather than absorbing it. The mitigating argument, stated as an argument and not as a closure: what sits at rest is now an **independently revocable** grant rather than a copy of the desktop's own credential, so the same exfiltration is *recoverable* in a way it was not before. **(R-32b) the authorization-URL substitution class above**, with its socially-engineered residual. **(R-32c) the loopback listener.** The lane's `claude` holds an open `127.0.0.1` listener for the login window **[code, observed on 2.1.247]**; the co-tenant cannot inject a valid code (PKCE) but can abort the login with `?error=` and can detect that one is in progress and against which lane. A DoS and a metadata channel, inside the shell-equivalence envelope, but **new**, so it is named rather than absorbed. **(R-32d) `org:create_api_key` is in the lane's scope set.** The authorization request asks for `scope=org:create_api_key user:profile user:inference user:sessions:claude_code user:mcp_servers user:file_upload` **[code, observed verbatim on 2.1.247]**, so a lane credential can mint an Anthropic API key — a *separate* credential with its own lifetime that revoking the OAuth grant does not necessarily invalidate. **Not a regression** (the same scope rode the pushed blob), but per-lane login multiplies the number of independently-held grants that can do it, and it makes "revoke the lane" feel more complete than it is. The design states that **lane revocation is not proof no API key was minted**, and the incident runbook gains a Console API-key check (§6's S9-L4). The CLI offers no scope flag, so a narrower set is not obtainable today (§7 question 16). **(R-32e) two grants on one account share one quota** (§2k) — a UX residual, not a security one, but it reads as a bug the first time it bites. **Rev 34 adds one more, and it is the residual left over from closing round 2's blocker. (R-34a) a login that outlives the host process, and the pass that reaps it.** §2f's fence holds only while Orca is alive; after a hard kill a `detached` login child (`claude-accounts/service.ts:1109`) **[code]** can leave a complete grant in a directory no session owns. The startup account-store reconciliation deletes every unindexed directory (§2f), which is fail-closed and is the right direction — with two named edges. It **destroys a usable grant** when the crash landed between the capture write and the index write, costing one browser round-trip; and destroying it locally **does not revoke it upstream** — that grant stays valid at its source until it expires or the person revokes it, so “the box is clean” is a claim about the box and not about the account. Same caveat as R-32d, for the same reason, and the incident runbook says so. **Rev 35 re-gates that pass and adds the third edge.** The index it judges against now lives **in the lane** (`<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json`, §2a rule (2)) rather than in Orca's persisted state, so a lost or corrupt state file can no longer make every login in every lane unindexed at once — a bad index costs one lane, not the box. And when a lane's index is missing or unparseable the pass **quarantines** instead of deleting (§2f arm B), which inverts the trade for that one input: **a quarantined directory is a complete grant left at rest**, under a name nothing resolves, until an operator removes it or the lane's next logout / last-grant revoke / deprovision sweeps it (class (3), §2f). It is **logged and not published** — no wire field carries it — so the operator's signal is that log line plus `ls <lane>/claude-accounts`; and because `MAX_LANE_LOGINS` counts index rows, a quarantined store can carry more than eight grants at rest (§2f). The inversion is deliberate: a parse error is evidence about the index, not about the directories, and destroying N working logins on one is the larger harm. The owner is asked to ratify the trade at §7 question 19 rather than having it absorbed.

**Residuals carried forward unchanged:** OpenClaude cannot be lane-isolated (§2a, Q10 = block); enterprise **managed policy** outranks a lane's own settings and is invisible to §2j's refusal; the **base-URL** env class lets a lane owner redirect their own pane's traffic; Codex selection stays host-wide and unscoped (`accounts.ts:128-140`) **[code]**; a grant with **no** lane can still move the shared lane under other lane-less peers (§2d — Q5, and rev 32 *widens* this to every account by deleting the lane-residency carve-out); WSL panes isolate per account, not per person (§2i, §2n); lane usage is statusline-only on Windows until `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` is explained; a lane forks Claude's session history (Q9, intended); an anonymous local caller can inherit any pane's lane (§2a case (ii)); a **principal** is a larger blast radius than a grant (Q1); on Windows a local administrator is inside every lane's ACL by construction (§2m(6)); and the shell-rc class (§2m(8)). **One residual is re-graded rather than removed:** §2a called a mis-designation *inert*. It is not, after rev 32 — a wrongly-ticked grant of the right person can complete a login — and it is re-stated as **bounded to that principal's own devices and recovered by a re-tick** (§2l).

If the owner wanted R1 to hold against a hostile grant, the achievable control would be **lane-strict mode** — and **Q6 is answered: OFF, it is not built.** So **R1 is an authority guarantee and not a confidentiality one**, stated here once more because it is the single most important limit in this document.

**Version pin, and rev 32 makes it a slice gate rather than a note.** The design has always rested on one behavior — the running CLI re-reads relocated credentials — and Orca already pins its OAuth constants against a specific binary: "verified against the installed `claude` binary (**2.1.177**)" (`oauth-refresh.ts:4-8`) **[code]**. **The installed binary on the VPS is 2.1.247 [code, checked 2026-08-27], so the pin has already drifted by seventy patch releases**, and rev 32 adds a *second*, weaker contract on top of it: the login flow's observable shape — the printed URL and its `platform.claude.com` redirect, the OSC-8 wrapping, the literal `Paste code here if prompted > ` prompt with no trailing newline, the `Invalid code…` re-prompt, the `--email` flag — is **scraped, not documented**. Every string in the login-session parser is a version dependency. **Decision: re-verifying the OAuth constants and the login parser against the pinned build is a gate on S9-L1, not a follow-up** (§6); the tested range is recorded, `claude --version` is probed at lane provisioning and at login-start, a version outside the range refuses `accounts.lane.login_cli_unsupported`, and a parse failure surfaces as a named lane state rather than a hang. **If the §5 live probe of the re-read fails, S9 still does not die; R2 degrades** exactly as before: lanes still isolate, and a switch takes effect for **new** terminals in that lane only, with `laneState: 'restart-required'` and the `AccountsPane.tsx:802` hedge becoming accurate instead of unconditional.

## 5. Test plan

Vitest under `config/vitest.config.ts`. Each new guard gets a negative control; each authorization check gets a mutation proof (delete the check → a named test must fail).

- **S9a lanes + launch** — `principal-credential-lane.test.ts`: symlink, escape, foreign-marker and wrong-segment rejections; a 36-character non-UUID lane id refused **at creation**; lane `settings.json` carries the managed hook block plus exactly the §2a mirror allowlist — asserted item by item: `permissions.allow/deny/ask/additionalDirectories` and `model`/`outputStyle` present, `permissions.defaultMode`, user `hooks`, `env`, `apiKeyHelper` and `awsAuthRefresh`/`awsCredentialExport` **absent** (mutation proof: mirroring the host's `env` or `apiKeyHelper` turns a named test red); a fresh lane's `.claude.json` carries the onboarding allowlist and the mirrored `mcpServers` **minus** any entry whose `env` names `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or an auth var, and **no** project-trust map and **no** project `history`; the lane's mirrored `agents/`, `commands/`, `skills/` and `output-styles/` are present item by item (**mutation proof:** dropping any one turns a named test red); a lane's transcripts are its own and pre-S9 resume paths are refused (§2a/§2g); an `accounts.lane.loginStart` from a grant bound to no principal, and one against an **unprovisioned** principal, are each refused and **spawn nothing** (**mutation proof:** auto-provisioning on login-start turns it red — this is rev 22's push fixture re-pointed in rev 33 at the writer that replaced the push, §2a); provisioning a device entry with **no `pendingExpiresAt`** is refused while a minted row provisions (**setup, corrected in rev 19:** the provisioning row needs a **designation** and nothing else — a bound grant the human has ticked, connected or not — so the `pendingExpiresAt` discriminator is reached on its own terms rather than being shadowed by an eligibility refusal that would have made the arm pass for the wrong reason, §2a; **mutation proof:** dropping the discriminator turns it red); startup reconciliation deletes an orphan lane dir, and **a registry whose `load` threw deletes nothing** (**mutation proof:** removing the `loadSucceeded` gate turns it red and wipes every lane); a planted `.credentials.json.<pid>.<uuid>.tmp` is removed by both the wipe and the next lane open (**mutation proof:** a filename-scoped wipe turns it red). `runtime-auth-service.test.ts` (FULL sweep, 3932 lines): the lane branch returns the lane `envPatch`, `stripAuthEnv: true`, `managedRefreshDeferredByLivePty`, and a `provenance` that is `lane:`-prefixed, contains no `pairedDeviceId`, and fails `isManagedClaudeAuth`; a WSL target from a lane-holding grant returns the WSL branch; host branch unchanged. `ipc/pty.test.ts` (FULL sweep): two creates on two grants get two different `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` values; an anonymous create gets none; an SSH pane (`connectionId` set) gets **no** lane path **and keeps its own client-supplied `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` verbatim** — the guard 3(a)/3(b) split (**mutation proofs:** deleting the `connectionId` condition from the lane resolver turns the first red; making guard 3 delete client config dirs unconditionally turns the second red); a spawn env taking the WSL branch never contains a lane path (`local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`, `pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`); a split of a lane-owned pane inherits that lane. **Command-independence — the §2a decoupling, its own block:** a lane-pinned `terminal.create` with **no `command`**, a worktree **default tab**, a `session.tabs.createTerminal` plain shell, and a **non-Claude `startupAgent`** each carry the lane `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, are registered in the lane's live-PTY set, and refuse an auth-env-defining `env` (**mutation proof:** re-gating the lane branch on `isClaudeLaunchCommand` (`pty.ts:1924-1931`) must turn *all four* red). **Closure principle — host-wide settings must not shape a lane launch:** with `settings.update({ agentDefaultArgs: { claude: '--settings /tmp/b.json' }, agentDefaultEnv: { claude: { ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: 'x' } } })` applied by a *second* grant, a lane launch on the first grant reaches the PTY (path A, `pty.ts:5019`/`:5066`) with neither the flag nor the env — asserted for a bare `command: 'claude'` in a repo-backed workspace **and** for a `startupAgent` create, the two branches of `resolveAgentTerminalCreateOptions` (`:26096` and `:26109`). **Mutation proofs:** computing the lane launch *before* `resolveAgentTerminalCreateOptions` (rev 6's placement) or at `ptyController.spawn` in `orca-runtime.ts` (rev 7's) must turn both red — the second because the `agentTeamsPlan` re-derivation sits between that anchor and the spawn. A shared-lane launch with the same settings still receives them. **Split and renderer edges:** the same computation covers `splitPtyBackedTerminal` and `pty:spawn` because it lives in `pty.ts` (**mutation proof:** moving it back into `createTerminal` turns the split `env`-vector test *and* the renderer-backed create test red). **Funnel coverage — one test per spawning RPC, all asserting the caller's lane and not the shared one:** `terminal.create`, `terminal.createAgentSession`/`ensureAgentSession` (proving the new `pairedDeviceId` is read, not the collapsed `clientId`, **and** that the ensure branch's direct `provider.spawn` at `pty.ts:5019` gets the lane), `session.tabs.createTerminal` (both with and without an authoritative window), `worktree.create` (default-tab, startup and setup panes), `orchestration.workerStart` for `worktree: 'current'` (`:65`) **and for `'new-child'`/`'new-top-level'` (`:145` → `createManagedWorktree`, whose worker terminal and setup pane must land in the coordinator's lane, not shared — mutation proof: dropping `credentialLane` from `createManagedWorktree`'s args turns it red)**, federated worker start, the coordinator loop, and pane recovery. **Mutation proof for the funnel:** making `credentialLane` optional with a `'shared'` default must turn *every* one red; and a host-internal caller passing neither a lane nor explicit `shared` must throw. **Ownership predicate:** grant B calling `orchestration.workerStart` with `params.from` naming grant A's coordinator pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` on both topology paths and creates no worker terminal, worktree or setup pane; B splitting A's pane is refused, **and so is B reaching the same split through `agentTeams.tmuxCompat`** (mutation proof: placing the predicate in the `terminal.split` handler instead of `splitPtyBackedTerminal` turns the tmuxCompat test red while the split test stays green); the same `workerStart` from an anonymous local socket still inherits; **a federated worktree create whose link *is* bound creates in the link's principal's lane, and one whose link is unbound is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound` and creates no worktree, no terminal and no setup pane** — rev 15's §7-question-8 answer, asserted on both arms (**mutation proofs:** falling through to `{ kind: 'shared' }` turns the unbound arm red; binding `{ kind: 'grant' }` as the lane **value** turns the bound arm red, because the `credentialLane` union has no grant case — rev 18's restatement, since the link **row** is grant-keyed *by design* (`{ homePeerFingerprint, boundDeviceId, boundAt }`, the create reading `principalOf(boundDeviceId)`, §2a/§6/§7 q8), so the red arm is the lane value and never the row's key). **And the binding's *key* rule, its own three assertions — rev 16's blocker fix, which is the fail-open direction and therefore needs its own fixtures:** a bind attempt on the fingerprint of the runtime's **shared** `authToken` (`runtime-rpc.ts:507`) is **refused as unbindable**, and a `federationAttachStart` arriving over the local socket therefore still gets `terminal.lane_link_unbound` (**mutation proof:** accepting any fingerprint the consent surface is handed turns it red, in a fixture where developer B's local `orca` call creates a worktree, a startup terminal and a setup pane in developer A's lane — the fail-open twin of the refusal); the same for the `sha256('authenticated_transport')` fallback value (`orchestration-mutation-executor.ts:132`); a fingerprint matching **zero** or **two** registry rows' `token` is not offered and not bindable, while one matching exactly one row binds and records `boundDeviceId` (**mutation proof:** dropping the one-row requirement turns the zero-match case red); and a **re-paired** grant's link — its `token` rotated at `device-registry.ts:97` — no longer matches its stored row and its create is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound` rather than served on the stale binding (**mutation proof:** storing only the fingerprint with no `boundDeviceId`/token check leaves the stale binding live and turns it red); a restored pane with no lane id refuses both split and recovery. **Mutation proof:** deleting the predicate must turn the workerStart and split tests red. **Env/arg injection — four vectors × three spawning RPCs.** `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `launchConfig.agentEnv.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `envToDelete: ['CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR']` and a settings-redirecting `agentArgs`, each through `terminal.create`, `session.tabs.createTerminal` and `worktree.create`, plus the `env` vector via `terminal.split`. The `envToDelete` case asserts the *resulting spawn env* on **both** paths, after `:4839` and after `:6498`. With `computeLaneLaunch` deleted, guard 3(b) must still refuse all of them — **including in the zero-lane state**. Containment: `ompResumeFilePath` and `resumeProviderSession.transcriptPath` pointing into another lane, or into the shared `~/.claude`, are refused on all three RPCs. **Post-anchor invariant — the rev-9 anchor, its own block:** a lane spawn's *provider* env (the object `LocalPtyProvider` hands the process, after `buildSpawnEnv` at `local-pty-provider.ts:712-722`, after the `envToDelete` replay at `:725-726` and after `promoteAgentTeamsShimPath` at `:851`) carries exactly the lane's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and none of `CLAUDE_AUTH_ENV_VARS` — asserted on the object itself, not on the host env (**mutation proofs:** letting `spawnOptions.envToDelete` keep `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` past `computeLaneLaunch` turns it red, because `:725-726` then deletes the lane; anchoring `computeLaneLaunch` at the assembly points instead of `spawnForStablePane` turns the `buildSpawnEnv` hook assertion red); and the agent-session-ensure spawn (`pty.ts:5019`) is covered too — a lane `terminal.createAgentSession` reaching `provider.spawn` **directly** must carry the lane env (**mutation proof:** applying the anchor only inside `spawnForStablePane` turns this one red, and *only* this one). **Pane-record binding — its own block:** a lane `terminal.create` with `focus: true` and with `rendererBacked: true` is served on the background branch and its pane carries the lane (**mutation proof:** honoring either flag for a lane create, i.e. letting it reach `terminal:requestTabCreate`, turns it red); a lane create with no workspace selector is refused rather than served unbound; a `pty:spawn` on a lane pane whose `args` carry `env.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, a settings-redirecting `launchConfig.agentArgs` **and a forged lane-shaped field** spawns with the pane's own lane and none of the three (**mutation proof:** reading the lane from `args` instead of the pane record turns it red); a client-hinted `tabId`/`leafId` naming a pane bound to another lane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` **for every caller class** — asserted once for a lane caller and once for a lane-**less** `{ kind: 'shared' }` caller, and asserted again with the target pane's PTY **dead**, where the respawn into that paneKey must run on the pane's persisted lane for its owner and be refused for anyone else, with the binding row unchanged (**mutation proof:** rewriting a bound row to `shared` on a refused adopt, or gating the check on the caller holding a lane, turns these red); a lane `session.tabs.createTerminal` **with an authoritative window present** is served by `createRuntimeOwnedMobileSessionTerminal`, its pane is host-minted and lane-bound, and no `terminal:requestTabCreate` is dispatched (**mutation proof:** restoring the window-presence branch for a lane caller — or letting the renderer-minted `identity` through at `orca-runtime.ts:27545` — turns it red), while the same call from a lane-less caller still takes the renderer branch; **Activate/materialize — its own controls, because the drop must *not* reach this edge:** a lane holder taps a **pending** tab of their own lane pane and `session.tabs.activate` materializes it — the pane is reattached (or respawned into the same paneKey when its PTY is dead) under the *same* `tabId`/`leafId`, the tab reaches `status: 'ready'`, and the worktree gains **no** second tab (**mutation proof:** dropping `identity` at `orca-runtime.ts:8181` the way the create call sites drop it turns this named test red on both assertions — the tapped tab stays non-ready and a second, host-minted tab appears); grant B tapping a pending tab of grant A's lane-bound pane is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned` and spawns nothing (**mutation proof:** removing the `:26704-26711` predicate turns this cross-lane materialize test red — it is the *only* gate on this edge); a lane holder tapping an **unbound** pending tab is refused `terminal.lane_pane_unbound` and no PTY is created on the shared config dir (**mutation proof:** falling through to `shared` turns it red); and a lane-**less** caller's materialize behaves exactly as today, identity intact; the lane survives a runtime restart on the persisted binding row — asserted across **all four** `persistPtyBinding` writers, including the two reattach writers (`pty.ts:820`, `ssh-relay-session.ts:2558`), whose rewrite must preserve the lane (**mutation proof:** re-emitting a row without the lane on reattach turns it red) — and a restored pre-lane pane refuses split, recovery and resume; `terminal.recoverPane` on a relay-lease pane stays `credentialLane: 'remote'` and never adopts a lane. **Wake and resume:** grant B's `worktree.activate` over grant A's slept lane agent leaves it asleep (`wake_refused_not_owned`) and creates no pane; A's own activate resumes it in A's lane, with B's `settings.update({ agentDefaultArgs, agentDefaultEnv })` and `agentCmdOverrides` absent from the spawn (**mutation proof:** waking a lane record through the renderer builder turns both red); a cold-restore respawn of a lane pane keeps the lane and drops the renderer's settings-derived args. **Agent Teams:** a lane launch whose `agentTeamsPlan` rebuilds `command`/`env`/`envToDelete` still spawns with the lane env intact (**mutation proof:** anchoring `computeLaneLaunch` before the plan, i.e. rev 7's placement, turns it red). **Hooks:** with a peer's `settings.update({ agentStatusHooksEnabled: false })` applied, a lane spawn still carries the agent-hook env (`pty.ts:1770`) while a shared-lane spawn does not — asserted **twice, once per provider mode**: on the daemon path (`pty.ts:4730`/`:6455`) and on the LocalPtyProvider path, where the read is `:2478` inside `buildSpawnEnv` (**mutation proofs:** a host-wide read for lane panes turns both red; dropping the lane from the `buildSpawnEnv` ctx turns *only* the local-mode one red — the round-8 MAJOR A regression). Same shape for `networkProxySettings` (row 28/30): a lane pane's proxy settings come from the host, and the assertion is that nothing lane-scoped depends on them. **OpenClaude:** a lane `startupAgent`/command naming OpenClaude is refused `terminal.lane_agent_unsupported`, and a hook-observed OpenClaude session in a lane pane flips its row to `shared-runtime` and drops its usage instead of attributing it. `keychain.test.ts` (macOS reader, runs on every platform over a faked backend): a lane read/write resolves the **scoped** service only (**mutation proof:** restoring the `[scoped, unsuffixed]` walk of `:108-113` on the lane branch turns it red). `claude-accounts/service.test.ts`: `addClaudeFromConfigDir`/`addCodexFromHome` refuse a path under `claude-lanes`, **including from an anonymous local caller** and **including a symlink whose realpath lands inside** (mutation proof: a `resolve`-only `startsWith` passes the symlink and turns it red).
- **S9-L1 login sessions — new in rev 32, and the block with the most mutation proofs, because every one of its guards is the only thing standing between a relay and a durable grant on someone else's account (§2d, §4).** `lane-login-session.test.ts`: **Authorization.** `loginStart` from a grant bound to no principal is refused and spawns nothing; from a **bound but undesignated** grant it is refused `accounts.lane.login_not_designated`; from a principal with **no designation** it is refused `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated`; from the designated grant it succeeds (**mutation proof:** accepting a lane or principal id out of the params, or deriving the lane from anything but `principalOf(ctx.pairedDeviceId)`, turns a named test red in a fixture where developer B starts a login that lands in developer A's lane). **URL delivery.** The `authorizeUrl` appears **only** in the reply to the starting grant's own `loginStart` and in **no** frame on the lane status stream — asserted by draining a second grant's `statusSubscribe` for the whole session and requiring the URL never to appear in it (**mutation proof:** publishing the URL on the `login-started` frame, which is the obvious implementation, turns it red). **Printed URL only.** The handler asserts the relayed URL's `redirect_uri` host is `platform.claude.com`; a fixture feeding it a stdout stream that also carries a `http://localhost:<port>/callback` variant must relay the hosted one (**mutation proof:** relaying the localhost URL turns it red — the two differ in one query parameter and the harm is a code delivered to an arbitrary loopback port on the person's own desktop, §2b). **Parsing.** An OSC-8-wrapped URL is extracted once, from the **first** match on the live stream and not from `runClaudeCommand`'s tail-truncated 4000-char buffer (`service.ts:1133-1134`) **[code]** (**mutation proofs:** "last URL wins" turns a red test on the doubled OSC-8 fixture; reading from the accumulated buffer turns a second red on a fixture that emits >4000 characters after the URL); an unparseable stream refuses `accounts.lane.login_url_unparsed` rather than hanging. **PKCE, same process.** `loginSubmitCode` for a session id the caller did not start is refused `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown` **before** anything is written to any stdin — asserted cross-grant *and* cross-session within one grant; a submit after the minting process has exited is refused `accounts.lane.login_session_expired`; and a host restart between `loginStart` and `loginSubmitCode` invalidates the session rather than resuming it (**mutation proof:** persisting the session across a restart, or keying the submit on the lane instead of on the session, turns these red). **Concurrency.** A second `loginStart` for a lane with one in flight is refused `accounts.lane.login_already_in_flight` and does not spawn a second `claude` (**mutation proof:** queueing instead of refusing turns it red, in a fixture where two URLs are live for one lane and the human cannot tell which prompt belongs to which). **Identity verification (I6).** With `expectedEmail: a@x`, a login whose `auth status --json` reports `b@y` is refused `accounts.lane.login_identity_mismatch`, the login directory is **swept**, and `laneState` never reaches `loaded` (**mutation proof:** verifying after the transition, or trusting `oauth-account.json` without the `auth status` cross-check, turns it red); the matching case loads. **Bounding.** A session whose code is rejected re-prompts at most N times and then refuses; a session with no submission is killed at `LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS` and its process group is torn down (**mutation proof:** removing the cap turns a named test red in a fixture where a co-tenant holds a lane in "logging in" indefinitely). **Half-initialized lane.** An aborted login leaves `.claude.json` and `backups/` in the lane's login dir **[code, observed on 2.1.247]** and no credential; `laneState` distinguishes *configured, no grant* from `loaded`, and the abort path sweeps those artifacts (**mutation proof:** reporting `loaded` on a config-only directory turns it red). **Secrets.** The `code` never appears in any log line, any published row or any PTY stream — asserted by scanning the whole captured log and the whole status stream for the fixture's code value. **Version pin.** `claude --version` outside the recorded tested range refuses `accounts.lane.login_cli_unsupported` at `loginStart` (**mutation proof:** removing the probe turns it red, §4). **The wipe fence — new in rev 33, and the arm the rev-32 draft was missing (§2f).** An `accounts.lane.logout` arriving while a login session is in flight **cancels it before the sweep**: the child's process group is killed, the half-written `<laneAccountId>/auth` is swept, every later `loginSubmitCode` for that id is refused `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown`, and the post-sweep re-read finds the lane empty (**mutation proof:** sweeping without cancelling turns it red on a fixture that scripts the login's capture write to land *after* the sweep pass — the login-session twin of S9c's probe fixture, and it must be a **separate** test from the start-side one below). **Rev 35 scripts the ordering that mutation proof does not reach, because a queue-ordered capture passes it while still writing afterwards.** A second fixture drives a login to the point where the **child has exited** and the capture tail is pending, has the capture step **enter the lane's write queue first**, and only then issues `accounts.lane.logout`; the assertions are that the wipe sets its wipe-pending mark **before** entering the queue, that the capture's turn ends in a refusal — `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress`, with **no** write to `<lane>/.credentials.json`, **no** `oauthAccount` merge and **no** index row — and that the post-sweep re-read finds the store empty with `laneState` still `absent` (**mutation proofs:** checking wipe-pending *before* acquiring the queue instead of after — the shipped `lane-wire-authority.ts:112`-vs-`:120-122` shape **[code]** — turns it red with a complete grant written after the sweep; scoping `cancel` to sessions whose child is still running turns it red on the same fixture, because that session is past its child's exit and short of `captured`; and the **same** fixture run with the queue order reversed, logout first and capture second, must produce the **identical** refusal, which is what proves the fence is precedence and not ordering). **Start-side, asserted separately:** a `loginStart` into a lane whose wipe is pending is refused `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress` and spawns nothing (**mutation proof:** dropping the precondition leaves the cancel test green while the very next call re-loads the lane that was just wiped — the same shape §2k's close-wipe bullet names for the probe). The last-grant revoke and the deprovision arms assert the same two things. **Two entry points, one session — new in rev 33 (§2d(iv)).** The host-inline `orca lane login` and a designated grant's `loginStart` take the **same** per-lane single-in-flight lock: with a CLI-started session live a grant's `loginStart` is refused `accounts.lane.login_already_in_flight`, and with a grant-started session live the CLI's start is refused the same way — asserted in **both** directions (**mutation proof:** scoping the lock to the RPC surface leaves the CLI arm green while two `claude` children, two verifiers and two authorization URLs race one `<lane>/claude-accounts` root and one automatic switch, which is the §9 runbook's own mandatory path on a headless box). **Session lifecycle, three arms — new in rev 33 (§2d).** A `loginSubmitCode` after a simulated Orca restart is refused `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown` because the session map is never persisted; a submit after the child has exited unexpectedly is refused the same code rather than surfacing an untyped `EPIPE` (**mutation proof:** writing to the held stdin without an exit-reaped session check turns it red with a raw throw, the class §3's Rule-3 row forbids); and clearing the designation, or revoking the grant that owns the session, cancels it and kills the process group (**mutation proof:** leaving the session live across a revoke turns it red). **Rev 34's arms, one per finding round 2 returned.** *(Restart reaping — the blocker.)* A fixture drives a login to the point where the CLI has written a complete credential into `<laneAccountId>/auth`, kills the host process without running any shutdown path, restarts it, and asserts the directory is **gone** after startup and that `listLaneAccounts` never showed it (**mutation proofs:** writing the `.orca-managed-claude-auth` marker *after* the capture instead of before the spawn turns it red, because the purge can no longer resolve the directory; skipping the account-store reconciliation at startup turns it red with a complete grant still at `0600`; gating that reconciliation on the registry's `loadSucceeded` turns it red on a corrupt-registry boot). **Two arms rev 35 adds to that same fixture, one per half of the re-gating.** *(The index is the lane's own.)* Between the kill and the restart, Orca's **persisted state** is wiped to its defaults — the `Persistence.load` → backup-chain → `getDefaultPersistedState(homedir())` path (`persistence.ts:3160`, `:3866-3868`, `:3888`) **[code]** — and every **indexed** login in every lane is asserted **still present** after startup (**mutation proof:** holding the index in `principal-lane-store.ts` / `persistence.ts` rather than in `<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json` turns it red with every login on the box deleted at once, which is round 3's blocker in a single assertion). *(An index that cannot be read quarantines.)* One lane's `index.json` is truncated to invalid JSON and the assertions are that that lane's account directories are **renamed** `<laneAccountId>.quarantined-<ts>` and **not** removed, that a **second** lane with a good index reconciles normally in the same pass, that `listLaneAccounts` on the damaged lane returns **empty** while its active `.credentials.json` still loads, and that a later `accounts.lane.logout` removes the quarantined directories and the index file too (**mutation proofs:** deleting on a parse failure turns the first red; letting one lane's bad index abort or skip the whole pass turns the second red; falling back to a directory walk when the index will not parse turns the third red with an unvouched-for login offered to a human; leaving quarantined names out of the purge's directory walk turns the last red with a complete grant at rest after a logout). A second arm plants an unindexed directory whose contents are **re-created after the sweep** — the surviving-child case — and asserts the lane publishes `absent` **and** `laneWipePending: true` rather than reporting a clean store. *(The designation precondition, both entry points.)* With the principal's designation cleared, **both** a grant's `loginStart` and the host-inline `orca lane login` are refused `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` (**mutation proof:** exempting the inline path from (i-a) leaves it green while §2a, §3's revoke row and this arm all say the opposite). *(`--email` required.)* An inline start with no `--email` is refused before a session is minted (**mutation proof:** making it optional turns it red on the fixture that then authenticates as a different account and is not caught). *(Designation move.)* Moving the designation from grant A to grant B while A holds a live session **cancels A's session** and kills its process group, and A's later `loginSubmitCode` is refused `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown` (**mutation proof:** treating only a *clear* as the canceller leaves A free to complete, which is the state §2a's “recovered by a re-tick” promises cannot exist). *(`loginStatus` authority.)* A second bound grant of the same principal, holding the `loginSessionId` off its own `login-started` frame, is refused `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown` on `loginStatus` (**mutation proof:** answering from the map without the owning-grant comparison turns it red). *(Submit authority across surfaces.)* No grant may `loginSubmitCode` into a CLI-started session, asserted for the **designated** grant specifically. *(Three members, one queue — rev 35.)* An `accounts.lane.selectAccount` issued while a login capture holds the lane **waits and then applies**, in queue order, and neither path ever surfaces a bare `Error` (**mutation proofs:** dropping `serializeLaneWrite` from either writer turns it red with the untyped throw; the defensive arm asserts `accounts.lane.switch_in_progress` is what a caller sees if the gate is ever found closed). The **third** member is §2f's wipe, and it outranks both: a `selectAccount` queued behind a logout is refused `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress` on its turn and rewrites nothing, asserted in **both** queue orders (**mutation proof:** re-checking the wipe mark before acquiring the queue instead of after it — `lane-wire-authority.ts:112` vs `:120-122` **[code]** — turns it red with a credential re-materialized into a lane that has just been swept); the capture-tail arm of the same rule is the fence fixture above. *(Store cap.)* A ninth `loginStart` on a lane holding eight logins is refused `accounts.lane.login_store_full` and spawns nothing. *(I6 unverifiable.)* A login whose `auth status --json` yields **no identity at all** is refused and swept and never reaches `loaded` (**mutation proof:** falling back to `oauth-account.json` when `auth status` is silent turns it red — the same mutation the mismatch arm forbids, on the other input).
- **S9-L1 fence precedence (rev 36)** — `lane-login-session-wipe-race.test.ts`: a login whose child has exited into a successful `auth status --json` is admitted to the lane write queue **after** the wipe's continuation has already cleared the wipe-pending mark; the capture must refuse `accounts.lane.login_cancelled` on session state alone, with no `.credentials.json` write and no index row (**mutation proof:** having the re-check read the mark instead of the session state turns it red); the reversed order (capture admitted first) must end with the sweep removing what it wrote and the post-sweep re-read clean.
- **S9-L1 per-lane store** — `principal-lane-account-store.test.ts`: the lane store enforces the same rules as `managed-auth-path.ts` re-rooted at the lane — symlink refusal ordered first, canonical containment, the exact `<laneAccountId>/auth` two-segment match, the marker file whose contents must equal the id, and `0600` atomic writes (`managed-auth-path.ts:16-66`, `:68-93`) **[code]** — asserted item by item against the lane root (**mutation proof:** reverting any one of them to a bare `startsWith` on a non-native realpath turns a named test red, and on `win32` an 8.3 short name defeats it). **Two lanes, two accounts:** two lanes are provisioned, each logged into a **different** account, and each `claude auth status --json` reports its own; deleting one lane's `.credentials.json` leaves the other unaffected and makes the first report **no** account (**mutation proof:** sharing one store root between lanes turns it red — this is the promoted form of rev 31's §5 step (10)/(13), which the design already called "the single highest-severity failure mode" and which is now the **primary acceptance test of the model** rather than a Windows gate on it). **Store purge (rev 33, §2f):** `purgeLaneAccountStore` removes every `<laneAccountId>/auth` directory under the lane — **and, rev 35, the lane's `index.json` with its `.tmp` siblings and every `<laneAccountId>.quarantined-<ts>` directory** (**mutation proofs:** keeping the index turns a named test red on a post-logout `listLaneAccounts` that still names deleted grants; skipping quarantined names turns a second red with a complete grant at `0600` after a logout reported done) — and leaves the lane's non-secret content — `settings.json`, the mirrored user content, the transcripts — untouched (**mutation proof:** purging the lane root turns it red on the transcript assertion, and purging only the *active* account's directory turns it red on a three-login fixture). **In-lane switch:** selecting a second login rewrites the lane's `.credentials.json` and merges its `oauthAccount` under `beginClaudeAuthSwitch(laneId)`, a spawn in that lane during the switch is refused and a spawn in another lane is not, and a second switch back to the first login **also applies** rather than no-opping (**mutation proof:** the no-op is the shape rev 5's watermark bug took, and it must turn a named test red even though there is no watermark left to be stale).
- **S9b scoping + attribution, rewritten for rev 32 — the push half of this bullet is deleted with the push** — real-socket integration following `src/main/runtime/multi-client-navigation-isolation.integration.test.ts` (two E2EE-paired clients over real `ws`) **[code]**: A's lane login writes only A's lane, and B's lane and the shared lane are untouched; B cannot target A's lane (no param); B's `selectClaude` is refused **only when B holds a lane**, and an unprovisioned B's still works (mutation proof: "any lane exists" turns it red); `accounts.list` **and both `subscribe` emit points** (`accounts.ts:194-196`, `:221`) to B never contain A's email or usage; an anonymous local socket keeps host-wide selection. **Mutation proof:** replacing `ctx.pairedDeviceId` with a params-derived id turns a named test red. **The deletion arms, asserted positively rather than by the absence of a test — this is how the incident is prevented from returning.** `selectClaude(X)` **succeeds** for every caller class — B, an unprovisioned grant, the renderer and the anonymous local socket — while X is signed into A's lane, and A's lane keeps working across it: no clearing of any runtime credential file, no refusal, and A's next launch still resolves A's lane (**mutation proofs:** restoring `assertManagedClaudeAccountNotLaneResident` at `claude-accounts/service.ts:472`, or `assertClaudeAccountNotDelegatedToLane` at `:475` **[code]**, turns *this* test red — the suite is written so that re-introducing the incident is a failing test rather than a silent regression). `removeClaude` of a managed desktop account succeeds while a lane is signed into the same account, and the lane is unaffected afterward. **Peer-visible label (Q3), re-pointed:** grant B reading a terminal row of principal A's lane sees A's principal label **and** the **owner-set** label of §2b, and does **not** see the email, the account id or `laneUsage` (**mutation proof:** sourcing that label from the lane's `oauth-account.json` `emailAddress` turns it red — the label a peer reads is the one the local human wrote, never one a file asserted). **Deleted from this bullet with their subjects:** every push-validation arm (oversized/extra/malformed members, `displayName` bounds, `push_not_delegated`), every watermark arm (the two-consecutive-switches regression, stale-sha, older-issued-at, `cli-observed` receipt, replay refusal), every residency arm (`account_resident_elsewhere`, the sha-keyed host row, the unmanaged `~/.claude` collision), and every lease arm (the `delegatedGrantId`-suppression assertions and the `foreign-rotation` detection path). **Kept from this bullet, unchanged:** the statusline attribution arms — a post carrying lane A's configDir lands on lane A's row and not the shared bar, an unknown configDir is still dropped (`rate-limits/claude-usage-attribution.ts:105-125`) **[code]**, and a published `usageMetadata.authProvenance` for a lane contains no `pairedDeviceId`.
- **S9c lifecycle + gate, with rev 32's trigger set** — the wipe **fence** is asserted exactly as S9c shipped it and the **triggers** are asserted to have shrunk, which needs both a positive and a negative arm. *Negative arms, new in rev 32:* the principal's last connection closes and the lane's `.credentials.json` is **still there** afterward, with `laneState` still `loaded` and the next launch still resolving the lane (**mutation proof:** restoring the `'last-connection-close'` arm of `LaneWipeReason` (`principal-lane-lifecycle.ts:24`) **[code]** turns it red); the runtime restarts with a lane loaded and the credential **survives**, while any planted `.credentials.json.<pid>.<uuid>.tmp` and `${target}.<pid>.<ms>.<hex>.tmp` sibling is **swept** (**mutation proofs:** restoring the `'startup'` arm turns the first red; scoping the startup sweep to a filename instead of the pattern turns the second red, §2f), **and — new in rev 34 — every *indexed* login in `<lane>/claude-accounts` is still present after that restart while a planted *unindexed* directory beside them is gone** (**mutation proof:** reconciling on the directory listing instead of the index deletes a live login and turns it red; skipping the reconciliation leaves the unindexed one at rest and turns its twin red, §2f); **and rev 35 adds the negative arm:** the same restart with that lane's `index.json` unreadable leaves **every** directory in place under a `.quarantined-<ts>` name and deletes nothing (**mutation proof:** deleting against an index that did not parse turns it red, with the three live logins gone). *Positive arms, kept:* `accounts.lane.logout` wipes and refuses `accounts.lane.logout_incomplete` rather than reporting a sweep that did not finish — **and rev 33 adds the sweep's *scope* to what is asserted, because the shipped fixture certified only the active file:** a lane holding **three** logins is logged out and the assertion is that `<lane>/claude-accounts` holds **no** `auth` directory afterwards, not merely that `.credentials.json` is gone (**mutation proof:** the shipped top-level-only sweep — `listLaneCredentialArtifacts` / `sweepOneLanePass`, `principal-lane-credential-sweep.ts:48-58`, `:109-117` **[code]** — passes the old assertion and turns this one red, with two complete, independently usable grants still at `0600` in the store); the same scope assertion runs for deprovision and for the last-grant revoke; **and rev 34 extends that scope in two directions.** The logout fixture plants an **unindexed** `<laneAccountId>` directory beside the three indexed ones — a crashed login's leftovers, no index row and a **missing marker** — and asserts the purge removes it too (**mutation proof:** looping the purge over `listLaneAccounts`, or gating deletion on `resolveOwnedClaudeManagedAuthPath`, leaves that directory at rest and turns it red); and the S9-L1 restart fixture above asserts the same store holds no unindexed directory after a `kill -9` and a start, which is the arm no logout test reaches at all; **revoking a principal's *last* grant removes the directory while revoking one of two grants bound to that principal removes neither** — the site reads `socket.device.deviceId` (`runtime-rpc.ts:1384`) **[code]**, never `deviceToken`, and captures `principalOf(deviceId)` **before** `removeDevice` drops the row (**mutation proof:** keying the revoke wipe on the grant, or hooking it beside `forgetGrant` after `removeDevice`, turns the two-grant case red); a deprovision removes it whatever the grant count. *The fence, unchanged from S9c and re-asserted because its subject changed:* with a probe running in lane A, an `accounts.lane.logout` marks the lane wipe-pending **before** aborting, kills the probe's `claude` through `fetchViaPty`'s `options.signal` → `settleAborted` → `cleanupHiddenRateLimitPty(term, termDisposables, { kill: true })` **[code]**, drops the result, sweeps, **re-reads**, and only then reports done; a probe requested while that wipe is pending is refused rather than started; and a wipe whose probe cannot be confirmed dead publishes `laneState: 'absent'` **and** `laneWipePending: true` and retries (**mutation proofs:** sweeping without killing turns it red on a scripted post-sweep write; clearing `laneWipePending` at the mark instead of at the clean read-back turns a second red). The lane gate: a spawn in lane A during an **in-lane switch** in lane A is refused — asserted once for a `claude` command and once for a **plain shell** — and a spawn in lane B is not (**mutation proof:** dropping `begin`/`endClaudeAuthSwitch(laneId)` from the switch handler turns it red). A launch into an unloaded lane fails closed, including a plain-shell lane pane. `live-pty-gate.test.ts` (FULL sweep) unchanged.
- **Principal binding + the phone (S9-L1 and S9-L2 arms)** — `principal-binding.test.ts`: a lane is provisioned for a **principal**, and two grants bound to it resolve the *same* lane directory (mutation proof: keying the lane by `pairedDeviceId` turns it red); a device row with **no `pendingExpiresAt`** cannot be bound at all (mutation proof: dropping the discriminator turns it red); the `--pair-name` string is **not** consulted anywhere in the binding path (mutation proof: resolving a principal by matching `DeviceEntry.name` turns a named test red — two rows both named `Ana` must resolve to two principals). **Adopt/materialize across one person's devices:** the phone materializes a pending tab the desktop minted and it succeeds;
