the other principal's is refused `terminal.lane_not_owned`; an unbound pane is refused `terminal.lane_pane_unbound`. **Wipe predicate (revoke arm only, after rev 32):** revoking one of two grants of one principal leaves the lane, revoking the last removes it. **Bind, unbind, audit (§2a):** binding onto an already-bound row is refused, re-binding is unbind-then-bind, and all five consent writes append an audit row. **Designation, re-pointed to its new meaning (§2d(i)) — every rev-19 fixture in this bullet survives with its subject changed from "may push" to "may start a login".** The surface still lists **every** grant bound to the principal and no grant bound to another, still filters on nothing, and still succeeds for a grant whose handshake carries only the two-element list every client sends today (`shared/remote-runtime-client.ts:140`) **[code]** (**mutation proof:** filtering on `scope`, on `ctx.clientCapabilities` or on any `clientKind` value turns it red — the host makes no device-kind judgement); the identical tick still succeeds at provisioning time, an hour later, and after a restart with no reconnection (**mutation proof:** confining it to mint or to first connect turns all three red); it still **persists** across restart, close and disconnect (**mutation proof:** a process-lifetime in-memory set turns it red); unbinding or revoking the designated grant still **clears** the designation and writes its own audit row. **What changes, and it is the fixture that has to be rewritten rather than re-pointed:** rev 19's mis-designation arms asserted a designated phone leaves the lane at `laneState: 'absent'` with **no credential byte written**. **After rev 32 that assertion is false and asserting it would encode a guarantee the host cannot make.** The replacement asserts what actually holds: a designated **phone** can run `loginStart` and `loginSubmitCode` and the lane **loads** (**mutation proof:** refusing a phone *at designation* turns it red — the control is the human tick plus re-designation, §2a); an **undesignated** bound grant is refused `accounts.lane.login_not_designated` and spawns nothing; and a grant of **another principal** cannot start a login for this lane at all, asserted in the fixture where developer B's `loginStart` must not land in developer A's lane (**mutation proof:** the §4 substitution fixture, and it is the highest-severity mutation in this suite). **Phone switching (§2l):** with the caller's principal holding a lane, the mobile module calls `accounts.lane.selectAccount` and **never** `accounts.selectClaude`; with no lane it stays `accounts.selectClaude` (mutation proof: routing unconditionally either way turns one arm red); the switch **completes synchronously** with no `pending` state and no dependence on any other grant being connected (**mutation proof:** re-introducing the liveness precondition turns it red — asserted against the real `mobile/src/accounts/` module under `mobile`'s own Vitest suite, beside `mobile-accounts-route.test.ts` **[code]**); and refusals render the **host's** `message` verbatim with no client-side code table. **Deleted from this bullet with their subjects:** every `requestSwitch` arm (`pending`, the `switch-requested` frame, `desktop_unavailable` with and without a frame, `switch_timed_out`, the pooled-shared-control liveness precondition and its CLI-grant arm), every delegable-list arm, and every lease arm including rule (iv)'s runtime-file clearing. **Pane binding is a principal id:** unchanged — a pane created by grant G of principal P still resolves P's lane after G is revoked, and unbind re-points no live pane (§2a rule (iii)).
- **Per-lane usage pull (Q7)** — `lane-usage-pull.test.ts`, unchanged except where the watermark was its subject: a preparation is built per **loaded** lane and `resolveOAuthCredentialReadOptions` receives that lane's `configDir` (`claude-fetcher.ts:236-248`) **[code]**; a lane with no credential loaded is skipped, not probed. **The interactions, now three rather than four (§2k):** a probe running in lane A registers a **synthetic, non-persisting** gate id through `markEphemeralClaudePtySpawned`/`markEphemeralClaudePtyExited` (`live-pty-gate.ts:136`, `:142`) **[code]** and releases it on every exit path, and the assertion it now defends is that a probe in lane A **defers an in-lane switch in lane A** and not another lane's (mutation proofs: not registering the probe turns it red; registering through the **persisting** arm turns a second red, asserting the synthetic id never reaches `persistence.addClaudeLivePtySessionId`); a probe that starts while `isClaudeAuthSwitchInProgress(A)` is **skipped and not queued** (mutation proof: queueing it turns a named test red by posting usage for the pre-switch account); and the two start-side preconditions of the wipe fence hold (no probe into a lane whose wipe is pending or whose `laneState` is `absent`). **New in rev 32:** the fetcher's own refresh arm does **not** fire for a lane preparation — with a lane credential whose `expiresAt` is in the past, `refreshClaudeOauthCredentials` is **not called** and the lane's `.credentials.json` is byte-identical afterward (**mutation proof:** restoring the delegation-keyed gate at `claude-fetcher.ts:1216` **[code]**, which is false for every lane once the lease is deleted, turns it red — Orca rotating a lane's chain is exactly the double rotation the whole model exists to remove, §2e). **Deleted with the watermark:** the post-probe `syncLane` arm, its idle-lane fixture and the trigger-(2)-exemption mutation proof (§2k). **Kept unchanged:** the stale-probe kill on a mid-flight lane write, asserted on the process and not only on the result; the `paneKey → lane → account` row join and its Windows-cased fixture; the probe's pane-identity scrub (`shared/pane-identity-env.ts:15`) **[code]**; `laneUsage` **omitted** and not zeroed for a peer; and the `win32` arm asserting the pull is disabled (`claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064`).
- **Windows-only controls** (`describe.runIf(process.platform === 'win32')`, skipped elsewhere and never faked green) — **DACL:** a freshly provisioned lane directory reads back protected, with exactly the three allowed SIDs at FullControl and no deny ACE; a simulated `ensureWindowsUserDataAclGrant` (`icacls <userData>\* /grant:r …(OI)(CI)(F)`) and a simulated `grantDirAcl` on the lane root leave it protected (mutation proof: creating the lane through the async best-effort path, `secure-file.ts:59-73`, turns both red); `restrictWindowsPathSync` returning false makes provisioning **fail closed** (mutation proof: ignoring the return value turns it red); **and the rule is scoped to a native lane — an S9e lane root matched by `parseWslUncPath` (`shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`) skips the DACL step entirely and fails closed on the in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` read-back instead**. **Which half of that runs in which slice — rev 16, round 4's fourth major, because gate (v) below makes these controls a merge gate and S9e is deferred:** the **skip** is asserted in **S9a** (feed the provisioner a `wsl.localhost` candidate and assert `restrictWindowsPathSync` is never called and provisioning does not fail on a DACL), while the **read-back** half **activates with S9e** and is skipped — not faked green — until that slice lands, because the in-distro `chmod`/read-back lives in `claude-accounts/principal-credential-lane-wsl.ts`, which §6 assigns to S9e. Gate (v) therefore binds S9a to the skip arm only (**mutation proof:** rev 13's unconditional rule turns the S9a skip fixture red, because `restrictWindowsPathSync` returns `false` on a path it cannot set a DACL on (`secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]** and every S9e lane is then refused — the twin of the UNC-refusal conflict rev 13 fixed on the other rule and not this one). **Write:** an **in-lane switch** — and, identically, a **login capture** — publishes through `renameFileWithWindowsRetry` and succeeds against a held-open target inside the retry window, while a bare `renameSync` throws (proving the combined writer of §2m(2) is load-bearing); a target held open **past** the window is refused `accounts.lane.switch_write_locked` with the lane file byte-identical (rev 33: rev 32 renamed the code in §3's matrix and left this fixture naming `push_write_locked` and a watermark that no longer exists) (**mutation proof:** letting the rethrow at `fs-utils.ts:220` escape untyped turns it red — the client gets a raw `EBUSY` with no sentence, the class §3's Rule-3 row forbids); the wipe removes **both** temp shapes — `${target}.${pid}.${uuid}.tmp` and `${target}.${pid}.${ms}.${hex}.tmp` — and the second temp left by the EPERM retry path. **Containment:** an 8.3 short-name form, a drive-letter case flip, a `\\?\` extended form and a planted **junction** into a lane are each refused, and the symlink check is asserted to run **before** the directory check (mutation proof: reverting any check to `realpathSync` + case-sensitive compare turns a named test red) — **including `resolveOwnedClaudeManagedAuthPath` (`managed-auth-path.ts:36-42`) **[code]**, which §2m(4) now decides adopts the same module rather than being named and left alone**; a **remote** UNC lane root is refused at provisioning, while a `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…` root is **not** refused by the Windows-side check — that half is **S9a**, and it is what gate (v) binds S9a to — and is instead **routed** to §2n's in-distro canonicalization, whose fixture **activates with S9e** and is skipped until then, since the canonicalizer is S9e code (**mutation proof:** an unscoped UNC refusal — rev 12's rule — turns the **S9a** recognise-and-do-not-refuse fixture red, which is how the two rules were found unable to ship together). **Env case:** a lane create supplying `claude_config_dir` / `Claude_Config_Dir` in `env`, in `envToDelete`, and in `launchConfig.agentEnv` is stripped or refused (mutation proof: exact-case comparison turns all three red); **and the collapse is asserted on the env the *provider* hands the process, not on `spawnOptions.env` — round 3's third blocker:** with the **host process env** carrying `Claude_Config_Dir` (row 19, `runtime-paths.ts:14-25`) **[code]** and the lane patch carrying `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the object the child receives carries **exactly one** casing and its value is the lane's (**mutation proofs:** collapsing at `computeLaneLaunch` instead — rev 13's site — turns it red, because `...mergeGitConfigEnvProtocol(stripInheritedBuildModeEnv(process.env), args.env)` (`local-pty-provider.ts:675-676` → `git-credential-prompt-env.ts:11`) **[code]** reinstates the inherited spelling *after* the anchor and Win32 resolves the **first** case-insensitive key in block order (`pty/windows-path-segment-merge.ts:18`) **[code]**, so the pane silently runs on `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`; and omitting the collapse from the **local** provider while adding it to the daemon turns a second red, since only the daemon has the `collapseWindowsPathEnvKeys` precedent today, `daemon/pty-subprocess.ts:163-190` called at `:833`); **and the same fix on the one call site that never passes through `computeLaneLaunch`, in *both* halves — rev 15 asserted only the first, which is round 4's second major.** (a) A lane usage probe launched with a lowercase `anthropic_api_key` in the host process env must have it stripped by `applyClaudeEnvPatch` — the strip loop at `environment.ts:36`, inside the `stripAuthEnv` block `:35-39` — before `rate-limits/claude-pty.ts:247-251` spawns, asserted on the `spawnEnv` object itself (mutation proof: reverting that loop to exact-case turns it red, and the probe's usage is then attributable to the wrong account, §2m(5)). (b) **The lane-key collapse is asserted on the probe's `spawnEnv` too:** with the host process env carrying `Claude_Config_Dir` (row 19) and the lane's `authPreparation` supplying `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, the object handed to `pty.spawn` (`claude-pty.ts:303-311`) carries **exactly one** casing and its value is the lane's (**mutation proof:** omitting the collapse from this call site while keeping it in the provider and the daemon turns it red — `applyClaudeEnvPatch` writes by exact key at `environment.ts:44-46` **[code]** over a plain spread, so both keys survive and Win32 resolves the inherited `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`; the fixture then asserts the **two** downstream writes §2k still names — the paneKey join and the synthetic lane-scoped gate id — land on the lane's row while the probe holds the shared credential; the third, the post-probe `syncLane` watermark move, is deleted with the watermark, §2c). **WSL classifier:** a lane pane whose shell resolves to `wsl.exe` is refused in the lane-aware `buildSpawnEnv` arm — asserted on the provider path *and* on the daemon twin, **and, rev 16, on both routes to that resolution: once from the host `terminalWindowsShell` setting (path A), once from a per-tab `pty:spawn` `shellOverride: 'wsl.exe'` (path B, `ipc/pty.ts:5923` → `:6074`/`:6167` → `local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:41`) **[code]**, which is row 36 and the edge every restored lane pane re-enters through, **and — rev 17, row 37 — once from `args.projectRuntime` of `kind: 'wsl'` (`ipc/pty.ts:6076`/`:6169` → `local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:52-57`) **[code]**, the arm that resolves to `wsl.exe` *regardless of* the per-tab override and therefore outranks the input row 36 enumerates** (**mutation proof:** asserting the refusal over `shellOverride` alone leaves the `projectRuntime` fixture green while the pane runs in WSL — the same shape as the path-A-only hole one clause up)** (**mutation proof:** asserting the refusal only over the setting leaves the path-B fixture green while the pane runs in WSL) — and **no** lane path reaches `addWslEnvKeys` (`local-pty-provider.ts:762-764`, `pty-subprocess.ts:762-764`) (**mutation proof:** siting the refusal at `computeLaneLaunch` instead turns it red, because the resolved shell is not in `PtySpawnOptions` and the fixture's pane is one the *anchor* classifies as host and the *provider* resolves to `wsl.exe` — the 22-line classifier gap of §2m(5), made into a test); after S9e the same pane carries a **Linux** lane path under that distro's lane root and nothing else (mutation proof: deleting the typed assertion turns it red). **Resolved shell is per-pane (row 36, rev 16):** a lane `pty:spawn` carrying `shellOverride: 'pwsh.exe'` and one carrying `'cmd.exe'` each spawn with the lane's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` intact and are **not** refused, and the managed statusline is the `cmd` variant in both (`statusline-script.ts:17-18`) **[code]**, so §2k's attribution is identical (**mutation proof:** treating the host `terminalWindowsShell` setting as the pane's shell — rev 15's reading — turns these red, because the setting names Git Bash or PowerShell while the pane resolved to neither). **And the assertion's scope, its own control:** a **lane-less** managed-WSL account pane still launches with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` = that account's `wslLinuxAuthPath` under `claude-accounts/` (`runtime-auth-service.ts:693`, `claude-accounts/service.ts:939`) and is **not** refused (mutation proof: applying the lane-root assertion to every WSL-branch spawn rather than to lane-pinned ones turns this red — it is how S9e would break the feature it is layered on).
- **S9d UI + handoff** — presence rows carry `credentialLane`/`laneState`; the owner label resolves through the participant join (§2h). **The designation surface (rev 19):** it lists exactly the principal's bound grants, in any runtime state, and a designated lane still at `laneState: 'absent'` renders *"lane designated to \<label\>; no login completed"* beside the "sign this lane in" action rather than an empty state (mutation proof: rendering a generic "not provisioned" string turns it red — the human must be able to see *which* grant they ticked and that the remedy is a login they run, not a push they wait for; rev 33 re-points this fixture, §2a). `openInMyLane`: grant B invoking it against grant A's terminal creates under **B's** lane; a caller with no lane is refused; **a caller with no *attachment* on the source ptyId is refused, while a merely-registered connection is also refused** — which proves the check is anchored to `attachmentsOf` and not `registerConnection`; an over-long or control-character seed prompt is rejected. **And the join is asserted at the principal, not the grant:** a person's **phone**, attached to a pane their own **desktop** grant is also attached to, is **allowed**, while a grant of the *other* principal attached to the same pane is refused (**mutation proof:** comparing `.pairedDeviceId` to `ctx.pairedDeviceId` — rev 12's wording — turns the phone case red while leaving the cross-principal case green, which is exactly how a grant-keyed join passes an inattentive suite). **Mutation proof:** swapping the attachment join back to a participants lookup turns a named test red.
- **Cross-version** — `tests/e2e/cross-version-wire/cross-version-terminal-wire.unit.test.ts` stays green (Rule 1 additive fields); the harness covers the terminal stream only (`docs/reference/remote-wire-compatibility.md:101-104`) **[code]**, so the Rule 3 changes (#3, #4, #5) need their own old-client test.

**Live verification on the real box** (the only proof of the §4 pinned assumption, and the gate on which shape of R2 ships). **Rev 32 rewrites steps (1)–(3b) around a lane login and adds two steps the model cannot ship without.** (0) Provision a **fresh** lane and open its first terminal — it must reach a usable prompt with at most the expected per-lane trust prompt, and `echo $CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` must print the lane path. (0b) **The plain-shell case:** open a lane pane with *no* command, confirm `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is the lane, then type `claude` and confirm it starts in the lane account — the §2a blocker, invisible to any agent-launch-only probe. **(1) Log the lane in.** From the designated device, start a login for the lane with the expected account named; complete it in a browser; paste the code back. *Pass:* the lane reaches `laneState: 'loaded'`, `claude auth status --json` against the lane path reports **that** account, and the URL never appeared anywhere but the requesting device. **(1b) Identity refusal (I6).** Repeat (1) naming account A as expected and authenticating as account B. *Pass:* refused `accounts.lane.login_identity_mismatch`, the login directory swept, `laneState` never `loaded`. **(2) Log the lane into a *second* account** — the same flow again, which must **add** a login rather than replace one. *Pass:* the lane lists two accounts. **(3) `/status` in a live session**, note the *Login method* / account row. **(3b) Switch in the lane** to the second login — a local switch, no browser — and re-run `/status` in the **same** session. *Pass:* the account row changes with no restart; *fail path:* the §4 fallback applies and `laneState: 'restart-required'` ships end to end. **(3c) Switch back** to the first login and confirm the second switch **also** applies rather than silently no-opping. (4) `claude auth status --json` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to the lane confirms file-level identity — Orca already shells exactly this (`claude-accounts/service.ts:232-237`; `runClaudeCommand` sets the config dir from its argument at `:1080-1085`/`:1091-1097`) **[code]**. (5) The other developer's open terminal, checked the same way, is unchanged. (6) Start an agent through `terminal.createAgentSession`, through `orchestration.workerStart` with `worktree: 'current'`, through `orchestration.workerStart` with `worktree: 'new-child'`, and through `session.tabs.createTerminal` from the phone **with the desktop window open and focused** — all four report the lane account. **(7) Logout, not disconnect.** Disconnect every grant of the principal and `ls -l` the lane — `.credentials.json` is **still there** (rev 32's deletion of the disconnect wipe); then `kill -9` the runtime, restart, and check again — **still there**, and the lane still `loaded`. Then run `accounts.lane.logout` and check again — **gone**, together with every `*.tmp` sibling.

**(L1) The coexistence step — the one the model rests on, and it is a live step because nothing in the tree can prove it.** With the lane signed into account X, sign the **desktop** into the same account X and use both: run a real inference in a lane terminal and a real inference on the desktop, in either order. *Pass:* both succeed, neither reports `invalid_grant`, and `claude auth status --json` on each side still reports X. **Partially discharged already:** on 2026-08-27 a headless login in a scratch config dir on the VPS produced a grant whose access token differs from the pushed lane blob's, a real inference ran on it, and the account remained usable elsewhere **[code, executed]**. What that establishes is that **two grants can coexist at the moment they are minted**. What it does **not** establish is the next step.

**(L2) The refresh-independence step — the gating one, and it must run *after* an access token has expired.** Hold both grants idle past the access-token lifetime, then force each side to refresh (a launch or a usage tick on each). *Pass:* **both** refreshes succeed, each chain advances independently, and neither side's refresh makes the other's fail `invalid_grant`. *Fail path:* if refreshing one revokes the other, the model collapses to today's behaviour with more moving parts, and **no slice past Gate 0 ships** (§6). This is the single experiment that converts the central claim from *demonstrated for one grant pair at mint* to *demonstrated*, and it costs one waiting period rather than one login. Measure two more things while it runs, both currently **[inferred]**: whether an *N*th login evicts the first (a concurrent-grant cap), and how long a refresh **grant** itself lives, since `expiresAt` in the blob is the access token's and not the refresh token's (§7 question 14).

**(L3) `claude auth logout`, server-side or local?** Log a scratch config dir in, copy the credentials out, run `claude auth logout` in that dir, then attempt a refresh with the copied refresh token. *Pass criterion is informational, not gating:* if the refresh succeeds, logout is local-only and §2f's UI must say "removed from this box", never "revoked" (§7 question 15). (7b) **OpenClaude, unchanged:** run `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<lane> openclaude` and check which store it reads. **(9)–(12c) Windows-only, unchanged in intent** — DACL read-back, credential-store isolation, rename under lock, containment, three shells, Git Bash passthrough — and **(10) is promoted**: "provision two lanes, log in to a different account in each, delete one lane's `.credentials.json`, then run `claude auth status --json` against that lane" is no longer only a Windows gate on §2m(3); it is the **primary acceptance test of the whole model** and is asserted in-repo as well (§5's S9-L1 store bullet). Every one of (9)–(12c) is Windows-only and **cannot execute on the Linux VPS CI** (§2m(7)). (8) **macOS, on the pinned version:** delete the lane's scoped Keychain item, launch into that lane, and assert the CLI **refuses** rather than falling back to the unsuffixed service (§2i) — unchanged, and note that a lane login now writes the **scoped** item itself, since `getActiveClaudeService(configDir)` suffixes by `sha256(configDir).slice(0,8)` (`keychain.ts:85-93`) **[code]**, so darwin isolation is per-config-dir and the lane's login is exactly the item the probe is about.

## 6. Implementation slices

Ceiling is 300 lines for `.ts` (`.oxlintrc.json:138-143`), **400 for `.tsx`** (`:144-149`, the glob at `:145`, the rule at `:147` — the one S9b's `mobile/` line turns on) and 800 for tests (`:156-161`) **[code]**; `mobile/.oxlintrc.json` inherits all three (`"extends": ["../.oxlintrc.json"]`) **[code]**. Seven files this design edits are already on the ratchet — `runtime-auth-service.ts` (1806, `config/max-lines-baseline.txt:29`), `claude-accounts/service.ts` (1275, `:31`), `ipc/pty.ts` (7887, `:99`), `src/main/persistence.ts` (`:120`), `runtime/orca-runtime.ts` (`:141`), `runtime/rpc/methods/terminal.ts` (`:149`) and `AccountsPane.tsx` (`:240`) **[code]**. The ratchet is a *file list*, not a per-file count (`config/max-lines-baseline.txt:1-4`) **[code]**, so growing them breaks no CI rule and no AGENTS.md rule — but the discipline applies to **all seven**: each edit is net-neutral or paired with an extraction, and nothing new lands in their bodies that could live in a new module. **One file this design edits is under a *hard* ceiling rather than the ratchet, and rev 21 states the difference because the mobile budget below turns on it (round 9's second major):** `mobile/app/h/[hostId]/accounts.tsx` is **not** on the baseline list, so it has no grandfathered budget at all — it is bound by the flat 400, and it cannot be given one, because a per-file `max-lines` bump in `mobile/.oxlintrc.json` is forbidden by name (`AGENTS.md:16`) and `config/max-lines-baseline.txt:1-4` restates the remedy as *"split the oversized file instead"* **[code]**. There the extraction is not a discipline but the only legal way to land the edit, and S9b budgets it.

It filters on nothing and asserts nothing about device kind. **Rev 32 — the re-basing slices, S9-L1 … S9-L4.** Everything above this line is the record of what S9a–S9d shipped, and it stays as the record. What follows replaces the *credential source* those slices built, and it is written as four slices plus a gate, sized per the tiering policy. **Nothing here re-sequences S9a's funnel, S9a's env/arg hardening, S9c's lifecycle fence or S9d's renderer seams; all four survive intact and are the reason this is a smaller change than its blast radius on this document suggests.**

- **Gate 0 — the refresh-independence experiment. Not a code slice, and no slice past it starts until it clears.** §5's live step (L2): hold a lane grant and a desktop grant on one account past the access-token lifetime, force a refresh on each, and confirm neither revokes the other. **Partially discharged on 2026-08-27** — a headless login in a scratch config dir on the VPS produced an independent grant, ran a real inference, and left the account usable elsewhere **[code, executed]** — so what remains is *longevity*, not feasibility. Measure the concurrent-grant cap and the refresh grant's own lifetime in the same pass (§7 question 14). **If it fails, the model collapses to today's behaviour with more moving parts and this whole section is withdrawn**; the failure is cheap to discover and expensive to discover late, which is why it is a gate and not a follow-up.
- **S9-L1 — host login sessions + the per-lane account store.** *model: opus, high effort — this is the only genuinely new mechanism in the re-basing, and it is security-sensitive parsing.* New: `claude-accounts/lane-login-session.ts` (~220) — `start(laneId, expectedEmail)` mkdirs `<lane>/claude-accounts/<uuid>/auth` **and writes its `.orca-managed-claude-auth` marker before anything is spawned (rev 34 — a marker written after capture makes a crashed login's directory unresolvable and therefore unpurgeable, §2a)**, spawns `claude auth login --claudeai --email <…>` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to it and **stdin as a pipe** (verified sufficient; no PTY, so none of `rate-limits/claude-pty.ts`'s hidden-PTY machinery is needed), reusing `runClaudeCommand`'s spawn/kill/process-group shape (`claude-accounts/service.ts:1099-1160`) **[code]** rather than re-deriving it; reads the **printed** URL off the **live stream** (OSC-8 stripped, first match, never the tail-truncated buffer at `:1133-1134`), asserts its `redirect_uri` host, and returns it. `submitCode(sessionId, code)` writes the code plus `\n` to the held stdin, awaits exit, then runs `['auth','status','--json']` in the same dir and verifies identity against `expectedEmail` before the lane transitions. Session state is per-lane, single-in-flight **across every entry point that mints a session — the host-inline `orca lane login` included (§2d(iv))** — TTL-bounded and retry-capped, and **in-memory, never persisted**, so an Orca restart ends every session and a later submit is the named `accounts.lane.login_session_unknown`. `cancel` kills the process group, **sweeps the half-written `<laneAccountId>/auth`** and — rev 35 — **invalidates the session in any state before `captured`, a child-exited-but-capture-pending session included (§2b, §2d rule (2), §2f)**, and it is invoked from **four** places: the TTL, an explicit `loginCancel`, a designation **clear or move** or a revoke of the owning grant (**the move is rev 34's addition, §2d rule (3)**), and **§2f's lifecycle fence** (logout / last-grant revoke / deprovision) — which also imposes the start-side precondition refusing a `loginStart` into a lane whose wipe is pending, `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress`. A child that exits unexpectedly **reaps its own session**, so the next submit is a named refusal and never an `EPIPE` into a dead pipe. New: `claude-accounts/principal-lane-account-store.ts` (~150) — `managed-auth-path.ts` parameterised over its root so `<userData>/claude-accounts` and `<lane>/claude-accounts` share one implementation, containment, symlink refusal, marker and `0600` atomic writes verbatim (`managed-auth-path.ts:12-14`, `:16-66`, `:68-93`) **[code]**; `listLaneAccounts` / `selectLaneAccount` / `removeLaneAccount` / **`purgeLaneAccountStore`**, with select reusing `LaneCredentialWriter` under `beginClaudeAuthSwitch(laneId)` and the purge **wired into `principal-lane-credential-sweep.ts` so §2f's wipe covers the store and not only the lane's top level** — today's sweep reads the lane directory's own level and matches `.credentials.json` plus `*.tmp` and nothing else (`principal-lane-credential-sweep.ts:48-58`, `:109-117`) **[code]**, which is exactly the gap, and closing it is an S9-L1 obligation rather than a note. Edits: `runtime/rpc/methods/claude-credential-lanes.ts` (the four login methods, `selectAccount`, `removeAccount`, `logout`; `push`/`pullRotated`/`clear`/`setDelegableAccounts` deleted in S9-L3), `runtime/lane-status-stream.ts:24-36` (the frame union loses `switch-requested`/`switch-failed`, gains `login-started`/`login-completed`/`login-failed`), `shared/claude-lane-refusals.ts` (mint **fifteen** — rev 34 adds `accounts.lane.switch_in_progress` and `accounts.lane.login_store_full` — retire sixteen; S9-L3 does the retiring), `shared/protocol-version.ts:109` (`agent.identity-lanes.v2`), `shared/lane-env-key-case.ts` (the **fourth** `win32` collapse site, on the login spawn's env, §3's degradation row), and `claude-fetcher.ts:1216` (the delegation gate becomes a lane-preparation gate, §2e). **Five obligations rev 34 adds to this slice.** Four land inside the two new modules above; the startup and shutdown arms of (3) also touch `index.ts:2241-2250` and `daemon-init.ts:1043-1061`, the same anchors S9c's startup ordering already uses. **(1)** A **lane-local account index** — one row per completed login (`laneAccountId`, verified identity, owner-set label, which is active), written **only after I6 passes**, living in **`<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json`** at `0600` through `writeFileAtomically(…, { mode: 0o600 })` (`codex-accounts/fs-utils.ts:7-15`) **[code]**, with `listLaneAccounts` joining it rather than walking directories and returning **empty**, never a walk, when it cannot be read. **Rev 35 reverses rev 34 on where it lives:** *not* in `principal-lane-store.ts` and *not* in Orca's persisted state — that store holds its rows through `persistence` (`principal-lane-store.ts:66`, `:135-137`; `persistence.ts:7304`) **[code]**, and `Persistence.load` falls through its backup chain to `getDefaultPersistedState(homedir())` on a corrupt file (`persistence.ts:3160`, `:3866-3868`, `:3888`) **[code]**, so one empty read there would make every login in every lane unindexed at once and obligation (3) would delete all of them (§2a rule (2), §2c). **(2)** `purgeLaneAccountStore` is a **directory walk** under the lane's `claude-accounts` root with containment and symlink refusal first, **not** gated on `resolveOwnedClaudeManagedAuthPath` and **not** a loop over the index — and it removes the **index file** and its `.tmp` siblings, plus every **`<laneAccountId>.quarantined-<ts>`** directory, along with the accounts (§2c rule (2), §2f classes (3) and (5)). **(3)** A **startup reconciliation** — `reconcileLaneAccountStore(laneDir)` — per lane, reading that lane's own `index.json` and walking **directory entries only**, with **two arms (rev 35)**: on an index that **parsed**, delete every unindexed directory, drop every dangling row and re-read under `LANE_SWEEP_PASSES`; on an index that is **missing, unparseable, or empty over a non-empty store**, **rename** each unindexed `<laneAccountId>` to `<laneAccountId>.quarantined-<ts>`, log the lane and the count, and **delete nothing**. It runs **before the seed**, **after** §2a's lane-level orphan pass and over the lanes that survive it, and it is **not** gated on the device registry's `loadSucceeded` — arm B is its fail-safe, not a borrowed gate. Plus a **shutdown arm** that cancels every in-flight session on the ordinary quit path (§2d rule (1), §2f). **(4)** The lane's write queue has **three** members and the wipe holds **precedence** (rev 35): `selectAccount` and the capture step both enter `authState.serializeLaneWrite(principalId, …)` before `beginClaudeAuthSwitch`, and both **re-check after acquiring it** — the wipe mark for each, and its own session's liveness for the capture — refusing `accounts.lane.wipe_in_progress` if either changed while they waited; the wipe sets that mark **before** entering the queue it is already inside today (`markLaneWipePending` at `principal-lane-lifecycle.ts:157`, the attempt loop at `:160`, `serializeLaneWrite` at `:210`, wired at `lane-credential-coordinator.ts:87`; `lane-wipe-pending.ts:21`, `:65`, `:70`) **[code]**. **The guard sits inside the turn, never before it** — `lane-wire-authority.ts:112` vs `:120-122` **[code]** is the shipped instance of exactly this defect, and it is the pattern this slice must not repeat. That gate's bare `Error` becomes `accounts.lane.switch_in_progress` (§2c, `live-pty-gate.ts:205-210`). **(5)** `MAX_LANE_LOGINS = 8` refused `accounts.lane.login_store_full` before any spawn, and `loginStatus`'s owning-grant comparison (§2c, §2d). **Gate:** re-verify the OAuth constants **and** the login parser against the pinned CLI before this merges — the pin at `oauth-refresh.ts:4-8` names 2.1.177 and the box runs 2.1.247 **[code]** (§4). **Rev 36 obligation (4′):** `cancel()` moves the session to a monotonic `cancelled` state *before* the wipe takes its queue turn; `submitCode`'s capture re-checks that state after `serializeLaneWrite` admits it and refuses `accounts.lane.login_cancelled`; the mark is not consulted for correctness.
- **S9-L2 — desktop and phone UI.** *model: sonnet, med-high.* Desktop: the lane section gains "Sign this lane into an account" (a modal showing the authorize URL as a clickable link **and** a QR — the browser that completes it may be a phone — plus the code field, labelled with the **principal, the lane and the expected account**, which is §4's fourth binding and therefore not decoration), the lane's own account list with an active-account radio and a remove action, and the owner-set friendly-name field that replaces §2b's pushed `displayName`. The existing `AccountLaneStatusSection.tsx` / `AccountLaneLeaseRow.tsx` seams are re-pointed rather than rebuilt; `DelegateHostRow` and `delegateAccountToHost` are deleted (§10(f) B3). Phone: `mobile/src/accounts/` routes the switch to `accounts.lane.selectAccount` and renders the lane's own account list, replacing `lane-delegated-switch-request.ts` (209 lines) with something smaller, and gains the login screen when the phone holds the designation (§2l). CLI parity in the same slice: `orca lane login --person <p> --email <e>` (**required, not optional — rev 34; the bracketed spelling made I6 skippable on the one path §9 makes mandatory**), `orca lane accounts --person <p>`, `orca lane use --person <p> --account <id>`, `orca lane logout --person <p>` — extending `src/cli/specs/lane.ts` / `handlers/lane.ts`, and on the host itself `login` can run the flow inline exactly as `orca account add` already does (`cli/handlers/account.ts:201-210`) **[code]**, which is what makes a headless serve box self-sufficient (§9 step 0). **That inline path is a second writer into a named lane, so it takes the session rather than a copy of it (§2d(iv)) — new in rev 33.** It registers in the same per-lane session map, is refused `accounts.lane.login_already_in_flight` against a grant-started session and refuses one in return, honours the same TTL, retry cap, I6 check and half-initialized-lane sweep, and is cancelled by §2f's wipe fence like any other session. It is exempt only from **(i-b) and (ii)** — there is no grant to measure against `delegatedGrantId` and no connection to scope a URL to, because the URL is printed to the operator's own terminal — **and it takes (i-a) like every other entry point: a principal holding no designation is refused `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` here too, with `orca lane designate` as the operator's one-command remedy at the same shell (rev 34, correcting rev 33's “(i) and (ii)”, which contradicted §2a, §3 and §5)**. That, and the now-required `--email`, are the whole of the difference — so what makes a headless box self-sufficient is the pair of verbs, `designate` then `login`, not `login` alone.
- **S9-L3 — deletion of the push model, and the migration of the one lane that is already loaded.** *model: sonnet, medium for the mechanical bulk; **opus review on the diff**, because it touches credential-handling code and a deletion that overreaches is indistinguishable from a deletion that is right.* **Deleted outright** (~1,900 lines plus their tests): `lane-delegation-lease.ts` (204), `lane-delegation-push-client.ts` (264), `lane-delegation-host-client.ts`, `lane-delegation-desktop-service.ts`, `lane-push-envelope.ts` (140), `account-residency-index.ts` (171), `runtime-credential-lane-clearing.ts` (109), `managed-account-lane-residency.ts` (112), `lane-sync-driver.ts` (207 — shrinks to a lane identity resolver rather than vanishing, §2c), `lane-delegation-directory.ts` (235), `shared/claude-lane-lease.ts` (79), `shared/claude-lane-watermark.ts` (92), and the delegable members of `shared/claude-lane-delegation.ts`. **Stripped from call sites:** `assertManagedClaudeAccountNotLaneResident` at `claude-accounts/service.ts:415` and `:472`, and `assertClaudeAccountNotDelegatedToLane` at `:475` **[code]** — the two lines that produced the incident; the delegation arm of `claude-fetcher.ts:1216`; the watermark rows in `principal-lane-store.ts`; the push arms of `lane-auth-state.ts` and its per-ACCOUNT rotation queue at `:111-120`, re-keyed per lane. **Retired refusal codes:** the sixteen §3's degradation row enumerates, against S9-L1's **fifteen** — a **net shrink of one, 57 → 56** (rev 34 mints `accounts.lane.switch_in_progress` and `accounts.lane.login_store_full`; rev 33's twelve-to-53 was correct for rev 33) (`shared/claude-lane-refusals.ts:7-65`, entries `:8-64`) **[code]**. **Retired wire rows:** §3 rows 1 (push), 2's `pullRotated`/`clear`, 9 (`requestSwitch`) and 12 (`setDelegableAccounts`). **Kept, and stated so the slice does not overreach:** `oauth-refresh.ts` in full (it still serves the managed desktop store and the shared lane), `live-pty-gate.ts` in full, `principal-lane-lifecycle.ts`'s fence and its `'grant-revoked'` arm, `principal-lane-credential-sweep.ts`, `LaneCredentialWriter` including its Windows arm, `lane-wire-composition.ts` and the T1/T2 composition-parity controls of §10(f), `principal-registry.ts`'s five consent writes, and every renderer seam S9d shipped. **Migration, and it is a slice obligation rather than a note.** The VPS lane is loaded **today** by a push. On upgrade its `.credentials.json` is a copy of a desktop credential Orca can no longer reason about, so the migration marks that lane **`unverified-legacy`**: **Orca** rotates nothing against it, it is never presented as a lane-owned grant, and the UI asks for one lane login to replace it. **It is not wiped on sight** — breaking a working box to make a bookkeeping point is the wrong trade — and it is not silently promoted either. **But "it keeps running" is on a clock, and rev 33 states whose.** That copy is byte-identical to the *desktop's* grant, and this same slice deletes `lane-delegation-lease.ts` and `runtime-credential-lane-clearing.ts` — the lease that suppressed the delegating desktop's own rotator and the rule (iv) clearing that kept the account out of that desktop's runtime file. With both gone the desktop's managed refresh re-arms against that account and rotates the single-use refresh token the legacy lane still carries, so the legacy lane is expected to fail `invalid_grant` at the **desktop's next rotation** — hours, plausibly, not weeks. It is the **last R-DR2 member** (§4, §2e), and the recovery is the one lane login this migration already asks for. **Landing order, and it is an obligation rather than a sequence note:** log the running host's lane in with a real lane login **before, or immediately after, S9-L3 lands**. S9-L3 landing *last* is necessary — the fallback code must still be in the tree while the cutover happens — and it is **not sufficient**, because the legacy copy dies on the desktop's rotation clock and not on the deployer's calendar.
- **S9-L4 — docs and runbook.** *model: sonnet, medium.* This document (done: rev 32); `docs/reference/windows-desktop-build.md`'s serve-box block and §9's day-one setup, both of which currently sequence a desktop-side "Delegate" action that no longer exists; and the **incident runbook**, which gains two items rev 32 creates: on a suspected lane compromise, revoke that lane's grant *and* check the Console's API-key list, because `org:create_api_key` is in the lane's scope set and revoking the OAuth grant may not kill a minted key (§4 R-32d); and the one-sentence shared-quota statement (§2k).

**Hard gates, rev 32 — two additions to the list below, which is otherwise unchanged and still governs S9a–S9e.** **(0) Gate 0 above blocks every slice**, including the deletion slice: nothing past it starts until refresh independence is demonstrated past the first access-token expiry (§5's live step L2, §7 question 14). **(vi) The OAuth-constant pin and the login parser are re-verified against the installed CLI before S9-L1 merges**, and `claude --version` outside the recorded tested range refuses `accounts.lane.login_cli_unsupported` at login-start (§4). Gates (i)–(v) below stand verbatim, with one subject change worth naming: §2i's macOS Keychain gate is now a gate on the **login** rather than on the push, because a lane login writes the scoped item itself (`keychain.ts:85-93`) **[code]**, and §2m(3)'s Windows credential-store gate is likewise now about where a lane's *own* login lands.

**Hard platform gates, restated as gates and not notes.** (i) **macOS** lane provisioning stays **off** until §5 step (8) passes on the pinned CLI (§2i) — unchanged from rev 10. (ii) **Windows** lane provisioning stays **off** until §5 step (10) establishes that the Windows CLI keeps no credential state outside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (§2m(3)); this is the same failure class as the macOS one — every lane silently collapsing onto one credential — and it is graded **[inferred]**, so it gets the same treatment. (iii) **Windows** lane provisioning additionally fails closed on an unverified DACL (§2m(1)), per lane, at creation — **on a *native* lane root only**, the same carve-out §2m(1), §2m(4), §2n(i), §3's degradation table and §5 all carry and rev 16's audit of this section left out of these two sentences (round 5 minor): a `parseWslUncPath` root (`shared/wsl-paths.ts:6-17`) **[code]** skips the DACL step and fails closed on the in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` read-back instead, because `restrictWindowsPathSync` returns `false` on any throw (`shared/secure-path-windows-acl.ts:60-63`) **[code]** and the unscoped rule therefore refuses every S9e lane. Dormant, since S9e is deferred. (iv) The **per-lane usage pull** ships disabled on `win32` (§2k Fact 2). (v) The Windows-only §5 controls and live steps (9)–(12c) **cannot run on the Linux VPS CI** and require a Windows runner or the owner's box; a slice does not merge with them unrun, and they must not be reported green by being skipped (§2m(7)). **Scoped to the slice that implements them — rev 16, because as written this gate contradicted the S9e deferral two bullets above it.** Two of the Windows-only controls have halves that only **S9e** can satisfy: the DACL arm's in-distro `chmod 0700/0600` **read-back**, and the containment arm's **routing** of a `wsl.localhost` root into §2n's in-distro canonicalizer — both live in `claude-accounts/principal-credential-lane-wsl.ts`, an S9e module. **S9a's gate covers the halves S9a implements** — the DACL **skip** for a `parseWslUncPath` candidate, and the Windows-side check **recognising** that root rather than refusing it — and the two S9e halves **activate with S9e**, skipped until then and never reported green. Nothing else in (9)–(12c) is S9e-dependent.

**Parallelism:** S9a's env/arg hardening and capture containment land first and alone; S9a's funnel plus the `isClaudeLaunch` decoupling is a single mechanical change best landed whole (the compiler enumerates the call sites); the S9b transport skeleton runs in parallel; S9b's refusals depend on S9a's provisioning seam; S9c depends on S9a; S9d depends on S9b; **S9b's phone-switch RPC depends on S9a's principal registry and on nothing else**, and **S9b's per-lane pull depends on S9c's per-lane gate**, so if the slices are reordered the pull moves with the gate, not with the writer the push became (rev 32: the in-lane switch and the login capture, §2c). **S9e depends on S9a and on nothing later**, which is what lets it be *deferred* cleanly rather than descoped: nothing in S9b–S9d waits on it, and reviving it later costs no re-sequencing. **Hard gates:** no slice merges without its mutation proofs; the §5 live verification — steps (0b) and (3b) especially — runs before S9b merges and **selects** R2's shape rather than blocking it (§4).

## 7. Owner decisions, and what is still open

**Q1–Q10 are RESOLVED (owner, 2026-08-22). S9 is approved in full: S9a–S9d all build.** Each answer is recorded with the consequence it had on this document, so a later reader can tell a decision from a default.

| # | Question | Owner's answer | Consequence in rev 11 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cross-grant delegation for one human | **YES** — *"if we use mobile it should be an extension of our desktops targeting the same shared dev box"* | The lane key becomes a host-minted **`principalId`** and a lane belongs to a **person**; grants are bound to a principal only by the host-side consent action, never by `--pair-name` (§2a). Every lane-keyed structure re-keys, the wipe predicate is rebuilt because B2's is grant-scoped, and the adopt/materialize gate now lets a person's phone wake their desktop's pane. The hard half — **what the phone may do on its own** — is answered in §2l, and **rev 32 answers it differently from rev 11**: the phone **acts directly on the lane**, switching among the lane's own logins with no other device involved, and it may hold the designation and start a login. Rev 11's answer, recorded here through rev 32 — "the phone selects, the desktop pushes, the host refuses `accounts.lane.desktop_unavailable` when the desktop is offline" — is deleted along with the push, the delegated switch and that refusal code (§2l, §3's matrix). Rev 10's default ("no — one grant, one lane") is **overridden**, and it is the only rev-10 decision this revision reverses |
| 2 | Rotated-token return path | **Pull it back on every receipt** | **RESOLVED BY DELETION (rev 32; struck in rev 33, which had left it stated as a live S9b requirement).** The answer was right for the model it was given for: the desktop pulled the rotated blob back over the grant-scoped channel on every `host` and `cli-observed` receipt, so its store never went stale and a post-wipe re-push could not restore a revoked blob. Under per-lane login there is **no shared blob to return** — the lane's chain is its own and the desktop's is its own — so `accounts.lane.pullRotated`, the receipt causes and the whole return path are deleted (§2c, §2e, §3 row 2). No decision is required; this is recorded so a later reader does not go looking for the pull |
| 3 | Identity label the other developer sees | **Grant/owner label + a friendly per-account name chosen by the owner** | The presence row and the projection carry the **principal** label (host-observed, unspoofable) plus an owner-authored per-account name. The **email is not shown by default** — it stays available only where the owner explicitly opts in. **The reason changed in rev 32 and the default did not:** rev 31 justified it by §2b's finding that the email was client-asserted, but under per-lane login the host observes the identity itself and cross-checks it against `auth status --json` (§2b), so the surviving reason is **privacy** — an account email is a peer-visible personal fact — and not provenance (§2h, corrected in rev 33). `laneAccountLabel` (§2k) is exactly this field |
| 4 | Handoff scope-out | **Self-switch continuity yes; cross-person handoff stays out** — *"if we're switching accounts for ourselves we want to continue the conversation, just with a different claude account"* | The half the owner wants is **native and needs no feature**: same lane, same `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, same transcripts, the running session re-resolves (§2g, new opening). §2g's cross-person scope-out **stands unchanged**, enforced by the containment refusal |
| 5 | Shared-lane switching | **NO — do not lock it** | The shared lane stays open to lane-less grants; the residual is **decided and named in full** in §2d, with the procedural mitigation (bind and provision both developers before either relies on the box) stated rather than implied |
| 6 | Lane-strict mode | **OFF** | §4's option is not built. R1 remains an **authority** guarantee, not a confidentiality one, and every seam continues to say so |
| 7 | Lane usage bars | **Per-person, per-account, and per the account attached to the terminal/agent** | The per-lane usage **pull moves into S9b** and is designed in §2k: per-lane preparation, the interactions with refresh deferral, the lane gate and §2f's wipe fence, each with a mutation proof (rev 32 deletes the fourth, the watermark, with its subject), and a `paneKey → lane → account → usage` row join that replaces the config-dir string compare. Two Rule-1 fields, `laneAccountLabel` and `laneUsage`. **Gated on Windows** by `claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064` |
| 8 | Cross-grant orchestration cut | **OK, confirmed** | §2a's ownership predicate ships as written, now comparing **principals** — which softens the cut exactly where it was most annoying: your own phone can drive your own coordinator pane |
| 9 | Lane session forking, mirror list | **OK, confirmed** | §2a's loss list and mirror allowlist ship item by item, asserted individually in §5. Forking is intended, not a regression to work around |
| 10 | OpenClaude in a lane | **Block it** | §2a's fail-closed refusal ships: `terminal.lane_agent_unsupported` for a lane launch, and a hand-typed session downgrades the row to `credentialLane: 'shared-runtime'` with its usage dropped. The §5 step (7b) probe can still retire the question, but the shipped default is the block |

**Host pivot, recorded as a decision rather than a question.** The shared dev machine will run **Windows Server**; both developers use Windows 11 desktops; the owner's personal VPS stays Linux and single-owner and is out of scope; the owner installs the Windows build on the shared machine personally. Consequences are §2m (a first-class section replacing rev 10's paragraph), §2n (S9e), the Windows gates in §6, and the honest statement in §2m(7) that the live probes cannot run on the Linux VPS CI. **Second half of the pivot, answered 2026-08-22 and recorded here as a decision rather than a question: the shells are native — Git Bash (the owner) and PowerShell (the other developer), deliberately not WSL.** Consequences are §2m(8) (the two shell facts, one graded [inferred] and gated by §5 step (12c), one a §4 residual), row 18 (the setting is host-wide, so both shells are in scope for every lane), the deferral of S9e out of the delivery order and out of every gate (§2n, §6), and §7 question 1, now resolved.

### New questions raised by the host pivot — two now answered (1 and 8), the rest open. **Citation note (rev 33):** this list restarts at 1, so it collides with the resolved table's Q1–Q10 above; anything citing these is written **"host-pivot question N"** in rev 33, and "§7 question N" on its own means the resolved table. The rev-32 list below continues *this* sequence at 11–18 and is cited as **"§7 question N"** without ambiguity, since the resolved table stops at 10.

1. **RESOLVED (owner, 2026-08-22): native Windows shells, not WSL — and the consequence is a *deferral*, not a descope.** The question was which shells the two developers' agents run in on the shared box. The answer: **native terminals only — the owner uses Git Bash, the other developer uses PowerShell, deliberately not WSL.** So S9a–S9d isolate everything that matters for daily work, and **S9e (§2n) is deferred**: a designed, independently shippable slice, **out of the S9 delivery order and out of every gate in §6**, with nothing in S9b–S9d waiting on it. No "BLOCKING" framing survives anywhere in this document. **Rev 16 closes the one place that claim was not yet true:** §6 gate (v) made the Windows-only §5 controls an S9a merge gate, and two of those controls had halves only S9e implements — the DACL arm's in-distro `chmod` read-back and the containment arm's routing into §2n's canonicalizer. Each is now split: the half S9a implements (the DACL **skip** for a `parseWslUncPath` candidate; the Windows-side check **recognising** that root rather than refusing it) gates S9a, and the S9e half **activates with S9e**, skipped and never faked green (§5, §6 gate (v)). **Two obligations came *back* from the answer rather than going away, and both are §2m(8):** Git Bash's MSYS conversion — it rewrites path-looking *arguments* and passes the environment block through, which is what lets a Windows lane path reach a native `claude.exe` from a Git Bash pane, graded **[inferred]**, gated by §5 step **(12c)** and carrying a named refusal (`terminal.lane_shell_unsupported`) if the probe fails — and PowerShell's **`$PROFILE`**, which runs at pane start, i.e. after every guard in this design, and is therefore a §4 residual of the shell-rc class, named and not closed. **And the answer is not an enforcement:** a project runtime of kind `wsl` still forces a WSL shell (`shared/local-windows-terminal-runtime.ts:52-57`) **[code]**, where a lane pane is *refused* (`terminal.lane_wsl_shell_unsupported`) rather than silently un-isolated — which is exactly what makes deferring S9e safe rather than optimistic.
2. **The Windows credential-store probe is a gate on the whole thing (§2m(3), §5 step (10)).** Orca's own code treats `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/.credentials.json` as the entire store on Windows, and nothing in the tree observes whether the Windows CLI *also* keeps a DPAPI or Credential Manager entry. Graded **[inferred]**. Until the probe runs on the owner's box, Windows lane provisioning is off — which means **S9 cannot be used on the target host at all**. Who runs it, and when? It is a twenty-minute manual check and it gates everything.
3. **Windows administrators are inside every lane's ACL by construction (§2m(6)).** The hardening script grants FullControl to the user, SYSTEM and `S-1-5-32-544` (Administrators). If both developers are local administrators on the shared box — the default for a machine they both install software on — then "co-tenant can read a resident lane file" holds for either of them regardless of which Windows account `orca serve` runs under. Accept it as the §4-class residual it is, or make one developer a non-administrator on that box (which changes nothing about R1 and everything about the residual)?
4. **RESOLVED BY DELETION (rev 32); recorded verbatim below because part (a)'s *tick* survives under a new name.** The delegation lease, the pusher designation, `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated` and the "no push received from \<label\>" state are all deleted (§2b–§2e). What survives is the consent tick itself, re-meant to *"which grant may start a login for this lane and see the authorization URL"* — and **question 13 below is where the owner is asked to ratify that meaning**, so nothing here is still open. Part (b), whether the lease should follow the most recently active desktop, has no subject at all. The original text is kept unchanged because it is the clearest statement of what the designation *was*, which is what makes question 13 legible: **Two desktops in one principal, and who holds the lease.** §2e's delegation lease is **host-published** and, since rev 14, **written only by the provisioning consent action**: it names one pushing desktop per principal, refuses the others `accounts.lane.push_not_delegated`, and — the part that actually closes the second rotator — is the signal every other bound desktop reads to suppress its own token refresh. Rev 13's alternative writer ("pinned from the first accepted push") is deleted, because an authority value written by a client action lets any grant of the principal seize the lease by racing (§2e). Two consequences to confirm. **(a)** A lane cannot be provisioned for a principal **none of whose bound grants has been designated as its pusher** — refused `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated` (§2a, §2e). **Rev 19 changes what that designation *is*, and it changes what you are being asked to accept, so it is restated here rather than left in §2a.** Revs 15–18 tried three times to have the *host* work out which grant is a pushing desktop — from `scope`, then from a capability the host advertises, then from one the client declares plus a memory of it — and the orchestrator has ruled that out: **the designation is a plain tick, by you, over the grants already bound to that principal, available at any time on the same local UI (or the same local `orca` command) that binds them, and persisted like a binding.** For you and one desktop that is **one tick**. The host makes **no** judgement about what kind of device a grant is; ticking your phone, or an `orca` CLI grant, is allowed and **inert** — no push arrives, the lane sits at `laneState: 'absent'`, the UI says *"no push received from \<label\>"*, and you re-tick. What you are accepting is therefore a recovery story rather than a prevention story, on a surface only someone at the machine can reach. Correct, or should provisioning be allowed to sit in a designation-less, permanently-`absent` state until you get round to it? **(b)** Is a manual "move the lease to this desktop" control in the UI enough, or do you want the lease to follow the most recently active desktop automatically? Automatic is friendlier and is exactly the R-DR2 shape that revokes tokens out from under live sessions, so the design chose manual; confirm.
5. **Revoking one device of a person does not wipe the lane (§2a).** Only the principal's last grant does. That is correct under Q1 and it is also surprising — "I revoked the lost phone, is my account still on that box?" The answer is yes — and **rev 32 makes it more surprising, not less** (corrected in rev 33, which still said "until the desktop disconnects"): it is yes until an **explicit logout**, a last-grant revoke or a deprovision. The disconnect wipe and the startup wipe are deleted, so the at-rest window is unbounded (§2f, R-32a, and question 12 below asks you to ratify exactly that). Should the revoke dialog offer a **"and wipe the lane now"** checkbox, which is a one-line addition to S9c's revoke path? The question is unchanged and the case for it is stronger than it was.
6. **Per-lane usage pull cost on the shared box.** §2k's pull is `N` hidden `claude` processes per tick, one per loaded lane, each holding that lane's token and deferring its rotation for the probe's lifetime. On this box `N ≤ 3` and the tick interval is unchanged, but every probe is a real process on a machine two people are working on. Acceptable as designed, or should the pull run at a **slower cadence than the shared-lane fetch** — say, only when a lane's row is actually on screen?
7. **RESOLVED BY DELETION (rev 32; struck in rev 33, which retired question *10* for this exact reason and left this one standing).** **Its subject is deleted:** the phone taps the **lane's own** account list, which the host derives from the lane's own store and has therefore always held, so there is no allowlist to curate in either direction and no ticked account names or opted-in emails reach the host at all (§2b, §2l, §3 row 12, question 11 below). The original text: *"The delegable-account list is an owner-curated allowlist (§2l). The phone can request a switch only to accounts the owner has ticked. Is per-account ticking the interaction you want, or should every account on the desktop be delegable by default with an opt-out? Default-in is friendlier; default-out is the direction every other decision in this document went."* No decision is required.

8. **RESOLVED (orchestrator, 2026-08-22): the federated link gets a principal binding — option (b). (§2a, raised in rev 13, answered in rev 15.)** The federated create (`orchestration-federation.ts:90`) has no parent pane (`lineage: { noParent: true }`, `:106`) and no `pairedDeviceId` — its only caller identity is a home-peer fingerprint (`:63`) **[code]** — so neither of §2a's derivations reaches it, and rev 12's "binds to the link's grant" named a key `credentialLane` has no case for. Rev 13–14 left it **failing closed**, which is correct and is also a functional cut on the primary federated-dispatch path: no link→principal binding exists in this tree, so with no registry write *every* federated worktree create is refused `terminal.lane_link_unbound`. Three options were put: (a) leave it closed and treat federated worktree creation as out of scope for S9 (rev 13–14's text); (b) add link→principal binding to `principal-registry.ts` as one more consent write, ~20 lines and one more thing the local human must tick per link; (c) let a federated create pass explicit `{ kind: 'shared' }`, which is the option this document refuses everywhere else and should be refused here too. **RESOLVED: (b), on the orchestrator's decision of 2026-08-22.** It ships as the **fifth** host-side consent write in the principal registry, beside bind / unbind / re-bind / designate-the-pusher, keyed by the link's **home-peer fingerprint** — the only stable link identity in this tree (`runtime/orchestration/types.ts:205`, column `runtime/orchestration/db.ts:504`, authorized against at `orchestration-federation-relay.ts:227` and `orchestration-federation-control.ts:175`) **[code]**. A federated create then binds `{ kind: 'principal', principalId }` from the link's binding **or fails closed** `terminal.lane_link_unbound` — never a grant, never an implicit `shared`. **The one extra human tick, stated because it is the whole of the cost:** each federated link that should be able to create worktrees is ticked once, on the same consent surface and with the same audit row as a device bind; an unticked link keeps rev 13's behaviour, which is the refusal. Option (c) stays refused for the reason it is refused everywhere else. §2a states it at the site; §6 carries it in `principal-registry.ts`; §5 asserts both arms. **Rev 16 corrects the *key*, and the correction is load-bearing rather than cosmetic.** Rev 15 called the home-peer fingerprint a link identity on the strength of where it is stored and compared. Re-opened at the function that produces it, `authenticatedCallerFingerprint` is `sha256(request.authToken || request.deviceToken || 'authenticated_transport')` (`rpc/orchestration-mutation-executor.ts:128-134`, spent at `:32`, threaded at `dispatcher.ts:100`) **[code]** — so it is the shared per-runtime `authToken` (`runtime-rpc.ts:507`, enforced `:1664-1669`) for **every** local caller including both developers on the one Windows account, and `sha256(deviceToken)` — per grant, rotated on re-pair (`device-registry.ts:97`) — for a paired peer. Binding a principal to it as written would have bound the *other developer's* local calls into that principal's lane. **The answer stays (b); the binding gains three clauses (§2a):** the two host-wide values (`sha256(authToken)`, `sha256('authenticated_transport')`) are refused by name; a bindable fingerprint must resolve to **exactly one** paired grant's `token`, and the row records `boundDeviceId` so the audit names the grant — **and, rev 17, records *only* that: the link row carries no `principalId` of its own, because the grant clause (b) resolves already has one and the create reads `principalOf(boundDeviceId)`, which removes a duplicate that could have disagreed with it and leaves one revocation point** — and a re-pair stops matching, so the link returns to `terminal.lane_link_unbound` until re-ticked — one more tick, and a refusal rather than a wrong-lane create in between. **Nothing else about the answer moves,** and the extra cost is bounded: federated creates from an anonymous local socket are now refused rather than bindable, which is the same direction every other decision here went.

9. **RESOLVED BY DELETION (rev 32; struck in rev 33).** Rule (iv) and the lease that carried it are deleted, so the choice this question offered — clear-and-refuse versus a loud warning plus detection — has no subject: the delegating desktop's own runtime file is never touched, a local `selectClaude` is never refused on lease grounds, and the double-rotation the rule prevented cannot arise, because the lane's chain is not the desktop's (§2e, §3's matrix). The `foreign-rotation` receipt path goes with it. **One exception is live and is not this question:** the `unverified-legacy` migration lane *does* hold a second copy of a desktop grant, and deleting rule (iv) is precisely what re-arms the desktop's rotator against it — that is §3's migration row and §6's S9-L3, not a standing policy choice. The original text follows unchanged, because it is the clearest statement of the hazard the re-basing removed: **The lease now reaches your own desktop's `~/.claude` — rule (iv), new in rev 14 (§2e).** Rev 13's lease suppressed Orca's rotator and Orca's managed launches on every bound desktop, and left a third copy of the credential untouched: the **runtime credential file on the delegating desktop itself**, written by an ordinary `accounts.selectClaude` there (`runtime-auth-service.ts:477-478`) **[code]**. A plain `claude` in any non-Orca terminal on that Windows 11 desktop reads that file and rotates the lane's single-use refresh token, and neither the host's residency index nor that machine's live-PTY gate can see it — R-DR2 on the machine §2e calls safe. Rule (iv) therefore **clears the delegated account out of the delegating desktop's own runtime file** and refuses a local `selectClaude` of it while the lease stands. **The cost is on your daily machine, which is why it is a question and not only a decision:** while account X is delegated to the shared host, a bare `claude` in your own Windows terminal no longer runs as X — you would switch it back with `accounts.lane.clear`, or use a different account locally. Accept that, or would you rather rule (iv) be a **loud warning plus detection** (the `foreign-rotation` receipt path) instead of a clear-and-refuse, accepting the double-rotation risk on your own box?

10. **Does the owner accept that the host holds their *ticked* account list? (§2b, §3 row 12, new in rev 22.)** §2l's phone flow cannot exist without it — the phone must be shown something to tap, and §2b forbids the list riding the push — so `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` ships the list to the host, where it persists in `claudeLaneDelegationRows`. That makes §2b's old blanket sentence ("the host never receives the owner's account **list**") false, and it is corrected there rather than left standing. The exposure is bounded — only ticked accounts, only an opaque token plus an owner-authored `displayName` and an **opted-in** `email`, no credential and no store id — and §2d keeps the list readable only by that principal's own grants. But §4 grades a paired peer shell-equivalent on this box, so a co-tenant who is already shell-equivalent learns the owner's ticked account names and any opted-in email. Q3 answered the peer-visible *label*; it did not answer this. **Options:** (a) accept as shipped; (b) drop `email` from the write entirely and let the phone disambiguate on `displayName` alone; (c) hold the delegable list — and with it §2l's phone switch — until the owner rules. Shipped today as (a), flagged here for ratification.

### Rev-32 questions — the credential-source re-basing (§2a–§2f, §2h, §2k, §2l, §3, §4). Every one carries a default, and **every default below is applied in this document ahead of ratification**, which is stated rather than implied. These continue the host-pivot list's numbering at 11 (see the citation note above).

11. **RESOLVED BY DELETION (rev 32).** Host-pivot question 10 above (the second list's tenth, not the resolved table's) asked the owner to accept that the host holds their *ticked* account list (`claudeLaneDelegationRows`). **Its subject is deleted**: under per-lane login the phone taps the lane's own account list, which the host has always held, so `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` and the delegation directory go (§2b, §3 row 12) and the host stops holding the owner's account names and opted-in emails entirely. §8 item 5 closes with it. No decision is required; this is recorded so a later reader does not go looking for one.
12. **Do you ratify the unbounded at-rest window? — the one axis on which rev 32 is *weaker* than push, and the only place it is.** A lane's credential now sits at rest from login until an explicit logout, a last-grant revoke or a deprovision. It is **not** wiped on disconnect, on idle timeout, on an Orca restart or on a host reboot. So a co-tenant's read window on a lane credential goes from *bounded* (push → close/idle/next-start) to *unbounded*, and on the Windows Server host the lane's DACL includes the local Administrators group by construction (§2m(6)). ***Recommended default: accept, and it is what §2f already implements.*** The reason is that the alternative fails your own stated requirement: with a browser round-trip as the recovery path, wiping on disconnect turns every dropped Wi-Fi into a re-authorization, which is worse than push on exactly the axis you objected to. The mitigating argument, stated as an argument: what sits at rest is now an **independently revocable** grant rather than a copy of your desktop's own credential, so the same exfiltration is recoverable in a way it was not before — and the whole window sits inside §4's shell-equivalence concession you have already accepted twice for this box.
13. **Do you ratify the designation's new meaning, and that a phone may hold it?** `delegatedGrantId` stops meaning "the one grant that may push credentials into this lane" and starts meaning "the one grant that may **start a login** for this lane and **see the URL**" (§2d(i)). Two consequences you are being asked to accept. **(a) A phone is a legitimate designation**, because a login moves no secret — the phone displays a URL and takes a code — and refusing it would encode a device-kind judgement the host provably cannot make (§2a). **(b) A mis-tick stops being inert.** §2a's "no push arrives, nothing is written, one re-tick fixes it" is false after rev 32: the wrong device *of the right person* can complete a login and load that person's own lane. It is bounded — designation ranges only over grants already bound to that principal, and I6 still refuses an account you did not name — but it is a real change in what a wrong tick costs. ***Recommended default: yes to both, single designated grant, phone allowed — which is what §2d implements.*** The alternative, named because it is the friendlier one and the design does not take it: let **any** grant bound to the principal start a login, which removes the re-tick entirely and widens §4's substitution audience from one device to all of that person's devices.
14. **GATING — will you run the refresh-independence experiment, and how long will you give it?** Today's VPS probe established that two grants **coexist at the moment they are minted** — a headless login in a scratch config dir produced an independent access token, ran a real inference, and left the account usable elsewhere. It did **not** establish that each chain still refreshes *after the first access token expires*, and nothing in the tree can establish it. §5's live step (L2) and §6's Gate 0 hold every slice on it. Two more unknowns ride the same experiment: whether Anthropic caps concurrent Claude Code grants per account (and whether an *N*th login evicts the first), and how long a **refresh** grant itself lives — `expiresAt` in the blob is the access token's, not the refresh token's. ***Recommended default: yes, on a low-value scratch account, before S9-L1 starts.*** It costs a waiting period rather than a login, and a false result collapses the model back to today's behaviour with more moving parts, which is much cheaper to learn now than after S9-L3 has deleted the fallback.
15. **May the UI say "revoked", or must it say "removed from this box"?** `accounts.lane.logout` can run `claude auth logout` inside the lane before sweeping — a capability push never had, because under push deleting the lane's copy left the grant alive on the desktop. **Whether `claude auth logout` performs a server-side revoke or only clears local state is [inferred] and unverified** (§5's live step L3 settles it in one probe). ***Recommended default: promise nothing until the probe runs.*** Until then `logout` means exactly what `clear` meant — the credential is gone from this box — and §2f's copy says that and no more. If the probe shows a real revoke, this is the largest single security improvement left on the table and the copy changes in one line.
16. **`org:create_api_key` is in the lane's scope set, and there is no scope flag.** The observed authorization request asks for `scope=org:create_api_key user:profile user:inference user:sessions:claude_code user:mcp_servers user:file_upload` **[code, observed verbatim on 2.1.247]**, so a lane credential can mint an Anthropic API key — a separate credential with its own lifetime that revoking the OAuth grant does not necessarily invalidate. **Not a regression** (the same scope rode the pushed blob), but per-lane login multiplies the number of independently-held grants that can do it, and it makes "revoke the lane" feel more complete than it is. `claude auth login --help` on 2.1.247 offers no scope flag **[code]**. ***Recommended default: accept and state it — lane revocation is not proof no API key was minted — and add a Console API-key check to the incident runbook (§6's S9-L4).*** Revisit if a narrower scope set ever becomes obtainable.
17. **How should the currently-loaded VPS lane migrate?** That lane holds a pushed copy of a desktop credential today. On upgrade Orca can no longer reason about it: it is not a lane-owned grant and it is not the desktop's live one either. ***Recommended default: mark it `unverified-legacy` — it keeps running, nothing rotates it, it is never presented as a lane-owned grant, and the UI asks for one lane login to replace it (§6's S9-L3).*** The two alternatives are both worse: wiping it on upgrade breaks a working box to make a bookkeeping point, and silently promoting it to a lane-owned grant asserts a provenance Orca cannot check.
18. **Should a lane's multi-account switch be exposed on first ship, or built and hidden?** With L1 gone, a lane may hold several logins and switch among them locally (§2c) — the store costs nothing extra, since it is the same per-account subdirectory shape `managed-auth-path.ts` already implements. ***Recommended default: build the store for N and expose the switch, because §2l's phone half is exactly this capability and hiding it would leave the phone with one verb it cannot use.*** The narrower alternative — one account per lane in the UI, N in the store — is available and costs only the S9-L2 surface.
19. **Do you ratify destroying an unindexed login directory at startup — and the eight-login cap? (New in rev 34.)** Round 2's blocker is that a login can outlive the host process: after a `kill -9` landing between the CLI's capture write and Orca's own bookkeeping, `<lane>/claude-accounts/<laneAccountId>/auth/` holds a complete, independently usable grant at `0600` that no session owns and that no rule in rev 33 reached. ***Recommended default, applied in this document and re-gated in rev 35: the lane's **own** account index — `<lane>/claude-accounts/index.json`, inside the lane rather than in Orca's persisted state — is the authority; startup deletes every directory with no row **in an index that parsed**, and an index that is missing or unparseable **quarantines** those directories instead (§2a rule (2), §2c, §2f).*** Three things you are accepting. **(a)** A crash inside that window **destroys a working grant** — the credential is deleted locally and the person signs in again, one browser round-trip. The alternative is keeping unindexed directories, which is a durable grant at rest that no wipe rule in this document reaches, and that is the failure this closes. **(b)** Deleting it locally **does not revoke it upstream** (R-34a, §4): the grant lives at its source until it expires or you revoke it, so “the box is clean” is a claim about the box and not about the account. **(c) A quarantined directory is a complete grant left at rest** — renamed `<laneAccountId>.quarantined-<ts>`, resolvable by nothing, logged rather than published — until an operator removes it or that lane's next logout / revoke / deprovision sweeps it. That inversion is deliberate: a parse error is evidence about the index, not about the directories, and deleting N working logins on one is the larger harm. It is also the price of not repeating rev 34's version of this pass, which read the index out of Orca's persisted state and would therefore have deleted **every login in every lane on the box** the first time that state file was lost, corrupted or restored from an older backup (§2f, R-34a). The same paragraph caps the store at **eight** logins (`accounts.lane.login_store_full`, §2c), replacing the `MAX_LANE_DELEGABLE_ACCOUNTS` bound rev 32 deleted with the delegable list and put nothing in place of; say if eight is the wrong number.

### Still unresolved, and not by choice

- **Whether the Windows CLI honours `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for *credentials* as completely as it does for config** is [inferred] and cannot be settled from this tree — question 2 above, §5 step (10). It is the single largest unresolved item, and it gates the target host.
