- **Why `getManagedUsagePanelAuthPreparation` returns `null` on `win32`** (`rate-limits/claude-fetcher.ts:1062-1064`) is unexplained in the code and unrecoverable from it. Until someone establishes the reason, the per-lane pull cannot be enabled on Windows on evidence — only on a guess, which this design declines to make (§2k Fact 2).
- **Whether OpenClaude honours `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`** remains the rev-10 [inferred], now moot for shipping (Q10 = block) but still worth the §5 step (7b) probe, because a positive result collapses a refusal into one allowlist line.
- **The `\\wsl.localhost` write path's failure modes under load** (§2n) are asserted from the 9p redirector's general behaviour, not observed here; S9e's first live step must measure a push through it rather than assume the Windows retry budget is sufficient.
- **Whether two OAuth grants on one Claude account keep refreshing independently past the first access-token expiry** is the load-bearing unknown rev 32 introduces, and it is the only one that is *gating*. Today's VPS probe demonstrated coexistence **at mint** — an independent access token, a real inference, the account still usable elsewhere **[code, executed 2026-08-27]** — and demonstrated nothing about the chains after they rotate. §5's live step (L2) and §6's Gate 0 hold every slice on it; §7 question 14 asks for it.
- **Whether Anthropic caps concurrent Claude Code grants per account, and how long a refresh grant itself lives**, ride the same experiment. `expiresAt` in the credential blob is the *access* token's, so nothing in the tree or in the blob states the refresh grant's own lifetime; if grants expire on a schedule or an *N*th login evicts the first, per-lane login means periodic re-authorization, which is the owner's exact objection reappearing. Degrades gracefully (re-login) rather than corrupting, but the UI copy has to say so.
- **Whether `claude auth logout` revokes server-side or only clears local state** decides whether §2f's logout can honestly promise revocation, and it is the difference between §4's largest claimed improvement being real or cosmetic. One probe settles it (§5's live step L3, §7 question 15).
- **Whether `claude auth login` into a config dir that already holds credentials replaces them cleanly**, or needs a `claude auth logout` first. §2c's in-lane switch does not depend on it, because it writes the blob directly; the re-login-after-logout path does. One probe, unrun.

## 8. Open review items (to resolve during implementation review)

Round 10 returned **zero blockers** and two majors. Both are recorded here verbatim rather than applied, and each is dispositioned to the slice review that must close it. **Items 1 and 2 are now closed in rev 22, during S9b review round 2; the dispositions below say how, and the item text above each is kept verbatim so the correction can be read against what was found.** **Rev 24 adds items 3–5, which are residuals the shipped S9b created or left standing (§10); they are open, not dispositioned away.** **Item 4 is closed in rev 29, during the 2026-08-24 release audit (§10(f)); its disposition below records what was actually wrong and what closed it.** **Rev 32 disposes of two of the five without a decision being taken, because the re-basing deletes their subjects: item 5 (the host holding the owner's ticked account list) closes with the delegable list, and item 4's residency predicates close with L1.** Item 3 stays open and its subject survives; items 1 and 2 remain resolved. **Rev 33 withdraws rev 32's claim that it created no new open review item.** Rev 32's *unknowns* were indeed experiments — they live in §7's *Still unresolved* list and in §6's Gate 0 — but the review round on rev 32 returned five blockers and ten majors, and three of those were **findings**, not experiments: the lane login session sat outside §2f's lifecycle fence though it is a post-sweep writer; the wipe's scope omitted the per-lane account store §2c had just introduced, so "the credential is gone from this box" was false for every non-active login; and I6 was credited as a control against URL substitution that it cannot be, because `expectedEmail` is attacker-supplied. **All three are applied in rev 33** — §2f, §3's matrix, §5 and §6's S9-L1 for the first two, §2b, §3 row 1 and §4 for the third — so they close here rather than opening as items 6–8. This sentence exists so a later reader knows they were findings and where they landed. Item 3 stays open and its subject survives; items 1 and 2 remain resolved.

1. **Round-9 major 1 was not applied at §2a; §2l cites §2a as already carrying a scope §2a does not write.**
   *Where:* `/home/ubuntu/orca-resilience-docs/docs/reference/agent-identity-s9-design.md:122` (§2a, "One residual the recovery does not cover") against `:313` (§2l) and `:403` (§3 degradation row).
   *Detail:* Rev 21's own preamble says round 9's first finding was "Corrected consistently at §2l, §5 and §3's degradation row" — §2a is not in that list, and it is the one site that still carries the pre-rev-21 scope. §2a:121 reads: "a designated grant that is *connected* but cannot act still satisfies §2l's liveness precondition, so a phone's `requestSwitch` is emitted rather than refused up front and runs to `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`." Under rev 21 that general clause is false for the very grant class rev 21 added: a designated `orca` CLI grant is *connected* (its one-shot socket authenticates) and does NOT satisfy the precondition, because the precondition's second conjunct is a live lane-status subscription and the CLI has no subscribe entry point at all — verified in this tree: `src/cli/runtime/websocket-transport.ts` is 51 lines exporting only `sendWebSocketRequest` (`:11` → `:19`) and `sendWebSocketRequestWithStatusPreflight` (`:28` → `:38`), `grep -rn subscribe src/cli` returns zero hits, and `subscribeRemoteRuntimeRequest` (`src/shared/remote-runtime-client.ts:494`) has exactly one production caller, `src/main/ipc/runtime-environment-transport-routing.ts:246`. Worse, §2l:312 asserts the opposite of what §2a says: "The `switch_timed_out` residual is the connected-**and-subscribed** case and only that, which is the scope §2a:121 already writes with the words *'a designated grant that is **connected**'*." The words §2a writes are "connected", full stop — the citation claims support §2a does not give, and the sentence rev 21 points at is exactly the sentence that needed correcting. (The rev-17 narrative in the preamble at `:7` carries the same stale form: "a *connected* one that cannot act still expires `accounts.lane.switch_timed_out`".) Not a blocker because §2a states no predicate of its own and explicitly defers to §2l, and §2l/§3 row 9/§5/§6 all state the shipped rule consistently — but it is a wrong claim at a normative site and an unsupported self-citation, and it re-creates the exact conflation round 9 found. Fix: restate §2a:121 as "connected **and holding a lane-status subscription**", keep the phone as its example, and drop or reword §2l's "which is the scope §2a:121 already writes".
   *Disposition:* **RESOLVED in rev 22 (S9b review round 2).** §2a:121 now reads "connected **and holding a lane-status subscription**", names the `orca` CLI grant as the class the old clause was false for, and keeps the phone as its example; §2l's self-citation is re-worded to cite the corrected §2a rather than claim support the old one did not give; the rev-17 narrative in the preamble carries the same two conjuncts. The tree was already right — `lane-delegated-switch.ts` keys `assertDelegatedDesktopSubscribed` on `hasSubscriptionForGrant` — so this is a doc-only correction with no code change.

2. **§2l step 1's delegable-account list has no writer: no §3 wire row, no §2b envelope member, and its only budgeted owner lands in a later slice than the RPC that validates against it.**
   *Where:* `/home/ubuntu/orca-resilience-docs/docs/reference/agent-identity-s9-design.md:307` (§2l step 1) against `:172` (§2b envelope), `:390` (§3 row 9), `:478` (§6 S9b) and `:480` (§6 S9d).
   *Detail:* §2l step 1 requires that "the desktop publishes into its principal's lane state a **delegable account list**: per account the owner ticks, an opaque `delegatedAccountId` … plus the display fields §2b already permits", and step 2 has the host "validate the token against that principal's delegable list". Every other artefact in the document covers only the READ side of that list: §3 row 9 puts "the delegable-account list on the lane status stream" (host→client) and defines no method for the desktop→host write; §3's table is asserted to be the complete wire-change list. §2b closes the push envelope at exactly three members and refuses "an extra member" with `accounts.lane.push_malformed`, so the list cannot ride the push, and §2b states normatively that "The host never receives the owner's account **list**, ids of unselected accounts…". §6's S9b budgets `rpc/methods/lane-delegated-switch.ts` (~120) for "`accounts.lane.requestSwitch`, the delegable-account **projection**, the lease-holder liveness precondition … and the request timeout" — projection only — and §6's S9d budgets the desktop-side "delegable-account curation tick (~20)" in `AccountsPane.tsx`, with §6's own parallelism note stating "S9d depends on S9b". So on the shipped order S9b ships `requestSwitch` plus §5's fixtures for "a valid `delegatedAccountId`" and "an unknown or another principal's `delegatedAccountId` is refused" while no code path anywhere can make a `delegatedAccountId` exist: the host mints the tokens ("a host-side random token, minted with the lane") but is never told which accounts to mint them for. This is the same shape as round 8's major (a §2l obligation with no budget in any slice); rev 20 half-noticed it — §6:480 says the tick is "stated in rev 20 so §2l step 1 has an owner in some slice" — but assigned only the UI, not the wire method, the host-side store, its schema/bounds validation (the display fields are owner-authored strings crossing the wire, exactly what §2b bounds elsewhere), or a §5 write-side control. Fix: add a §3 row for the desktop→host delegable-list write with its own bounds validation, budget it in the slice that ships `requestSwitch`, and move the S9d tick's dependency accordingly (or state explicitly that `requestSwitch` refuses everything until S9d lands).
   *Disposition:* **RESOLVED in rev 22 (S9b review round 2), on the "add the rows" branch rather than the "hold the widening" one.** §3 gains **row 12** for `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` with its authorization, its bounds and its refusal codes; §6's S9b budgets `runtime/lane-delegation-directory.ts` for the host-side store the row needs, and states that S9d's curation tick depends on S9b (until the owner ticks, the list is empty and `requestSwitch` refuses every token by name). §2b's normative sentence is corrected: the host **does** now receive the owner's *ticked* list, bounded to an opaque token plus a `displayName` and an opted-in `email`. Because that is a privacy widening no owner decision covers — Q3 answered the peer-visible *label*, not the list — it ships flagged rather than silently: **§7 question 11** puts it to the owner with three options, including dropping `email` or holding §2l's phone switch. The code half (`setDelegableAccounts`, `parseDelegableAccountInputs`) already shipped in S9b sub-slice 3b.

3. **`lane-write-lost` is reported for a PARTIAL publish on win32 even when the lane holds a working credential (§10 item 19).**
   *Where:* `claude-accounts/lane-credential-writer.ts` (the published-path DACL verification arm).
   *Detail:* the code is fail-closed by choice — the host cannot vouch for that file's ACL after the rename — but the refusal's name asserts the lane received nothing, which on win32 is not always true. The docstring says so; the wire name does not.
   *Disposition:* **STILL OPEN — rev 25 checked, S9c shipped without touching it.** `lane-sync-driver.ts:125` and `lane-auth-state.ts:48`/`:228` still carry the single `'lane-write-lost'` status for both the darwin Keychain refusal and the win32 unverified-DACL case; §10(c)'s in-flight-probe fence and `laneWipePending` are a different mechanism and do not bear on this naming. Re-owed to whichever slice next touches `lane-credential-writer.ts`'s win32 arm — either split the code, or add the §2i sentence that states one name covers both. **Rev 32 note: still open, and its subject survives the re-basing.** `lane-credential-writer.ts`'s win32 published-path DACL arm is kept in full (§2m(2)) — only its callers change, from the push handler and the sync driver to the in-lane switch and the login capture — so the naming problem travels with it and the code renames with the verb (`accounts.lane.switch_write_locked`). The darwin Keychain half of the same single status is if anything more load-bearing after rev 32, because a lane login writes the **scoped** Keychain item itself (`keychain.ts:85-93`) **[code]**. Re-owed to whichever slice next touches that arm, which is S9-L1.

4. **L1's residency predicates have no production caller (S9b minor 5), and the consequence is written at both sites rather than fixed.**
   *Where:* `claude-accounts/managed-account-lane-residency.ts` / `runtime/rpc/methods/accounts.ts` (commit `f29d6e56db`).
   *Detail:* `assertNotLaneResident` is required from `accounts.selectClaude` **and** `accounts.removeClaude` for every caller class including the anonymous local one, and `assertAccountNotResidentElsewhere` from `accounts.lane.push`. All three call sites need the target account's residency keys derived from managed storage, which is 3b's push/accounts work. The docstrings now say L1 is unenforced, that the rotation gate rests on it, and that `findLaneResidency` never reports the shared lane — so a live shared-lane `claude` would not defer a lane's rotation. Unreachable today because no push RPC exists, so no lane can be loaded.
   *Disposition:* **RESOLVED in rev 29 (2026-08-24 release audit, §10(f)).** Not by adding the missing call sites — both `doSelectAccount` and `doRemoveAccount` (`claude-accounts/service.ts:415`, `:472`) already called `assertManagedClaudeAccountNotLaneResident` — but because the guard those calls delegate to was never armed: `attachManagedAccountResidencyGuard` is only ever invoked from inside `attachLaneWireService` (`lane-wire-service.ts:156`), and nothing in `src/main` called *that* in production either (§10(f) B1). The predicate was unreachable for the same reason every `accounts.lane.*` RPC refused `accounts.lane.not_enabled`: the whole lane wire was unattached, not just this one edge of it. `lane-wire-composition.ts`'s production composition (called from `principal-lane-host-wiring.ts:67` and, transitively, `index.ts`'s `whenReady` chain) arms `LaneWireService`, which arms the residency guard as a side effect of its own constructor. No `accounts.ts` code changed; the fix is entirely in what calls the code that was already there. **Rev 32 note: the predicates this item was about are now deleted rather than composed.** L1 is retired (§2e), so `assertManagedClaudeAccountNotLaneResident`, `assertAccountNotResidentElsewhere`, `account-residency-index.ts` and `managed-account-lane-residency.ts` go with it, and the two call sites this item chased — `claude-accounts/service.ts:415` and `:472` **[code]** — are stripped by S9-L3. What survives from the item is its *lesson*, not its subject: §10(f)'s composition-parity controls T1 and T2 are kept unchanged and S9-L1's new `attach`/`set…Dependencies` exports are covered by them from the day they land.

5. **The host now holds the owner's *ticked* account list, and it shipped ahead of the owner's answer (§10 item 24, §7 question 11).**
   *Where:* `runtime/lane-delegation-directory.ts` (`claudeLaneDelegationRows`).
   *Detail:* opaque token + owner-authored `displayName` + opted-in email; no credential and no store id. §2b's "the host never receives the owner's account list" was normative and is now corrected, but the widening is live before the decision that authorizes it.
   *Disposition:* **CLOSED IN REV 32 BY DELETION OF ITS SUBJECT.** The delegable-account list, `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` (§3 row 12), `runtime/lane-delegation-directory.ts` and `claudeLaneDelegationRows` are all deleted with the push model: under per-lane login the phone taps the **lane's own** account list, which the host has always held and publishes on `accounts.lane.status` (§2b, §2l, §3 row 2). The host therefore stops holding the owner's ticked account names and any opted-in email, §2b's original normative sentence — "the host never receives the owner's account list" — becomes true again, and §7's host-pivot question 10 needs no answer. Recorded as a closure rather than a resolution because no decision was taken: the widening un-shipped when its reason did.

## 9. Windows verification checklist

**Everything in §2–§7 and §10 is re-derived against the code; nothing in this section is.** **Rev 26 gate note:** S9d Parts 2–4 landed only pure-resolver unit tests this round — no renderer/DOM component test, no i18n copy — because no rendering component landed (§10(d)); this checklist's scope is unaffected by that gap, since none of Parts 2–4 reaches a rendered surface yet for a live box to exercise. **Rev 27 gate note:** the rev 26 gap closed — renderer/DOM component tests and i18n copy landed with the wiring, the IPC seam and the designation surface (§10(d)) — and the remaining unshipped piece, the delegable-account curation tick, has no renderer caller yet at all (§10(d)), so there is nothing for a live-box step to exercise — this checklist's scope is unaffected by rev 27 either. Gate scope this round did not extend to the status-bar presence roster (`RuntimePresenceStatusRows`): it is participant-keyed, not per-terminal, so it has no lane row to join and was never in scope for a per-terminal seam — not a gap this checklist tracks. S9a–S9d are unit- and integration-tested on Linux, but the §5 live steps that need a real Windows Server box — the shared host this design is built for (§2m) — have never run, in this sandbox or any prior rev, because no such box exists here. This is the complete, ordered list of what remains before deploy. Run it on the shared Windows Server box, as the owner, with both developers' grants paired. Each step names the exact command or gesture, its pass criterion, and the slice(s) whose claim it is the only proof of. Stop and do not deploy on a failure — the step's slice ships in the degraded/refused shape §4–§6 already describe for that failure, not silently.

**Rev 29 gate note.** The 2026-08-24 release audit (§10(f)) found that the lane wire this whole checklist exercises had no production caller at all — every step below would have failed at `accounts.lane.not_enabled` on a real box, not because any individual claim in §2–§6 was wrong, but because nothing wired the class those claims describe into a running host. That composition gap is closed; nothing below was re-run against a live box by this audit (§10(e) — no such box exists in this sandbox), so steps 1–16 still stand exactly as before, now against code that can actually reach them. Step 0 items 4 and 5 are updated for the Windows provisioning override (§10(f) B2) and the shipped desktop delegate action (§10(f) B3).

**Rev 32 gate note.** The credential *source* changed under this checklist (§2b–§2f): a lane is no longer loaded by a push from a desktop but by a login run inside the lane. **Step 0 item 8 and steps 3–7 are rewritten accordingly**, and two live steps are added that no prior revision had a reason to run — §5's **(L1)** coexistence step (the lane and the desktop signed into the *same* account, both working) and its **(L2)** refresh-independence step (both still refreshing after the first access token expires), the second of which is §6's **Gate 0** and blocks every slice. **Rev 33 corrects this note rather than leaving it standing:** steps 1, 2, 8–13, 15 and 16 are unchanged in intent, but **step 14 was a push step**, **step 0 item 6 named a retired verb and a retired refusal code**, and **step 0 item 5 said "delegate"** — all three are rewritten below, and step 0 item 8 gains the mutual-exclusion note §2d(iv) requires. Step 10's wipe-on-disconnect assertion inverts (**the credential must still be there** after a disconnect and after a restart, and must be gone only after an explicit logout, §2f). Nothing below has been run against a live box by this revision — no such box exists in this sandbox.

**Rev 28 gate note — step 0, day-one setup.** Nothing below can run until both people have a provisioned lane, and on a headless serve box that setup has no desktop to drive it from: the host-only `orca lane` CLI (§10 item 39) exists for exactly this, over the same local-socket-only consent RPCs the desktop's Seam A(2) bridge calls, refused to any remote caller. Run this once, as the owner, at the shared box's shell, before step 1:

**Rev 31 gate note — step 0 now starts with the invite, not a pre-paired device.** `orca lane bind` has always required a device that is *already paired*, and until rev 31 day-one setup was silent on how that pairing happens — the unstated assumption was a coalesced `--pair-name` grant from `orca serve`, one shared row two people's clients could both land on. `orca lane invite` (§10 item 39, `src/cli/specs/lane.ts`, `src/cli/handlers/lane.ts`) mints a named, per-person grant instead, over the same host-only consent RPC (`accounts.lane.mintInvite`) `bind`/`designate`/`provision` already use — so step 0 below sequences invite → redeem → bind, and **`--pair-name` at serve start is no longer part of day-one setup.**

**Rev 34 gate note — two things below changed, and one live arm is new.** Step 0 item 8's `--email` is **required** rather than bracketed, and that item now depends on step 6 having run for that person **on this path too**: the host-inline `orca lane login` takes binding (i-a), so a principal holding no designation is refused `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` at the host's own shell exactly as over the wire, and `orca lane designate` is the one-command remedy (§2d(iv), §2a). And step 10 gains a **second `kill -9`, run with a login in flight and past its capture write** — the arm that exercises §2f's startup account-store reconciliation against a real detached POSIX process group, which is round 2's blocker and the one claim in this document no unit test can reach.

**Step 0 — day-one setup, in order, one pass per person:**

1. `orca lane create-person --name "<owner>"` and `orca lane create-person --name "<other developer>"` — one person per human who will get a lane (§2a); `orca lane persons` lists who already exists.
2. `orca lane invite --person "<owner>" --scope runtime [--address <serve-public-address>]` (repeat for `"<other developer>"`) — mint that person's own pairing invite (`accounts.lane.mintInvite`) and hand them the printed link. `--scope` defaults to `runtime`, the only scope `bind`/`designate`/`provision` accept; `--address` defaults to the address this `orca serve` was started with. `--ttl <hours>` (1–24) can only shorten the invite's life below the 24h ceiling — it cannot extend it, and at most `MAX_LIVE_MINTED_GRANTS` = 16 of these invites can be pending at once across the host; minting past the cap evicts the oldest still-unredeemed one, so mint invites as you use them rather than batching a stack of them.
3. That person redeems the printed link in **their own desktop's Add environment dialog** (or, on a headless redeemer, `orca environment add --name <name> --pairing-code <link>`). Both doors refuse a mobile-scope link outright — *"This link grants mobile-only access. Generate a link for another Orca client."* — which is why step 2 asks for `--scope runtime`; `--scope mobile` is for pairing a phone, not this flow.
4. That person opens **one terminal** against the newly-added environment so the grant is actually redeemed — a minted invite is a pending, unbound device until first contact, and `orca lane bind` (next) has nothing to bind before that.
5. `orca lane bind --device <grant> --person "<owner>"` and `orca lane bind --device <grant> --person "<other developer>"` — bind each now-redeemed device to its person (repeat for a phone grant too, if that person will switch accounts or sign their lane in from one, §2l — rev 33: "delegate" named the deleted push); `--device` takes a device id, a unique id prefix, or the pairing label.
6. `orca lane designate --person "<owner>" --device <owner-desktop>` and `orca lane designate --person "<other developer>" --device <other-desktop>` — tick each person's device as *their* lane's **login device**: the one grant that may start a login for that lane and see the authorization URL (§2a, §2d(i)). Provisioning refuses `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` until this has run for that person. **A phone is a legitimate tick** — it displays a URL and takes a code and holds no secret either way (§2l). *(Rev 33: through rev 32 this step read "tick each person's desktop as their lane's pusher (§7 item 4)" and named `accounts.lane.no_pusher_designated`; the pusher, the push and that code are all deleted. §7's host-pivot question 4 records what the tick used to mean; §7 question 13 asks the owner to ratify what it means now.)*
7. `orca lane provision --person "<owner>"` and `orca lane provision --person "<other developer>"` — create each person's lane now that both have a bound, designated device. **On Windows (and macOS), this refuses `accounts.lane.provisioning_platform_gated` until the platform's §9 live probe below has passed (steps 12 and 13, for Windows) — add `--accept-unverified-platform` to provision ahead of that (§10(f) B2). The override is recorded, not silent:** it writes `platformAcceptance: 'unverified-win32'` onto that lane's `provision` row, which `orca lane audit` (below) shows back as `platform=unverified-win32`. Drop the flag, and re-provision is not required, the moment steps 12–13 have actually passed on this box — the gate and the override both exist to make "verified" and "operator overrode" two different, visible states, never the same one.
8. **Sign each lane in — `orca lane login --person "<owner>" --email <expected>`, run at the host's own shell. `--email` is required (rev 34): an inline start without it is refused before a session is minted, because an optional expectation makes I6 skippable on exactly the path this runbook makes mandatory.** This replaces rev 31's desktop-side "Delegate" action, which pushed a credential from the person's own desktop and no longer exists (§2b, §6's S9-L3). The verb starts a login session in that person's lane, prints the authorization URL, and waits for the code; complete the URL in any browser and paste the code back. On the host itself the CLI can run the flow inline, exactly as `orca account add` already does (`cli/handlers/account.ts:201-210`) **[code]**; from a paired desktop or phone the same flow runs over `accounts.lane.loginStart` / `loginSubmitCode`, and **only the person's designated grant may start it** (step 6 above is what makes that person's device the designated one). **And the inline path is not a way around the session (§2d(iv), new in rev 33):** it takes the same per-lane single-in-flight lock as the RPC path, so if a paired grant already has a login in flight for that lane the CLI refuses `accounts.lane.login_already_in_flight`, and a grant's `loginStart` is refused the same way against a CLI-started session — there is never a moment with two authorization URLs live for one lane and a human guessing which prompt belongs to which. *Pass:* `orca lane status` shows that lane `loaded`, with the account you named. If you authenticate as a different account than the one you named, it refuses `accounts.lane.login_identity_mismatch` and sweeps — that is I6 working, not a failure of setup; and if `auth status --json` reports no identity at all it refuses the same way, because unverifiable fails closed (§2b, rev 34). **This step depends on step 6 having run for that person, on this path too (rev 34):** the inline login takes binding (i-a), so a principal holding no designation is refused `accounts.lane.no_login_device_designated` here exactly as over the wire, and the remedy is `orca lane designate --person <p> --device <d>` at this same shell (§2d(iv)).

`orca lane status` (add `--person` to scope it, `--json` for scripting) confirms both lanes before step 1 below; `orca lane audit` shows the consent trail steps 1–7 just wrote.

1. **Fresh lane, first terminal (S9a).** Provision a fresh lane for one developer's grant and open its first terminal. *Pass:* the terminal reaches a usable prompt with at most the expected per-lane trust prompt, and `echo $CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` prints the lane's path, not `~/.claude` or `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`. *Proves:* §2a lane provisioning and the launch-time `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` anchor actually reach a live Windows process.
2. **Plain-shell case (S9a, the §2a blocker).** Open a **second** lane pane with no command at all. *Pass:* `echo $CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` already shows the lane path before anything is typed; then type `claude` and confirm it starts in the lane's account. *Proves:* the command-independence decoupling — a plain shell is lane-scoped, not only a `claude` launch — which is invisible to any agent-launch-only probe and was the round-3 blocker.
3. **Baseline `/status`.** In the terminal from step 1, run `/status` and note the *Login method* / account row. *Pass:* it names the account the lane was signed into. *Setup only* — no slice claim on its own.
4. **Log the lane into a second account (S9-L1).** From the person's **designated** device, start a second lane login naming a different account, complete it in a browser, and paste the code back. *Pass:* the lane now lists **two** accounts and `laneState` stays `loaded` throughout; the authorization URL appeared only on the device that asked for it, and on no other grant's status stream. *Proves:* the login-session mechanism reaches a live process on this host, and §2d's URL-delivery binding holds on real transports rather than in a fixture.
5. **Switch in the lane, and re-run `/status` in the SAME session (R2).** Without restarting the terminal from step 1, switch the lane to the second login (a local file rewrite — no browser) and run `/status` again. *Pass:* the account row changed to the second account with no restart. *Fail path:* if it did not change, this is not a step failure — it is the result that selects R2's degraded shape: `laneState` must read `'restart-required'` end to end (presence row, AccountsPane hedge) rather than silently claiming `'loaded'`. Either outcome is recorded; record which one happened.
6. **Switch back, and confirm the second switch also applies.** Switch the lane to the **first** login and repeat step 5. *Pass:* it also applies (or degrades consistently with step 5's outcome) — it must not silently no-op. There is no watermark left to go stale (§2c), but a no-op here is the same *symptom* rev 5's watermark bug produced and is checked for that reason.
7. **File-level identity (S9-L1).** Run `claude auth status --json` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to the lane's path. *Pass:* it reports the account from step 6, confirming file-level identity independent of any running session's in-memory state. Then repeat against the **other** lane on this box: it must report **its own** account, and deleting one lane's `.credentials.json` must make that lane report **no** account rather than the other lane's — the promoted form of step 13 below, and the highest-severity check in this list (§5).
8. **The other developer's terminal is unchanged (S9a, R3).** Check the other developer's already-open terminal the same way (steps 3/5). *Pass:* its account is unaffected by every switch above.
9. **Four launch surfaces carry the lane (S9a funnel + Q1 phone half).** Start an agent through, in order: `terminal.createAgentSession`; `orchestration.workerStart` with `worktree: 'current'`; `orchestration.workerStart` with `worktree: 'new-child'`; and `session.tabs.createTerminal` **from the phone, with the desktop window open and focused**. *Pass:* all four report the lane's account, including the phone-triggered one — this is the round-8 blocker's only live proof, since it exercises the desktop-and-phone-share-one-lane path no unit test can reach.
10. **Residency across a disconnect and a crash, then a logout (S9c + rev 32 — this step *inverts* against rev 31).** Disconnect the lane's last grant and `ls -l` the lane directory. *Pass:* `.credentials.json` is **still there** and the lane still reads `loaded` — the disconnect wipe is deleted (§2f). Reconnect, then `kill -9` the Orca runtime process while the lane is loaded, restart it, and `ls -l` again. *Pass:* **still there**, the lane still `loaded`, and any `*.tmp` sibling **gone** — startup sweeps temp artifacts and, as of rev 34, reconciles the lane's account store, which leaves every **indexed** login exactly where it was. Finally run `orca lane logout --person "<owner>"` and `ls -l` once more. *Pass:* `.credentials.json` and every `*.tmp` sibling gone, **`<lane>/claude-accounts` holding no `auth` directory** — run step 4 first so the lane holds a *second* login and this assertion is not vacuous — `laneState: 'absent'`, and a launch into the lane fails closed. *(Rev 33: through rev 32 this step certified only the active file, which a lane holding N logins passes while N−1 complete grants remain at rest, §2f.)* *Proves:* §2f's new trigger set on a real OS process tree, in both directions — that the two deleted triggers really are deleted, and that the surviving one really still sweeps — which no unit test can prove for a `kill -9`. **Rev 34 adds the arm that covers the *login* writer, because the crash above is run with no login in flight and therefore never reaches it.** Repeat the `kill -9` with a login **in flight and past its capture write**: start `orca lane login`, complete the browser round-trip, paste the code, and kill the runtime in the same second. Restart, then `ls -l <lane>/claude-accounts`. *Pass:* the half-owned `<laneAccountId>` directory is **gone** after startup and `orca lane accounts --person "<owner>"` never lists it; and if the detached login child survived the kill and re-wrote, the lane reads `absent` with `laneWipePending: true` rather than clean. *Proves:* §2f's startup account-store reconciliation against a real detached POSIX process group — round 2's blocker, on the one path a unit test cannot reach.
11. **OpenClaude, the open question (informational, §2a).** Run `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<lane-path> openclaude` on the pinned build and note which credential store it reads. *Not gating* — record the result. If it honors the variable, §2a's OpenClaude refusal can relax to an allowlist line in a later rev; until this runs, the refusal stands regardless of the answer.
12. **DACL read-back (S9a, §2m(1) gate).** `Get-Acl` the lane directory from step 1. *Pass:* `AreAccessRulesProtected` is `true`, and the ACL holds exactly three entries at FullControl — the current user, `S-1-5-18`, `S-1-5-32-544` — with no deny ACE. *Fail path:* Windows lane provisioning stays off (§6 gate ii) until this passes.
13. **Credential-store isolation (S9a/S9b, the §2m(3) gate).** Provision two lanes, log in to a **different** account in each, delete one lane's `.credentials.json`, then run `claude auth status --json` against that lane. *Pass:* it reports **no** account. *Fail path:* if it reports the other lane's account, the Windows CLI keeps credential state outside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and Windows lane provisioning must stay off (§6 gate ii) — this is the single highest-severity failure mode in the design (every lane silently collapsing onto one credential) and is checked here, not inferred.
14. **Rename under lock (S9-L1 Windows write path — rewritten in rev 33; the push this step named is deleted).** With a live `claude` in a lane holding `.credentials.json` open, **switch that lane to another of its own logins** (`orca lane use --person "<owner>" --account <id>`), then repeat with a **login capture** by completing an `orca lane login` into the same lane. *Pass:* both writes succeed inside `renameFileWithWindowsRetry`'s window rather than throwing a raw `EBUSY`; a target held open **past** the window refuses `accounts.lane.switch_write_locked` with its complete human sentence, never an untyped error (§2m(2), §3's matrix and its Rule-3 row).
15. **Containment (S9a, §2m(4)).** Attempt `orca account add --config-dir` against a lane root through, in turn: an 8.3 short-name form; a drive-letter case flip; a `\\?\` extended-path form; and a planted NTFS junction into the lane. *Pass:* all four are refused.
16. **Three shells (S9a, §2m(8)).** Repeat step 2 (the plain-shell case) three times: once under **PowerShell**, once under **Git Bash**, once under `cmd` — the resolver's middle fallback — and drive **at least one** of the three through a per-tab shell override rather than the host's default-shell setting (the path every restored lane pane actually resolves its shell through). *Pass, each time:* `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (or `%CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR%`) reads as the lane path **from inside the pane, after the shell's own profile has run** — a `$PROFILE` or `.bashrc` that rewrites it must fail this step, not pass silently.

## 10. What shipped — S9a, S9b, S9c and S9d implementation deviations, plus rev 32's credential-source re-basing (rev 32)

**This section is the reconciliation.** Every item is a place where the *implementation* departed from §2–§6 as written, recorded as a decision with the reason it was taken. Nothing here is a proposal: it is all in the tree at the `feat/agent-identity-s9` tip. Where the code is right and this document was wrong, the sentence above has been corrected in place and the item says so. **Rev 25 adds (c) and (d)** — S9c and S9d, both now shipped — and extends the gate-skip retrospective, formerly (c) and now (e), with what those two slices' gates skipped. **Rev 26 extends (d)** with S9d Parts 2–4's pure-resolver layer (the restart-hedge classifier and the consent-surface resolvers) and restates, item by item, what of Parts 2–4 is still unbuilt. **Rev 28 adds item 39 below**, recording the headless-host `orca lane` CLI that drives the same host-only consent RPCs item 39 names, renumbers a gap in §7's list, and gives §9's preamble a day-one setup step built from it; no §2–§6 behavior changed. **Rev 29 adds (f)**, the 2026-08-24 release audit that found the entire S9c/S9d lane wire — and, with it, §8 item 4's residency predicates — sitting unattached in production despite being fully implemented and fully tested in isolation; it records the three blockers and one major the audit found, the two composition-parity controls it added so the same failure shape cannot recur silently, and the Windows/macOS provisioning override it shipped alongside the fix. §9 step 0 and `docs/reference/windows-desktop-build.md` are updated to match.
**Rev 30** records two operational incidents found and fixed after the S9c/S9d
lane wire went live in production, documented in full in their own reference
docs rather than reconciled line-by-line against §2–§6 here (neither changed
any lane-wire behavior this section covers): `terminal-reattach-stability.md`
(the 2026-08-25 mouse-report-spam/duplicate-tab/non-converging-close-all/
dead-tab-remint incident, fixed on `feat/terminal-reattach-stability` and
shipped as Artifact 5) and `sidebar-ghost-agent-rows.md` (the 2026-08-27
"3 agents" sidebar symptom from unretired agent-hook rows across a resumed
session, FIX 1/2/5 shipped, FIX 3/4 deferred). As of 2026-08-25 18:27 UTC the
VPS runs Artifact 5 (sha256 `83019f4dbbddeaa4b6c95feddc77d1530c2a7f77a6c70042c8d5391d310a99b8`,
integration tip `a7f23391e1`), installed via `orca-install-v3.sh`; a Clacy
named invite (`--pair-name Clacy`) is minted and pending pairing with the
owner's desktop.
**Rev 31** records `orca lane invite` (`accounts.lane.mintInvite`, `src/cli/specs/lane.ts`,
`src/cli/handlers/lane.ts`, `src/main/runtime/rpc/methods/principal-lanes.ts`) replacing the
coalesced `--pair-name` grant as day-one setup's pairing step (§9 step 0, and
`docs/reference/windows-desktop-build.md`'s serve-box block, both updated to match); no §2–§6
lane-wire behavior changed. Artifact 8 is the reference read for this rev: integration tip
`7d7edad969`, sha `161fe960…`.

### (a) S9a — env/arg hardening, capture containment, lanes, the funnel

1. **The pane lane is a discriminated union with `kind: 'principal' | 'shared'`, not a nullable `principalId`.** Reason: every consumer had to decide what "no lane" meant, and a union makes the shared lane a value the compiler forces each site to handle instead of a `null` that silently defaults.
2. **The `inherit` option carries the *caller*, not just the parent pane.** Reason: §2a's ownership predicate needs the principal doing the inheriting to refuse a split of someone else's pane; a parent-only option cannot express the refusal.
3. **Grant→principal bindings persist in the principal registry's own store (`runtime/principal-registry-store.ts:1-113`), and `DeviceEntry` is untouched.** Reason: §3 row 8 put `principalId` on `DeviceEntry`; a separate store keeps the device registry's corrupt-load path (`loadSucceeded`) and its orphan reconciliation independent of the binding table, which §2a requires to fail closed separately. **§3 row 8 is therefore wrong as written and is superseded by this item.**
4. **A WSL selection target on a lane-pinned launch is REFUSED, `terminal.lane_wsl_unsupported`.** Reason: §2a fell through to today's WSL branches; a fall-through would run a lane-pinned pane on an account-scoped, not lane-scoped, credential, which is the exact confusion the lane exists to prevent. S9e (§2n) is what lifts it.
5. **A lane-less caller splitting an *unattributed* pane resolves to the shared lane rather than being refused; a lane holder splitting one is refused.** Reason: refusing both would break every pre-existing pane on upgrade, and the asymmetry is safe — the shared lane is what an unattributed pane already ran in.
6. **A renderer-notified split fails closed, `terminal.lane_renderer_split_unsupported`.** Reason: the renderer path has no authenticated caller to derive a lane from, and §2a forbids taking lane identity from renderer-supplied input.
7. **The coordinator loop passes an explicit shared lane rather than omitting the argument.** Reason: `terminal.lane_unspecified` is fail-closed, so an omitted lane throws; the coordinator is host-internal and genuinely shared, and saying so is cheaper than a second nullable path.
8. **Anonymous and renderer-minted panes are bound to the shared lane at `registerPty`, not left unbound.** Reason: an unbound pane is indistinguishable from a lane pane whose registry row was lost, and the fail-closed lookup would then refuse legitimate local panes.
9. **The lane row lives in a `paneKey`-keyed map *beside* the binding row rather than inside it.** Reason: the binding row is persisted and the lane row is not always resolvable at persistence time; keeping them apart lets the lane be re-derived on load without a schema migration.
10. **`laneScopedAgentLaunchInputs` (`runtime/lane-launch-computation.ts:134-160`) is applied at six builders, not the seven `settings.agentDefaultArgs`/`agentDefaultEnv` consumption sites §2 row 13/14 enumerates** (`runtime/orca-runtime.ts:22321`, `:22399`, `:26139`, `:26290`, `:26463`, `:27647`). Reason: two of the seven old sites had collapsed into one builder by the time the change landed; the *set of launches* covered is unchanged.
11. **`runtime/lane-permission-narrowing.ts` (61) exists as its own module and preserves the host-wide narrowing.** Reason: §2a scoped permission narrowing to lanes; removing the host-wide arm would have silently widened every non-lane launch, so both arms ship and the module says which is which.
12. **Six refusal codes beyond §3's table**: `terminal.lane_remote_pane`, `terminal.lane_not_loaded` (two sentences, because the remedy differs by whether a lane exists at all), `accounts.lane.lane_path_not_contained`, `accounts.lane.lane_root_not_local`, `accounts.lane.provisioning_platform_gated` and `accounts.lane.grant_not_per_person`. Reason: each names a refusal §2 describes in prose but never gave a code, and an untyped throw reaches the person as nothing at all. §3's degradation row now carries the complete enumeration.
13. **`claude-accounts/managed-auth-path.ts` adopted the shared containment helper** (`shared/lane-path-containment.ts`, used at `managed-auth-path.ts:30-42`). Reason: §2m(4) required case-folded `realpathSync.native` containment for lanes; running two different containment rules in one tree is how the 8.3-short-name hole comes back.
14. **`agentArgs` is sanitized at `createAgentSession` *and* `resumeAgentSession`, not only at the anchor.** Reason: §2 row 8 put the allowlist at the anchor, but the two agent-session RPCs build their args before the anchor can see them (`lane-launch-computation.ts:148-160` says so in code).
15. **The spawn anchor is `spawnWithLane` in its own module (`ipc/lane-pinned-spawn.ts:21-27`), and `attachStablePaneOwner`'s reattach at `ipc/pty.ts:844` is deliberately NOT routed through it.** Reason: the reattach passes `attachOnly: true` with no command and then proves it reattached the same incarnation, so it creates nothing a lane computation could govern — and computing a lane over its bare `{ cols, rows, cwd }` would refuse a legitimate reattach whose lane happens to be unloaded. §2a's "all three `provider.spawn` calls" is corrected in place.

### (b) S9b — push, scoping, account-keyed refresh, attribution

16. **The reauth hold is a PERSISTED field on the watermark row (`ClaudeLaneCredentialWatermark.reauthRequired?: boolean`, additive, normalized from a literal `true` only), not the in-memory Set §2c/§2e imply.** Reason: the doc's watermark shape is secretless-by-design and so is this field, and the blocker only closes if the hold outlives both the next sync *and* a restart — an in-memory hold evaporates and writer 1 walks the watermark back onto the spent sha on the first post-restart launch. Deliberate side effect: it also closes round 1's concern-1 restart residual.
17. **Writer 1 no-ops while the lane is held for reauth.** §2c names writer 1 as recording what `syncLane` observed on all four triggers with no exception. Reason: without the exception the hold is undone by the very next sync — the reproduced blocker; only writer 2 (a push) or writer 3 (a rotation the lane received) lifts it.
18. **The sync driver also skips the rotation when the lane is held AND its file has not moved since the last observation.** §2c rotates on triggers 1–3 unconditionally. Reason: that file holds exactly the copy the last rotation spent, so rotating buys a network round trip and a refusal per launch with no path out. A moved file still rotates, so a real CLI rotation is not blocked.
19. **`lane-write-lost` keeps its name and its fail-closed direction for a PARTIAL publish** (darwin Keychain refusal after the file landed; win32 published-path DACL that will not verify after the rename) even though the lane may then hold a working credential. Reason: §2i's Keychain half genuinely makes the lane unusable on darwin; the win32 half is over-conservative on purpose, because the host cannot vouch for that file's ACL. The docstring now says exactly that instead of asserting the lane received nothing — and it is carried forward as a named residual in §8.
20. **The wipe is async, and `PrincipalLaneConsentService.deprovisionLane` and the `accounts.lane.deprovision` handler became async with it.** Reason: §2f/§2i's wipe now reaches the Keychain. No wire shape changed.
21. **The wipe takes the Keychain BEFORE the files** (§2f lists it after the file sweep). Reason: on darwin the Keychain item is what the CLI actually resolves, so removing it first is the fail-closed order; a throw from it stops the wipe being reported done either way.
22. **Round 2's mutation proof 10 is retracted as unreproducible** and replaced by `lane-keychain-scope.test.ts`. Reason: the darwin arm injects `writeKeychain`, so the production default was never executed by any test and the runtime-pair mutation left both files green; the replacement drives the production defaults over a mocked `security` and the mutation was re-run against it.
23. **The wire surface exceeds §3's table by three methods — `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts`, `accounts.lane.statusSubscribe`, `accounts.lane.statusUnsubscribe`** — resolved on the "add the rows" branch: §3 row 12 and an amended row 2 landed in revs 22–23. Reason: the delegable list had no writer at all (§8 item 2) and the subscribe/unsubscribe pair was left implied by "the lane status stream".
24. **§2b's normative "the host never receives the owner's account list" is FALSE in the shipped code.** The ticked delegable list persists host-side in `claudeLaneDelegationRows` (opaque token + owner-authored `displayName` + opted-in email; no credential, no store id). Reason: §2l's phone flow cannot exist without something for the phone to tap. The sentence is corrected and §7 question 11 asks the owner to accept the widening — **the widening ships ahead of that decision**, which is itself a residual.
25. **`accounts.lane.switch_lane_cleared` is one refusal ahead of the §3 table that asserts completeness** (added in rev 23). Reason: an outstanding switch against a lane that was cleared underneath it had no name, and the shared enumeration lets old clients render the host's sentence verbatim.
26. **§2d's peer row enumeration is now exactly closed** — `scope`/`occupied`/`ownerLabel`/`displayName`; `laneState` was dropped from the peer branch. Reason: the one remaining declared peer-visible widening is `displayName`, which §2b/§2k already authorize under Q3.
27. **`ClaudeLaneProjectionRow.laneState` is now optional (self rows only) rather than required.** Reason: a type-level consequence of item 26, not described in the doc.
28. **`LaneStatusStream` takes a `principalOf` lookup in its constructor** (`runtime/lane-status-stream.ts:1-110`); it was stateless in the doc's framing. Reason: a subscriber must be filed by grant and re-resolved per delivery.
29. **`lane-push-envelope.ts` applies BOTH a zod `.max()` (characters) and the UTF-8 byte check.** Reason: §2b's normative bound remains the byte check; the schema bound only moves the rejection earlier.
30. **Shared-lane attribution is NARROWER than §2k's plain "paneKey wins"** (`rate-limits/claude-usage-attribution.ts:105-125`). A shared-lane pane's post is attributed to the shared row only when the posted `configDir` also matches `sharedConfigDir`, and never falls through to a lane row; lane panes keep paneKey-wins unchanged. Reason: `state.claude` is the host-wide, peer-published bar, and a lane is single-account by construction while the shared lane is not — `rememberSharedLane` is rewritten per fetch target (`rate-limits/claude-usage-attribution.ts:68-71`), so the widening let a pane running under another target's dir write the host-wide bar, a post the pre-S9b `!==` dropped.
31. **New refusal `accounts.lane.clear_incomplete`, with a complete two-sentence message.** Reason: §2f requires "the sweep retried rather than reported done" but names no code, and `accounts.lane.clear` needs to fail closed instead of returning `{ cleared }`.
32. **Rev 25 — the other half of §2f's wipe fence landed with S9c and closes this item.** `claude-accounts/principal-lane-lifecycle.ts` runs mark-wipe-pending → abort every in-flight usage probe and await its `claude` dying → sweep → re-read → clear the mark, and nothing reports the wipe done before that clean read-back (`:9-19`, `completed` on `LaneWipeOutcome` at `:39`, `probeDeathTimeoutMs`/`WIPE_ATTEMPTS` retry at `:70`/`:76-77`); `laneWipePending` is a real published-and-cleared flag (`lane-wipe-pending.ts`, `markLaneWipePending`/`clearLaneWipePending`/`releaseUnconfirmedLaneWipe`, imported at `:3-7`). This item — rev 24's kill-only description — is superseded.
33. **Rev 25 — `isSwitchInProgress` is now lane-keyed and closes this item.** `claude-accounts/lane-credential-coordinator.ts:76` reads `isClaudeAuthSwitchInProgress(laneId)`, and `beginClaudeAuthSwitch`/`endClaudeAuthSwitch`/`isClaudeAuthSwitchInProgress` (`live-pty-gate.ts:205`/`:212`/`:216`) all take a `laneId` — §2f's lane scoping, previously host-global, shipped with S9c. This item — rev 24's host-global description — is superseded.
34. **`setLaneAccountRowResolvers` accepts `null` for either resolver as an explicit UNSET** (previously `?:` only). Host-local type widening; no wire surface.
35. **Major 3's documentation half was fixed OUTSIDE the pinned worktree**, in `/home/ubuntu/orca-resilience-docs` (revs 22–23). Reason: the task pinned work to the code tree, but the finding's file was this specification and §8 dispositioned both items to that review, so the doc could not be closed from the worktree.

### (c) S9c — lifecycle + per-lane gate

**No implementation deviations from §2f/§6 as written.** `claude-accounts/principal-lane-lifecycle.ts` (269 lines), `claude-accounts/principal-lane-startup-wipe.ts` (73) and `runtime/principal-lane-connection-lifecycle.ts` (116) ship the close/idle/startup/revoke wipes, the `laneWipePending` publish/clear pair and the in-flight-probe fence exactly as §6's S9c bullet and §5's mutation proofs describe them; `shared/claude-live-pty-session-lane.ts` (51) carries the lane beside each `claudeLivePtySessionIds` entry per §2h. **This closes items 32 and 33 above**, both of which named the pre-S9c state and are corrected in place.

### (d) S9d — desktop UI + handoff

**Shipped, as of rev 27: the backend and, with one exception, the renderer half too.** `terminal.openInMyLane` (`runtime/terminal-open-in-lane.ts`, 51 lines: `assertLaneSeedPromptWithinBounds`, `callerMayOpenSourceLane`), wired into the runtime at `openTerminalInMyLane` (`orca-runtime.ts:26648-26696`) and exposed as the RPC method `terminal.openInMyLane` (`rpc/methods/terminal.ts:1582`, schema `TerminalOpenInMyLane` at `:1049-1057`); `terminal-presence-registry.ts:276` (`grantsAttachedTo`) as §2h's join anchor. **The renderer half — the designation surface, the chip/badge wiring and the consent rows — is now built** (rev 27, below); **the one piece still not built is the delegable-account curation tick.**

**Rev 26 — Parts 2–4's pure-resolver layer shipped; nothing that renders, wires, or bridges IPC shipped with it.** Components/files:

- **Part 2 — the restart-hedge classifier.** `renderer/src/components/settings/claude-account-switch-restart-hedge.ts` (54): `resolveClaudeAccountSwitchRestartHedge` over `ClaudeAccountSwitchLiveTerminal[]`, returning `'none' | 'required'` per §2h's R2 rule — a lane re-resolves live and needs no restart, except a `'restart-required'` lane or a shared/host terminal, both of which still do.
- **Part 3 — the attribution/open-in-my-lane predicates (from the prior round, corrected this round).** `renderer/src/components/terminal-pane/terminal-credential-lane-attribution.ts` (65, `resolveTerminalCredentialLaneAttribution`) and `terminal-open-in-lane-action.ts` (41, `viewerOwnsCredentialLane` + `shouldOfferOpenInMyLane`) — corrected in this round to read the `terminal.list` presence participant rather than the per-pane stream row (deviation below).
- **Part 4 — the consent-surface resolvers.** `renderer/src/components/settings/principal-consent-surface-rows.ts` (83, `resolveConsentDeviceRows`: joins paired grants against the principal registry into one row per device, with `canBind`/`canDesignate`/`isDesignatedPusher`) and `principal-consent-audit-rows.ts` (65, `describeConsentAuditRow`: resolves one audit row to display labels, falling back to a short id prefix when the principal or device it names is since-deleted) — both consumed only by their own unit tests, over the pre-existing, unmodified `principal-consent-authority.ts` (29, S9a) and its `authorizeHostConsent` locality gate, which `principal-consent-authority.test.ts` (22, new this round) now also asserts directly.

**Rev 27 — the renderer wiring, the IPC seam and the designation surface all landed; only the delegable-account curation tick is still budgeted.** Components/files, by the seam each fills:

- **Seam A(1), the per-pane feed Part 3 lacked.** `renderer/src/lib/pane-manager/terminal-credential-lane-state.ts` (164): a module-`Map` keyed by `ptyId` holding `credentialLane`/`laneState`/`laneAccountLabel`/`laneUsage`/`credentialLaneOwner`, filled and stale-row-reconciled per scope by `applyTerminalCredentialLaneRows(scopeKey, rows)` and `clearCredentialLaneScope`, with a `useSyncExternalStore` selector `useTerminalCredentialLane(ptyId)` defaulting to the frozen `UNATTRIBUTED_CREDENTIAL_LANE` (`'unknown'`) row. `terminal-pane/terminal-credential-lane-feed.ts` (97) and `use-terminal-credential-lane-feed.ts` (11) are the `terminal.list` producer that calls it.
- **Seam A(1b)/A(3), this desktop's own lane-status feed.** `shared/principal-lane-status-ipc.ts` (38, `PrincipalLaneStatusRow`/`PrincipalLaneStatusSnapshot` with `lanes` + `delegationLeases`) is read host-only by `main/ipc/principal-lane-status-bridge.ts` (157, sender-gated get; a module-level broadcaster with exported `notifyPrincipalLaneStatusChanged()` for the changed republish) via `resolveLaneResidencyState` + `getLaneDelegationLeaseStore().list()`, exposed through `preload/api/principal-lane-status-api.ts` (23) and consumed by `renderer/src/components/settings/principal-lane-status-store.ts` (93, `usePrincipalLaneStatus()` / `getPrincipalLaneStatus` / `getDelegationLeases`) and `principal-lane-status-subscription.ts` (35, hydrates from `get` then stays fresh through `onChanged`, with a republish beating the initial read winning the race).
- **Seam A(2), the host-only consent bridge.** `shared/principal-consent-ipc.ts` (74, `ConsentPrincipalRow`/`ConsentBindingRow`/`ConsentAuditRow`, `PrincipalConsentSnapshot`, write request/result types) is written host-only by `main/ipc/principal-consent-bridge.ts` (236): sender-gated on `event.sender === mainWindow.webContents` (the `terminal-presence.ts` precedent) with `authorizeHostConsent({})` constructed only after that check for create/bind/unbind/rebind/designate/provision/deprovision, a snapshot read that returns empty to a foreign sender, a republish on every write, and `notifyPrincipalLaneStatusChanged()` fired after provision/deprovision so Seam A(1b) picks it up. `preload/api/principal-consent-api.ts` (32) exposes it.
