The hedge itself is no longer unconditional: `claude-account-switch-live-terminals.ts` (51) reduces a point-in-time `terminal.list` read — the desktop still has no single central feed for this call site, only the per-pane Seam A(1) one above — into Part 2's `{onLane, laneState}` input, defaulting to `'required'` on a read failure.
- **Tests and i18n, closing both gaps this section named below.** `TerminalPresenceLaneChip.test.tsx`, `TerminalOpenInMyLaneAction.test.tsx`, `AccountLaneStatusSection.test.tsx`, `PrincipalConsentSurface.test.tsx`, `terminal-tab-presence-badge.test.tsx`, `terminal-credential-lane-state.test.ts`, `principal-consent-bridge.test.ts`, `principal-lane-status-bridge.test.ts`, `principal-lane-status-store.test.ts` and others; new `en.json` keys for every new string (the consent surface's bind/designate/provision copy, the lane-status section's residency and mis-designation copy, the tab badge's owner label).

**Still budgeted, not shipped: the delegable-account curation tick** (§7 question 7's owner-curated per-account allowlist for the phone). No renderer file references `setDelegableAccounts`; the main-side write (§6 S9b, row 12) has no renderer caller. See §6's rev-27 note on the same bullet.

**Deviations from this report:**

- **The restart-required lane state is modelled locally, not on the shipped wire type.** `ClaudeAccountSwitchLaneState` (`claude-account-switch-restart-hedge.ts:11-15`) is `'loaded' | 'absent' | 'reauth-required' | 'restart-required'` — the shipped `RuntimeTerminalLaneState` (`shared/runtime-types.ts:510`) carries only the first three. `'restart-required'` is §10(e)'s not-yet-wired seam value (the §4 live-probe fallback); the classifier is written to be correct the moment that value ships, and the wire type itself was not widened — that is main-process/wire work, out of this round's renderer scope.
- **`viewerOwnsCredentialLane` reads the `terminal.list` presence participant, not the per-pane stream participant.** It takes `RuntimeTerminalPresenceParticipant[]` (`shared/runtime-types.ts:519`, carrying `credentialLaneOwner` from the projection's owner-label join) rather than `RuntimeTerminalStreamPresenceParticipant` (`:345`), which has no such field. This matches §2h — the owner label is a `terminal.list` projection join — and was a same-round fix to Part 3's earlier landing (commit `3b65efca49`), which had originally reached for the stream row.

**What did not land — updated for rev 27; every item rev 26 listed here is now shipped except the one below:**

- **The delegable-account curation tick.** §7 question 7's owner-curated "let my phone switch to this one" allowlist has no renderer surface: nothing calls `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` from `AccountsPane.tsx` or anywhere else in the renderer. This is the one piece of §6's S9d bullet still budgeted rather than shipped.

36. **Refusal codes: §2g/§5 name the behaviors to refuse, not the codes.** Shipped: `terminal.lane_open_no_lane` (a caller with no lane), `terminal.lane_open_forbidden` (attached-but-wrong-principal, or not attached at all), `terminal.lane_seed_too_long`, `terminal.lane_seed_control_char` (`shared/claude-lane-refusals.ts:56-59`); an unknown source pty reuses the existing `terminal.lane_source_unknown` rather than minting a fifth. Reason: an untyped throw reaches the person as nothing at all — the same gap §10 item 12 closed for S9a/S9b, now closed for S9d.
37. **The wire schema caps `seedPrompt` at 64KB (`z.string().max(64*1024)`, `rpc/methods/terminal.ts:1051-1054`) BEFORE the runtime's authoritative 4096-byte + control-character check runs** (`assertLaneSeedPromptWithinBounds`, `terminal-open-in-lane.ts:12-31`, `MAX_LANE_SEED_PROMPT_BYTES = 4096` at `:5`). Reason, stated in the code's own comment at `:1047-1048`: the wire cap only stops a hostile client forcing the host to buffer megabytes before the byte check runs; §2g's real bound is enforced afterward and unchanged. Additive and Rule-1 wire-compatible — a new optional field on a new method.
38. **Seed delivery does NOT auto-submit.** No trailing `\r` is appended (`orca-runtime.ts:26691-26693`): the prompt lands on the shell's input line for the person to read and submit, matching §2g's "delivered as terminal input" — the spec does not mandate submission, and a literal `\r` in the seed itself is rejected as a control character by item 36's `terminal.lane_seed_control_char`.
39. **Rev 28 — a headless-host CLI ships beside the desktop consent surface, driving the same RPCs.** `orca lane {persons, create-person, bind, unbind, rebind, designate, provision, deprovision, bind-link, status, audit}` (`src/cli/specs/lane.ts`, `src/cli/handlers/lane.ts`, `src/cli/lane-format.ts`) is the shell-only counterpart to Seam A(2)'s `main/ipc/principal-consent-bridge.ts`: it drives the same host-only consent RPCs the desktop's `PrincipalConsentSurface.tsx` drives, over the local socket, refused to any remote caller — `--environment` and `--pairing-code` are rejected outright rather than silently ignored, because this only ever runs on the host itself. `bind-link` covers §7 item 8's federated link→principal binding from the shell. Reason: a serve box provisioned headless (§9's target Windows Server machine) has no desktop to drive `create-person`/`bind`/`designate`/`provision` from, and the CLI is the only route to those four writes plus `deprovision`, `unbind`, `rebind`, `status` and `audit` on such a box. **Not covered:** the delegable-account curation tick (`accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts`, §7 item 7) has no CLI verb, matching §10(d)'s renderer gap — that one write stays desktop-only either way. §9's day-one setup step 0 sequences these verbs in the order a fresh box needs them.

### (e) What the gate skipped, and what is therefore unobserved

- **`typecheck:web` was skipped in every S9b round** under the memory rule (free memory 5518–6097 MB against a 7000 MB floor). No web or renderer file is touched by the credential-lane slices, so the exposure is narrow, but it is unrun.
- **No `describe.runIf(process.platform === 'win32')` test was added for the win32 half of the partial-publish fix.** It is exercised through the injected `WindowsLanePublishOps` on Linux; the real ACL script remains unobserved here. The win32 lane-key-collapse arm in `lane-usage-probe-env.test.ts` is likewise unobservable on this box.
- **The §5 macOS live probe (step 8) is unrun** — no Mac in this sandbox — so darwin lane provisioning stays gated off at `runtime/principal-lane-consent-service.ts`. The Keychain wipe that now landed means the gate no longer hides a write-without-wipe asymmetry when that probe lifts it.
- **The mutation proof for `lane-push-envelope`'s schema `.max()` bound is recorded `observed: false`** — it is behaviourally indistinguishable from the byte check that follows, so no test can go red on it.
- **Mutation proofs for guards landed in earlier rounds of the S9b sub-slice were not re-run** in the final round; only that round's guards and the new join test were mutated.
- **The renderer lane chip is unwired, stated in the code as deferred to S9d** (`TerminalPresenceChip`'s `lane` prop doc and `terminal-lane-account-chip-state.ts`'s header, `:5-6`). Reason: the desktop renderer consumes no `terminal.list` lane field anywhere, so wiring the chip means building a per-pane feed of the runtime terminal rows, which §6 budgets as S9d. **Rev 25: S9d has now shipped and this did not ship with it** — the code comment is unchanged and therefore stale (§10(e)'s S9d bullet, below).
- **The `terminal.list` identity proof is schema-shape, not behavioural**, and was left in place rather than deleted; the behavioural half is covered by the two-socket case at `lane-usage-row-isolation.integration.test.ts:115-121`.
- **Rev 25 — S9c's gate skipped `typecheck:web`**, the same rule that skipped it every S9a/S9b round: available memory read 4870MB against the 7000MB floor at review time, per the memory gate rule. No web or renderer file is touched by the lifecycle slice, so the exposure is narrow, but it is unrun.
- **Rev 25 — S9d shipped the backend only; Parts 2–4 of the renderer are NOT DONE.** The `AccountsPane.tsx` per-lane section (delegated accounts, `laneState` loaded/absent/reauth-required plus the `restart-required` seam, the R-DR2 operating-rule text, the Q3 editable friendly name, the delegation-lease view) and the `laneState`-conditional restart hedge that was to replace the unconditional one at `AccountsPane.tsx:802` (Part 2); surfacing the owner label, the friendly account name and `laneUsage` on the chip/badge/status-bar components — the deferred `TerminalPresenceChip` `lane` prop wiring, still marked "DEFERRED TO S9d" in the code's own comment (`terminal-lane-account-chip-state.ts:5-6`) even though S9d, the slice that comment names, has now shipped (Part 3); and the consent-surface renderer rows — bind-to-person, designate-pusher, provision/deprovision lane, audit rows (Part 4). No renderer component tests exist for Parts 2–4. §6's S9d bullet — the designation surface, the delegable-account curation tick, and those `AccountsPane.tsx` edits — therefore still stands as *budgeted*, not as shipped; see §6's rev-25 module list.
- **The §5 live-box verification steps — (9) through (12b), Windows-only — remain unrun**, unchanged from every prior round: no such box exists in this sandbox. Gathered into the new **§9. Windows verification checklist**, below §8.

### (f) Release audit 2026-08-24 — three blockers, one major, built but not composed

**Trigger.** Every S9a–S9d slice above shipped with green unit and integration tests against its own harness. None of them proved that production ever *constructed* the object those tests exercised. This audit went looking specifically for that gap — a module exporting `attachX`/`startX`/`setXDependencies` with a real implementation and a real test suite, but with zero non-test caller anywhere under `src/main` — and found it three times, at blocker severity, plus one lower-severity major in an adjacent write path the audit passed over on its way through.

**Root cause, one sentence: built but not composed.** Each of B1–B3 below is a class or module that was fully implemented, fully unit-tested, and *never wired to anything that runs*. A host boot never called the function that would have made it live, so the RPCs and lifecycle hooks behind it either refused by a stated code or silently no-op'd — both of which look, from the outside, like a working system that simply has nothing to do yet. Nothing in S9a–S9d's own test suites could have caught this, because every one of them supplies its own hand-built instance of the class in question rather than going through the seam production actually uses.

- **B1 — `LaneWireService` had no production caller.** `attachLaneWireService` (`runtime/lane-wire-service.ts`) has existed since S9c. Nothing under `src/main` called it outside tests, so every `accounts.lane.*` RPC refused `accounts.lane.not_enabled` on the target host, and the close/revoke wipe hooks in `principal-lane-connection-lifecycle.ts` read a null coordinator and did nothing. This is also the direct cause of §8 item 4 above: `attachManagedAccountResidencyGuard` is called from nowhere except `attachLaneWireService`'s own body, so L1's residency predicates were unreachable for the same reason and by the same fix. Closed by `runtime/lane-wire-composition.ts` (`setLaneWireHostDependencies`, called once from `index.ts`'s `whenReady` chain right after `ClaudeRuntimeAuthService` is constructed; `composeLaneWireForRegistry`/`attachComposedLaneWire`, called from `principal-lane-host-wiring.ts:67`, the same seam that already attaches the consent surface) — the same module-singleton-provider-plus-per-registry-composer shape the consent service and the residency guard already used, so the wire now has exactly the production caller its sibling seams always had.
- **B2 — the win32/darwin provisioning gate had no way out.** §6's gate (ii) made Windows and macOS lane provisioning refuse outright (`accounts.lane.provisioning_platform_gated`) until the platform-specific §5/§9 live probe ran — correct as a default, but with no operator path past it short of editing code, on the one platform (§2m, the Windows Server host pivot) this design is actually built for. **The Windows-override decision, made and shipped in this audit: the gate stays ON by default; the override is explicit, named, and audited, never silent.** `provisionLane` takes an optional `acceptUnverifiedPlatform`; when the calling platform is gated (`PROVISIONING_GATED_PLATFORMS`, `principal-lane-consent-service.ts:34-39` — win32 and darwin, each naming the §9 step that would clear it) and the flag is set, provisioning proceeds and `PrincipalRegistry.recordLaneProvisioned` writes a `platformAcceptance: 'unverified-win32' | 'unverified-darwin'` field onto that lane's `provision` audit row (`principal-registry.ts:238-244`) — an *ungated* platform, or a gated one where the flag was not passed, records no such field, so the audit trail distinguishes "never asked" from "verified" from "operator overrode." Surfaced three ways over the one RPC: the CLI's `orca lane provision --accept-unverified-platform` (`cli/specs/lane.ts:65-73`, `cli/handlers/lane.ts:159-169`); a host-only checkbox on the desktop's `PrincipalConsentDeviceRow.tsx`, offered only when `provisioningPlatformGate` is non-null; and `orca lane audit` / the desktop's audit list, which render the `platform=unverified-win32` field back out (`cli/lane-format.ts:40,183-184`). **The override does not retire the gate — it records a deliberate, attributable exception to it.** The actual acceptance test for "is this platform safe to run ungated" is §9's live-box steps 12 and 13 (DACL read-back, credential-store isolation); when those pass on the shared box, provisioning stops needing the flag because the platform is no longer in `PROVISIONING_GATED_PLATFORMS`, not because the flag was used to talk the gate down.
- **B3 — the desktop's own half of the delegation wire had zero production construction.** `LaneDelegationPushClient` (the desktop's end of §2c/§2e/§2l step 3 — the client that pushes a credential to a paired host, pulls a rotation back, and publishes the delegable-account list) and `attachLaneDelegationLeaseStore` (the lease store that arms rules (i)–(iv) on the delegating desktop, §2e) both existed, fully built, with no production caller. Closed by `claude-accounts/lane-delegation-desktop-service.ts`, constructed once from `index.ts`: it attaches the lease store and drives one push client per paired host on three triggers — reconnect, local account-selection change, and an explicit action on the desktop. That explicit action is the piece §9 step 0's day-one setup names below: a **"Delegate"** button per host row under **"Load an account onto a host"** (`AccountLaneStatusSection.tsx`'s `DelegateHostRow`, wired to `window.api.principalLaneStatus.delegateAccountToHost` → `lane-delegation-desktop-service.ts:188`). One built-in deviation from §7 question 7 as posed: the push client's `listDelegable()` offers every managed account on the desktop, not an owner-curated tick list — `accounts.lane.setDelegableAccounts` still ships and is still the wire method that carries the list (§3 row 12 unchanged), but the desktop now calls it automatically with the full account set rather than waiting on a curation UI, which is the "default-in" branch §7 question 7 left open, chosen here as a side effect of closing B3 rather than answered as its own question. §7 question 7 is not re-dispositioned by this note; it is flagged so a later reader does not mistake the shipped default for an owner ruling.
- **M1 — a grant from an unredeemed per-person invite could be bound and designated before anyone opened the invite.** `PrincipalRegistry.assertBindable` and `designatePusher` now both independently refuse a grant whose `lastSeenAt === 0` (`principal-registry.ts`, re-checked at both writes, not only at bind, because a row bound by an older build can already be unredeemed by the time designation runs) — otherwise whoever eventually redeemed the invite would inherit a binding and a pusher designation made before they had ever opened Orca. `listGrants` exposes the same predicate as `redeemed` so `orca lane status` can render "invite outstanding." Lower severity than B1–B3 because it is a race on first setup, not a permanently-inert production seam, but it shares the audit's finding that a consent write can be reached before the precondition guarding it has actually run.

**T1 — the composition-parity guard, so this failure shape cannot recur silently.** `runtime/lane-composition-parity.test.ts` statically scans every source file under `src/main`, finds every `attach*`/`start*`/`set*Dependencies` export, and asserts each one has at least one non-test caller in the same tree — exactly the check that would have failed the day B1 and B3 landed. `KNOWN_OPEN_GAPS` is the tracked-and-audited allowlist for composition work not yet done; it is empty as of this audit, and shrinking it (never growing it) is the only correct response to a new failure here. A follow-up mutation pass (closing a T1 blind spot: the export-name pattern originally matched `attach*`/`start*` only, so a `set*Dependencies` provider — the actual B1 fix, `setLaneWireHostDependencies` — was invisible to it) widened the pattern and added direct tests proving B3's `notifyLaneDelegationHostReachable`/`notifyLaneDelegationHostUnreachable` triggers actually fire from the runtime-environment transport layer, not merely that the call-site text exists in source.

**T2 — one real end-to-end test over the production wiring itself.** `runtime/principal-lane-production-wiring.integration.test.ts` is the one test in the tree that boots the *actual* composition path — real `setLaneWireHostDependencies` + `attachPrincipalLaneHost`, the same call chain `index.ts` and `OrcaRuntimeRpcServer.start()` use, never a hand-built `LaneWireService` — and drives the whole day-one flow over a real socket: create-person → bind → designate → provision → a push over the socket through the real `LaneDelegationPushClient` → the lane loaded → a lane-pinned spawn's env carrying `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` → the last socket close → the credential file gone. `lane-wire-composition.test.ts` (B1) and `principal-lane-close-wipe.integration.test.ts` (S9c) each already covered one half of this over their own harness; T2 is the one place both halves run together through the real composition, so a regression that breaks only the *wiring* between them — not either class on its own, which is exactly the shape B1 and B3 were — fails here where nothing failed before.

**One corrective, folded into the same pass rather than left standing.** An intermediate version of B1's residency callback replaced §2d's documented fail-open behaviour (warn and continue) with a throw of a new refusal code whenever any principal lane held a credential — a behaviour change outside this audit's B1/B2/B3/M1 scope, itself untested (every existing test bypassed the callback entirely), and one that would have made an account whose Orca-owned auth directory was deleted out of band permanently unremovable and unselectable on any host with a lane loaded. Reverted to match the guard's documented default: warn, change nothing (§2d).

**Not found by this audit; not claimed closed by it.** The audit's method was exhaustive only for the `attach*`/`start*`/`set*Dependencies` export shape T1 now guards; it is not a general claim that every S9 seam is composed, only that this specific failure shape — implemented, tested, never called — is now checked mechanically rather than by re-reading the tree by hand.

### (g) Rev 32 — the credential-source re-basing (design record, no code yet)

**This subsection is a design record, not a reconciliation.** Everything in §10(a)–(f) describes code that is in the tree. Nothing in rev 32 is: it is the chair's decision of 2026-08-27 and the sections it rewrites — **§2a, §2b, §2c, §2d, §2e, §2f, §2h, §2k, §2l, §3, §4, §5, §6, §7, §8 and §9**, which is the list rev 33 reconciled the title against after the review round found §2a, §2h, §2k, §3, §5, §7 and §9 still carrying push-model text (rev 32's title claimed only "§2b–§2f, §3, §4"). It is recorded here in the same place as the shipped record so a reader who opens §10 first is not left thinking the push model is still current.

**What the decision is.** The S9 credential **source** changes from *"the desktop pushes its token blob into the lane"* to *"each lane holds its own OAuth grant(s), obtained by running `claude auth login --claudeai` inside the lane with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to it, with Orca relaying the authorization URL to the requesting person's device and the pasted code back to the **same** host process"*. PKCE is what makes the round-trip safe and what makes it stateful: the `code_verifier` never leaves the host process, so the code is worthless in transit and must be returned to the process that minted it.

**What was verified on the VPS on 2026-08-27, and what was not.** *Verified, by execution:* `claude auth login --claudeai` on the installed **2.1.247** binary, run headless in a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` with stdin on a plain pipe, prints an authorization URL and accepts a pasted code with no TTY and no local callback server; the login completed as `superiorpvpmc@gmail.com`; the resulting access token **differs** from the pushed lane blob's; a real inference ran on it; the account remained usable elsewhere throughout. `claude auth login --help` on that build offers `--claudeai`, `--console`, `--email <email>` and `--sso`, and no `--no-browser`, `--print-url`, `--code` or device-code flag. *Observed in the same session and load-bearing for the implementation:* one login emits **two** `redirect_uri` values — a `localhost:<ephemeral>` one to the browser opener and the hosted `platform.claude.com` one to stdout — and binds a loopback listener for the login's duration; the printed URL is OSC-8-wrapped, so it appears twice in the stream; the paste prompt carries no trailing newline; a bad code re-prompts rather than exiting; the scope set includes `org:create_api_key`. *Not verified, and gating:* whether **each chain still refreshes independently after the first access token expires** (§6's Gate 0, §5's live step L2), whether Anthropic caps concurrent grants per account, how long a refresh grant itself lives, and whether `claude auth logout` revokes server-side or only clears local state.

**What the design deletes.** Invariant L1 and the residency index; the delegation lease and all four of its rules, including rule (iv)'s clearing of the delegating desktop's own runtime credential file; the push envelope, `accounts.lane.push` and `pullRotated`; the watermark and its three writers; the lane sync driver's sync half; `accounts.lane.clear`; the delegable-account list, `setDelegableAccounts` and the delegation directory; `accounts.lane.requestSwitch` and the whole delegated-selection flow with its liveness precondition, `desktop_unavailable`, `switch_timed_out` and `switch_lane_cleared`; **R-DR2 as a class**; and sixteen refusal codes. **It deletes two lines in particular** — `assertManagedClaudeAccountNotLaneResident` at `claude-accounts/service.ts:472` and `assertClaudeAccountNotDelegatedToLane` at `:475` **[code]** — which are the mechanism of the 2026-08-27 incident, and it deletes them rather than relaxing them, because under per-lane login there is nothing left for them to protect.

**What the design keeps, and this is the larger half.** Per-person lanes and the `principalId` key; the closure principle and every one of its 37 enumerated surfaces; `computeLaneLaunch`, the `spawnForStablePane` anchor and `spawnWithLane`; §2a's binding, consent, designation (re-meant) and five consent writes, plus the `orca lane` CLI of item 39; the ownership predicate, the adopt/materialize gate and the pane-record binding; §2f's wipe **fence** in full, with only its trigger set shrunk; §2g's `openInMyLane` and its principal-keyed attachment join; §2h's presence labels; §2i's macOS scoping — which per-lane login makes *cleaner*, since a lane login writes the scoped Keychain item itself (`keychain.ts:85-93`) **[code]**; §2j's launch scrub; §2k's statusline attribution and the `paneKey → lane → account` join; §2m's Windows section entire; §2n's S9e design; and §10(f)'s composition-parity controls T1 and T2.

**What is graded [inferred] and unverified in rev 32, listed together because the document's other sections state each at its own site.** Refresh independence past the first expiry, and any concurrent-grant cap (gating); the refresh grant's own lifetime; whether `claude auth logout` revokes server-side; whether the Windows CLI keeps credential state outside `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (unchanged from §2m(3), and now the *login's* problem rather than the push's); whether a login into a config dir that already holds credentials replaces them cleanly, which the re-login-after-logout path depends on; and the login parser's stability across CLI releases, which §4 makes a slice gate rather than an assumption.

**Two facts about the reference tree, recorded because a later reader will check them.** The task pinned this revision to `7d7edad969` on `feat/resilience-presence-integration`; HEAD in `/home/ubuntu/orca-integration` is now **`b154b3a69e0bb6c823e83f67eaaab3f090cf0650`** on `feat/resilience-presence-integration` ("chore(integration): merge the lane section discoverability fix"), with **both** `7d7edad969` and rev 32's recorded `7648488313` — the `feat/lane-section-discoverability` tip, since merged — ancestors of it. **Re-verified 2026-08-27 for rev 33:** rev 32 recorded the branch tip; this records the merge that superseded it, and the substantive claim below is unaffected either way. The eight files that moved between them are all lane-delegation and lane-wire files this revision deletes or re-points (`lane-delegation-desktop-service.ts`, `lane-delegation-push-client.ts`, `lane-wire-service.ts`, `principal-lane-consent-service.ts`, `principal-lane-host-wiring.ts`, `shared/principal-lane-status-ipc.ts` and two test files); every `path:line` citation added by rev 32 was taken against the working tree and is identical at both commits unless the citation names one of those eight. And Orca's OAuth constants still pin **2.1.177** at `oauth-refresh.ts:4-8` **[code]** against an installed **2.1.247** — a drift of seventy patch releases, already realised, and §4 turns it into a gate on S9-L1.
